## 1. AnyCam: Anything Can Represent Camera for Video Generation

- **作者**：Weijie Lyu, Zhixin Shu, Yuntian Ke et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Project page: https://weijielyu.github.io/AnyCam/

Camera-controlled video generation re-renders a video along a target camera trajectory, yet moving the camera around an object, the most natural way to change the viewpoint of an object-centric scene, remains difficult. Existing methods condition either on camera parameters, which are scale-ambiguous in monocular video, or on explicit geometry such as warped point clouds or depth, which breaks down under large viewpoint changes. We present AnyCam, a method that represents the camera with an anchor object in the scene: we reconstruct it in 3D and render it along the target trajectory, a camera signal that is scale-aware, view-complete and easy to specify relative to the object. A video diffusion model transfers the content and dynamics of the source video to the new viewpoint. Its H-Concat layout aligns source and target frames in time, so the model can be distilled into AnyCam-AR, a streaming few-step generator for on-the-fly camera control. Both models are trained on paired videos with pixel-aligned anchor renderings from multi-camera videos and static-scene captures. Experiments on diverse datasets and trajectories show that AnyCam performs favorably against camera-controlled video generation and 3D reconstruction methods in camera control and visual quality.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34944) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34944)

## 2. Tracking Any Point with Agentic Visual Reasoning

- **作者**：Xuweiyi Chen, Jin Yao, Linzhan Mou et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Project page: https://agentap.cs.virginia.edu

A general-purpose Vision-Language Model (VLM) has surpassed expert vision models in many domains such as object detection and video recognition. However, point correspondence is known to be challenging for VLMs since it goes beyond object-level recognition: it requires pixel-level localization of the same physical surface point across observations, even when appearance changes or the point disappears and later reappears. In this paper, we introduce AgenTAP, a VLM-based agent that directly predicts point coordinates and visibility and actively reasons over videos. Starting from its own estimate, the agent autonomously chooses what to inspect and uses the resulting evidence to verify point identity and refine its predictions. With GPT-6 Astra, it surpasses expert vision models in both point localization and visibility prediction, without correspondence-specific fine-tuning. We further use AgenTAP to re-identify physical points after disappearance and reconnect fragmented tracks, constructing EgoTAP-4D, a dataset of 3D point trajectories in long real-world egocentric videos. On sampled reappearing points, its predictions approach human agreement, and adding EgoTAP-4D to D4RT training improves average 3D tracking performance.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34943) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34943)

## 3. Improving and Expanding Video Editing Capabilities Without Videos

- **作者**：Ji Xie, Yanhao Jia, Xiangpeng Yang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Image editing models can follow a wide range of instructions, but video editing models still lag behind, limited in part by the difficulty of collecting paired source and edited videos. Is it possible to transfer advanced image editing capabilities to video editors without new paired videos? We introduce Image-to-Video Transfer (I2VT), which improves pretrained video editors and expands them to new editing tasks using an image editor. It enables efficient post-training with no architectural changes and no new videos, using only individual images. Our key idea is to let the video editor role-play as an image editor: it edits an image as a one-frame video, with the image editor guiding the video editor's predictions at each denoising step. Although the video editor sees only single images during training, at test time it applies the improved and expanded capabilities to whole video clips while preserving temporal consistency. To push the boundaries of video editing, we introduce OpenVE++, an extension of OpenVE-Bench that adds four new editing tasks useful for video production, including action changes, expression changes, body movements, and scene relighting. Our experiments across three different video editors show that I2VT improves existing editing capabilities and expands them to new tasks, and our models achieve state-of-the-art (SOTA) performance on both OpenVE-Bench and OpenVE++.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34941) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34941)

## 4. LuxAvatar: Real-Time Relightable Head Avatars via Causal Neural Shading of 3D Intrinsic Proxies

- **作者**：Yipengjing Sun, Chenyang Wang, Boyao Zhou et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Project page: https://sypj-98.github.io/luxavatar/

We present LuxAvatar, a feed-forward framework for real-time head avatar animation and relighting from a single portrait. To achieve both 3D controllability and complex facial light transport, we factorize avatar synthesis into an animatable 3D intrinsic Gaussian proxy and a causal autoregressive neural shader. This proxy disentangles canonical geometry and reflectance attributes, rapidly rasterizing deformed Gaussians into view- and motion-consistent PBR buffers. Conditioned on these buffers, our neural shader formulates forward rendering as a conditional video diffusion process, bypassing explicit PBR computation to synthesize realistic shading effects under target illumination. We achieve real-time streaming through heterogeneous distillation from a pretrained multi-step bidirectional relighting teacher to a two-step causal autoregressive neural shader, inheriting broad synthetic lighting priors while minimizing conditioning and decoding latency. To further specialize these priors to human heads, we construct a hybrid relighting dataset for domain-specific fine-tuning, capturing authentic facial dynamics and physically grounded light transport. Extensive experiments show that our framework enables 3D-controllable, real-time portrait animation and relighting, outperforming state-of-the-art methods in visual fidelity, motion control, and lighting accuracy.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34940) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34940)

## 5. A Mesh for Every Moment

- **作者**：Roy Or-El, Jeong Joon Park, Ira Kemelmacher-Shlizerman
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.GR
- **备注**：Project page: https://memo-4d.github.io

We present MEMO (a Mesh for Every MOment), a method that reconstructs the geometry of complete dynamic scenes, captured by multiple synchronized cameras, and extracts a mesh at every frame. Existing dynamic Gaussian splatting methods either model appearance alone or target isolated objects segmented by foreground masks, and all of them recover meshes by TSDF fusion or Poisson reconstruction, whose fixed grids limit the detail they can capture. At the core of MEMO is a per-Gaussian motion model inspired by Gaussian processes, which represents each Gaussian's position and rotation over time as a combination of temporal Gaussian kernels. The model keeps every Gaussian a valid surface element throughout the sequence and carries an implicit representation of the dynamic geometry, so a mesh can be derived at any time from a field defined by the Gaussians. Although common in real captures, large and fast motion poses a challenge that dynamic Gaussian splatting methods usually overlook. To account for it, we design a track-based initialization scheme that fits each Gaussian's initial trajectory to point tracks in closed form. We evaluate MEMO on Blender-Moments, a new benchmark of nine animated scenes with ground-truth meshes at every frame, and on two real-world datasets. MEMO reconstructs the most accurate geometry among state-of-the-art methods, and remains competitive in novel-view synthesis and training time. Project page: https://memo-4d.github.io

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34939) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34939)

## 6. Can image generation help visual reasoning? An empirical study

- **作者**：Xueyang Yu, Yifan Jing, Taiming Lu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.LG, cs.CL, cs.CV

Language models think in words; then multimodal models might think in images. We test whether a generated helper improves the answer to a visual question. A model describes the helper it needs and then answers using the original and generated images together. Across 13 vision-language model and generator pairs, generation lowers mean accuracy by 3.7 percentage points, with declines on nine of ten tasks. Two unified models, BAGEL and Janus-Pro-7B, each lose accuracy on eight tasks. On VSTAR, adding a generated image to the same planning hint lowers accuracy by 10.47 points. Repeated image edits yield different outcomes across tasks. ChartQAPro and MathVista stay below their no-generation baselines, while ERQA gains three points after ten edits.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34935) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34935)

## 7. DynMF2: Rigid Motion Factorization by Expectation-Maximization for Monocular 4D Reconstruction and Motion Segmentation

- **作者**：Agelos Kratimenos, Yile Hua, Kostas Daniilidis
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

A single casual video leaves a moving scene under-determined: a point seen once from each place can slide toward or away from the camera and still match every frame. We constrain the reconstruction with the structure of the motion itself. Building on DynMF, we model a scene's motion as a handful of SE(3) trajectories, each rigid, and let every point follow one of them alone, never a blend. DynMF2 computes this factorization directly from 3D point trajectories, with no rendering, by Expectation-Maximization (EM). Its M-step fits, in closed form, every rigid motion to the points assigned to it, and its E-step reassigns every point to the motion that explains it with the least error. The background is simply one more motion, the camera's, so no masks are needed. Three tools then revise the factorization: requery adds trajectories where the rendering shows parts the tracker missed, merge joins motions that move together, and split separates those that do not. Thus the number of motions follows the scene. Without masks, DynMF2 clearly outperforms the mask-based baselines in view synthesis on iPhone and NVIDIA and in 3D tracking on iPhone. It recovers what masks leave out, from thin parts and details to small movers far in the background. Its motions segment the moving parts of MOVi-F and iTACO scenes competitively, although DynMF2 is never trained to segment. What moves in a scene is not decided in advance: it emerges from the rigid motions themselves.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34931) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34931)

## 8. High-Fidelity Image Matting with Pixel-Space Diffusion Transformers

- **作者**：Baihan Yang, Xin Lin, Yufan Wei et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：11 pages, 5 figures, 5 tables. Project page: https://white-yc.github.io/pixelmatte/

Diffusion models have shown strong potential for image matting by transferring generative priors learned from large-scale pretraining. However, existing approaches largely rely on latent diffusion models, whose compressed representations and VAE-based reconstruction introduce a mismatch with the high-frequency precision required for matting. We present PixelMatte, a framework that adapts pretrained pixel-space diffusion transformers for high-fidelity image matting. We show that strong image-conditioned matting capabilities can be acquired even from a pure text-to-image model without paired image-to-image pretraining. Our approach reuses the backbone's native patch embedding to jointly process the input image, trimap, and noisy alpha matte as spatially aligned tokens, enabling direct pixel-space prediction without additional condition encoders or adapters. With pixel-accurate synthetic supervision and real-image fine-tuning, PixelMatte predicts alpha mattes in a single forward pass. Two lightweight calibration modules, LayerShift and High-Pass Refiner, further improve regional opacity accuracy and boundary fidelity. Experiments across four benchmarks and two distinct pixel diffusion backbones demonstrate substantial improvements over existing methods, reducing SAD and gradient errors by 24-44% relative to RenderMatte on P3M-500-NP and AM-2K, while achieving competitive performance on AIM-500 and RenderMatte-2K. These results demonstrate the effectiveness of pixel-space generative priors for precision-sensitive image matting. Project page: https://white-yc.github.io/pixelmatte/.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34929) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34929)

## 9. Cairn: Object-Centric 3D Trajectory Priors for Steering Frozen VLAs

- **作者**：Agelos Kratimenos, Kostas Daniilidis, Evangelos Chatzipantazis
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.CV

Vision-language-action models (VLAs) are capable low-level controllers, but they tie where objects go to the layouts they were trained on: when objects are moved or obstacles block the way, they carry them to the wrong place or into collisions. Fine-tuning on new layouts needs robot data for each one and erodes skills the model already has. We introduce Cairn, which keeps the VLA frozen and steers it at test time along learned 3D trajectories of the object in its gripper. A small prior reads point clouds of the scene and the held object, along with its pose when grasped and the instruction, and samples SE(3) trajectories up to its release that lead it around obstacles and turn it to fit through gaps. The prior and the VLA form the two levels of a hierarchical planner in which feedback runs both ways: the VLA's intended motion chooses and reshapes the trajectories, and the chosen one guides the VLA's denoising. Its predicted actions, mapped to the held object's poses through the grasp, are pulled toward the next waypoint, while the VLA's weights stay untouched. Across 1,941 held-out LIBERO scenes with displaced objects and obstacles, Cairn raises the success rate of a frozen π0.5 from 24.5% to 95.1%, above that of a π0.5 fine-tuned on our training layouts, without hurting standard LIBERO. On the third-party LIBERO-PRO benchmark, it raises success from 27.3% to 38.0%, and from 54.4% to 81.1% on the episodes it steers. On a real Franka, with a prior trained purely in simulation, it guides the frozen DROID π0.5 around an obstacle and to a placement at the limit of the arm's reach, where it otherwise fails. All code, models and data will be made public.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34914) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34914)

## 10. LiveVAE: Causal Window Attention Makes Efficient Streaming Video VAE

- **作者**：Jiachen An, Sijie Zhao, Le Li et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Project page: https://livevae.github.io/ Code: https://github.com/MeiGen-AI/LiveVAE

While video diffusion models have grown faster and increasingly streamable, the video VAE remains a primary latency bottleneck across interactive generation and editing pipelines. Existing VAEs and their acceleration methods rely mainly on heavy convolutions or full frame attention, and most focus only on decoding, leaving encoding costly for streaming conditional generation and editing. This raises a natural question: Do video VAE encoding and decoding actually require such global spatiotemporal modeling? By analyzing the effective receptive fields of pretrained video VAEs, we find that both encoding and decoding a patch rely almost entirely on its spatial and temporal neighbors. To exploit this strong locality, we introduce LiveVAE, a streaming encoder and decoder built on causal window attention, which cuts per frame compute while keeping the temporal KV cache bounded for streaming. Decoupled encoder/decoder distillation followed by joint finetuning preserves the pretrained latent space, making LiveVAE a drop-in replacement for the original VAE. On MiniMax-H3, LiveVAE achieves 5.2-6.2x faster encoding and 3.0-3.3x faster decoding than the official VAE with higher PSNR. Paired with our dedicated inference infrastructure, LiveVAE pushes the speedup to 13.4x with almost no quality loss. Code and models will be released.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34912) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34912)

## 11. Agentic LongLive: Asynchronous Agentic Memory for Long Video Generation

- **作者**：Shaoteng Liu, Shuai Yang, Wei Huang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：28 pages, 12 figures. Project page: https://nvlabs.github.io/LongLive/Agentic-LongLive/

Real-time autoregressive video generators stream minutes of video and accept new prompts on the fly, but struggle to keep people, objects, and places consistent over a long interactive story. Agent systems provide such memory for offline multi-shot generation, yet they read the whole script in advance, plan each shot before generating it, and regenerate failed shots, which a live stream does not allow. We present Agentic LongLive, an asynchronous agentic memory for long video generation. A memory agent on separate GPUs reads only the prompts revealed so far, never the generated video, and keeps a text state and evolving images for every person, object, and scene. From this entity memory it composes a global image and a per-shot memory image, which an auxiliary GPU prefills into the generator's attention sink before the next shot starts, and writes entity cards for the prompts that name them, so the video never waits for memory and video errors are not written back. A small language model, trained with reinforcement learning and an image reward calibrated using downstream video outcomes, decides what to remember before the next prompt is known. On LongStory-Online, a new benchmark of 25 stories (857 shots) revealed one shot at a time, Agentic LongLive improves persistent-state accuracy of LongLive-2.0, its base generator, by 26.3 points, protagonist identity by 44.9, and revisited-place consistency by 40.4, at about 1% extra generation time on H100. The full system streams faster than real time on one 8xB200 node with the generator in BF16 (34.3 FPS), keeps image quality stable over 14-minute stories, and improves identity on two MiniMax-H3 generators, one of which, LongLive-H3, also runs in real time with it. To our knowledge, it is the first real-time video generator with text and image memory kept by an LLM agent.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34907) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34907)

## 12. ProbFaS: Probabilistic Video Frame Selection for MLLMs via Multi-Fidelity Calibration

- **作者**：Tianru Zhang, Mayank Nautiyal, Hugo Eidmann et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Processing long videos with multimodal large language models (MLLMs) is prohibitively expensive, making informative frame selection essential for accurate video understanding. The default uniform sampling often misses task-relevant keyframes, while recent training-free selectors instead pick frames by how well each one matches the question under an image-text alignment (ITA) score. However, we show that ITA signals correspond poorly with the MLLM's true needs: their rankings correlate weakly with downstream accuracy and are even negatively correlated on over 40% of questions, whereas the MLLM's internal yes/no judgment yields a much stronger signal. We present ProbFaS, a training- and label-free probabilistic pipeline that fuses both signals for key frame selection. ProbFaS treats dense ITA scores as cheap, biased measurements and sparse MLLM judgments as accurate, costly ones, unifying them via co-kriging, a multi-fidelity Gaussian process, to calibrate relevance across all frames. A temporal determinantal point process then selects frames capturing relevance peaks while preserving temporal coverage. Across Video-MME, MLVU and LongVideoBench, ProbFaS is the most accurate among fourteen state-of-the-art training-free selectors and uniform sampling in 13 of 15 settings (8-128 frames), outperforming the strongest baseline by up to 3.1 percentage points. On MLVU and LongVideoBench, 32 ProbFaS frames match or surpass the top baseline at 128 frames and uniform sampling with up to 768 frames.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34903) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34903)

## 13. Enhancing Maritime Domain Awareness using Latent Space Representations

- **作者**：Rikard Vinge, Miguel A. Belenguer-Plomer, Isabelle Wittmann et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.LG
- **备注**：SPIE Conference Proceedings

This work presents a novel application of Earth Observation (EO) embeddings for Maritime Domain Awareness (MDA) from the perspective of the geospatial foundation model TerraMind. By operating directly on compressed latent-space representations rather than on the original Sentinel-1 imagery, this approach directly addresses the data gravity bottleneck that increasingly constrains operational EO processing, in line with the objectives of the Embed2Scale project. In particular, linear and non-linear probing of compressed embeddings, latent space representations of synthetic aperture radar (SAR) imagery, is used to detect, localise, and characterise vessels from Sentinel-1 GRD images. Vessel detection achieves a ROC-AUC of 0.95, while vessel localisation has a median error of around 300 m for vessels longer than 100 m, and vessel AIS type-code classification achieves 62% accuracy for the majority classes. The latter, however, is highly correlated with vessel length. These results reveal both the potential and current limitations of Geospatial Foundation Models (GeoFMs) when applied to fine-grained MDA understanding in real-world scenarios. Additionally, a new maritime remote sensing dataset is released, pairing about 2,000 Sentinel-1 images with temporally aligned AIS signals from commercial vessels. Through manual inspection, each candidate sample was verified for bounding-box quality and for consistency between the AIS-reported vessel length and the observed vessel extent, cross-checking vessel identity and location against publicly available vessel records and, where available, photographs. The dataset was made ready for use with the NeuCo-Bench neural compression benchmark framework, publicly available on HuggingFace through https://huggingface.co/datasets/embed2scale/S1-single-ship-downstream .

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34899) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34899)

## 14. What a Drop Reveals: Sim-to-Real Material Estimation for Deformable Objects from Video

- **作者**：Ziyue Peng, Yanxi Hu, Junyoung Kim et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.GR, cs.LG
- **备注**：74 pages. Project page: https://bjxdw.github.io/PhysReader/

How an object deforms when it hits the ground reveals what it is made of. From a single real video of a drop, we estimate a deformable object's density-normalized stiffness $E/ρ$, and its yield stress $σ_Y/ρ$ when the impact causes plastic deformation, with a feed-forward predictor trained only in simulation. Real videos carry no material labels, so such predictors are developed and validated on synthetic data alone. We show that synthetic validation ranks their geometric inputs in the wrong order. Material acts through 3D deformation, and tracklets lifted to 3D with estimated depth are indeed the most accurate input in simulation. However, on real drops they are the least accurate, and better depth does not repair them: the failure persists with simulator depth in training, LiDAR depth at test time, and exact depth on rendered replays of the real drops. Short 2D optical-flow tracklets do the opposite: they improve nothing in simulation, yet they transfer best. Material is therefore better read from what perception measures consistently in both domains than from what is most informative in simulation. Our predictor, PhysReader, reads the video and 2D tracklets and learns the lift to 3D in simulation by matching a teacher that sees exact 3D deformation. To measure transfer, we introduce PhysReader-Real: 16 everyday objects dropped five times each, with reference materials fit jointly to each object's drops and checked against tensile tests. On PhysReader-Real, PhysReader is closer to the reference materials than every other input and baseline. The one exception is a per-scene optimizer started from PhysReader's prediction, which ends slightly closer but takes 49 minutes per video instead of 12 seconds.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34894) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34894)

## 15. FineDex: Bridging Fine-Grained Human-Object Interaction Generation to Dexterous Humanoid Manipulation

- **作者**：Yujia Zeng, Yuxin Chen, Yuzhen Song et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.CV
- **备注**：Project page: https://finedex.github.io/

Dexterity is, at its core, finger-level control, yet existing dexterous manipulation and human-object interaction (HOI) generation methods rarely let one specify how an interaction is performed: five-fingered hands are often reduced to grippers, or finger usage is copied from individual human demonstrations. We present FineDex, a framework that makes finger-level intent both expressible and executable. FineDex learns coordinated human body, hand, and object motions that follow fine-grained instructions on hand selection, individual fingers, and contacts with articulated objects. To bridge generation and execution, FineDex leverages generated interactions as actionable priors for an agent that discovers physically feasible contact strategies through practice in a physics-based digital twin. By adapting at test time without per-instance retraining, FineDex generalizes across fine-grained instructions and object configurations. Experiments demonstrate state-of-the-art HOI generation quality and fine-grained controllability, substantial improvements in dexterous manipulation success, and successful deployment on a real dexterous humanoid robot.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34884) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34884)

## 16. Stranger Things: When Objects Appear Without Their Typical Neighbors

- **作者**：Siddhartha Gairola, Jiahao Xie, Anna Kukleva et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.LG
- **备注**：This paper was accepted to the 40th Conference on Neural Information Processing Systems (NeurIPS 2026). Track on Evaluations and Datasets. Dataset: huggingface.co/datasets/sidgairo18/strange-bench

Visual context is a powerful cue for object recognition: scenes inform what, where, and at what scale objects are likely to appear. Yet over-reliance on context becomes a failure mode when models predict objects based on their typical neighbors rather than the objects' own visual features, which we call typicality bias. While extensively studied in the pre-Transformer era, it has often been assumed that architectural and scale improvements would naturally solve it, and a systematic diagnostic across the modern vision stack remains absent. We provide such a diagnostic framework. We measure typicality bias via normalized pointwise mutual information (NPMI) over object pairs, and introduce STRANGE-Bench (Synthetic Typicality-RANked Grounded Evaluation Benchmark): a photorealistic, layout-controlled benchmark spanning 17 sub-tiers from typical to never-co-occurring object pairs. Across five model families (specialist detectors, open-vocabulary detectors, self-supervised, vision-language, and multimodal large language models) and three recognition tasks (supervised detection, open-vocabulary detection, and classification), the bias is prevalent: gaps of 5-11 p.p. persist when objects appear outside their typical company, and do not consistently shrink with scale. To mitigate it, we turn to data augmentation: existing augmentations are designed for visual diversity, not for breaking co-occurrence shortcuts, and sample from the very distribution that produces typicality bias. We propose Anchor-Atypicality Sampling (AAS), a simple sampling rule for paste-based augmentations that uses the NPMI signal to tilt the training-time co-occurrence distribution toward atypical partners. AAS reduces the typicality gap by up to 1.6 p.p. on natural-image COCO splits and 23.0 p.p. on synthetic STRANGE-Bench across three model families, without sacrificing overall performance.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34881) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34881)

## 17. Towards Closing the Performance Gap in Cross-Modal Place Recognition

- **作者**：Ofer Idan, Dan Badur, Yoli Shavit
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Cross-modal place recognition (CMPR) matches natural language descriptions against databases of geo-tagged reference images, enabling localization without visual queries. Despite its potential for language-guided navigation, existing vision-language models lag far behind visual place recognition (VPR) baselines, even after task adaptation. Unlike general-purpose text-to-image retrieval, place recognition relies on subtle local cues to distinguish visually similar locations. We therefore hypothesize that a primary bottleneck in CMPR is capturing fine-grained correspondences between image regions and text details, which requires higher visual token granularity, strong text representation capacity, and local cross-modal matching. To address this challenge, we propose CrossPlace, a two-stage CMPR framework. Stage 1 performs global retrieval by pairing a jointly aligned DINOv2-B/14 visual backbone with a progressively fine-tuned BGE-Large text encoder, while Stage 2 refines top candidates using a lightweight token-level cross-attention reranker. Through systematic analysis, we isolate the drivers of retrieval accuracy and show that visual token density provides the largest performance improvement, while text encoder capacity and task fine-tuning offer complementary gains. To evaluate robustness under realistic conditions, we also extend CMPR evaluation to city-scale and severe weather and illumination settings. Across all existing and new benchmarks, CrossPlace achieves double-digit Recall@1 improvements over prior cross-modal methods, closes the gap to unimodal text retrieval, and narrows it to unimodal VPR. Code and benchmarks are available at https://github.com/oferidan1/CrossPlace

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34877) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34877)

## 18. Focus Only on What's Important: Training-Free Focus Maps for Frozen Vision Transformers in Long-Video Object Segmentation

- **作者**：Harsha Karipineni, Rajesh Reddy Chintha
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.LG
- **备注**：26 pages, 3 figures, 10 tables. Code: https://github.com/boletoms/focused-cv

Vision transformers are strong but costly image encoders for video object segmentation. Training-free token merging cuts compute uniformly across the frame, including on tracked objects. We give a frozen DINOv3 ViT inside a trained Cutie head a training-free focus map: full resolution where the segmenter expects its objects and coarser merging elsewhere. Protecting only the previous frame's mask works on short clips but fails on long videos, where fast motion pushes targets outside the margin and into background merging, locking in loss. Our policy instead takes the union of velocity-shifted and block-matched masks, runs a camera-compensated surprise check, delegates uncertain regions to the compared merger, and falls back to it when all objects are lost. On long videos (LVOS V2 valid, pre-specified protocol, three ViT-S seeds), it beats ALGM 0.98 by +2.76 J&F (95% object CI [+1.22,+4.44]; video-cluster [+0.99,+4.52]) at 16% fewer counted encoder FLOPs (0.385 vs. 0.459), sitting 1.0 J&F point below the dense-encoder baseline; at ViT-B the gain over ALGM 0.95 is +3.89 [+2.03,+5.87]. On DAVIS 2017 val it stays within -0.19 J&F of the dense encoder at ViT-S (-0.12 at ViT-B). Controls qualify the result: protecting only the previous mask under ALGM matches or exceeds accuracy at higher FLOPs (0.486), so expectation and surprise mainly reduce counted encoder compute. Practically, lower counted encoder compute did not reduce startup-inclusive mean Cutie step time on a 12-video LVOS sample (3%-8% lower in steady state; an equally engineered ALGM is faster at ViT-S and ViT-B). Section 5.2 separately examines a Cutie permanent-memory schedule and its latency and memory trade-offs. Code: https://github.com/boletoms/focused-cv

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34863) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34863)

## 19. BOP-Refer: Benchmark for Monocular 3D and 2D Object Grounding

- **作者**：Tomas Hodan, Vineet Bhat, Martin Sundermeyer et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.RO

We introduce BOP-Refer, the first benchmark for monocular 3D and 2D grounding of everyday and industrial objects sourced from 9 diverse BOP datasets. We pair images from these datasets with VLM-generated and human-verified referring expressions, and derive accurate ground-truth 3D and 2D bounding boxes of the referred objects from their 3D models and 6DoF pose annotations. Our systematic evaluation of 17 frontier VLMs across the major proprietary and open-weight families (Gemini, GPT, Claude, Qwen, Gemma) shows that the best model, Gemini 4 Carbon, reaches 72.6% AP_IoU2D in 2D but only 12.4% AP_IoU3D in 3D, despite the more lenient IoU thresholds in 3D. The predicted 3D boxes are usually centered on the target objects in the image but often off in depth by more than the object's size, suggesting that metric scale estimation, which needs reliable object size priors, is currently by far the main source of error.

## 20. DocDUR-Net: Degradation-Aware Unified Document Image Restoration

- **作者**：Haowei Xu, Peirong Zhang, Xuhan Zheng et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Accepted to ICIG 2026

Captured document images often suffer from diverse degradations caused by uncontrolled imaging conditions, which reduce readability and obscure fine text structures. Single-degradation restoration methods are usually trained and deployed for individual degradation types, and therefore require model selection before restoration. Unified restoration models alleviate the need to maintain multiple restoration networks by sharing one network across tasks, but representative unified document image restoration methods still depend on external task selection. This dependency limits automatic document image restoration. We propose DocDUR-Net, a degradation-aware unified restoration network that derives restoration guidance directly from the input image. It introduces a Degradation Perception Module (DPM) to extract degradation representations and predict degradation weights, and Degradation Prior-guided Adaptive Feature Fusion (DP-AFF) to combine learnable degradation priors with degradation features extracted from the input. The resulting guidance is injected into a shared restoration backbone, enabling adaptive restoration without external task selection. DocDUR-Net also supports a manual mode with explicit task selection. Experiments on document image restoration benchmarks show that DocDUR-Net achieves the best average PSNR of 29.405 dB and SSIM of 0.960 among unified methods while remaining competitive with single-degradation methods.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34860) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34860)

## 21. TIDE-Flow: Fine-Grained Temporal Evidence and Structured Displacement Matching for Spike Optical Flow

- **作者**：Hanwen Liang, Wenxuan Liu, Wenxin Huang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Spike cameras capture high-speed motion through an integrate-and-fire mechanism, providing fine-grained temporal observations without exposure blur. However, their binary streams encode accumulated illumination rather than instantaneous intensity, making reliable motion estimation challenging. Existing spike-based methods exploit temporal aggregation to construct motion representations, but this process may weaken fine-grained firing patterns. Meanwhile, correlation-based estimators often rely on learned matching features without explicitly characterizing the spatial distribution and ambiguity of candidate displacements. To address these challenges, we propose TIDE-Flow, a spike-based optical flow estimation framework that jointly exploits Temporal Integration Evidence (TIE) and Displacement Evidence Encoding (DEE). TIE combines raw spike patterns, multi-scale firing statistics, temporal moments, and boundary-aware interval cues, preserving complementary temporal information before spatial feature compression. DEE further integrates structured candidate-grid features with explicit displacement statistics to characterize matching geometry and competing correspondences. Both components are incorporated into a difference-correlation recurrent refinement framework, enabling effective temporal motion modeling and correspondence estimation. Extensive experiments demonstrate the effectiveness of TIDE-Flow, with controlled ablations validating the contributions of temporal and displacement evidence modeling.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34854) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34854)

## 22. Real2SimLab: A Unified Platform for Real-to-Simulation Transfer

- **作者**：Yongchang Zhang, Jie Huang, Yunhe Ni et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.CV
- **备注**：Project page: https://real2simhub.github.io/

Real-to-simulation transfer, which builds simulations from real-world observations, has long been studied in research and industry. Recently, coding agents have become capable of generating simulation programs from video recordings, and several benchmarks have been proposed to evaluate them. However, each individual benchmark defines its own inputs, deliverables, and scoring pipeline. This leads to $N \times M$ integrations when evaluating $N$ methods on $M$ benchmarks. To this end, we present Real2SimLab, a unified platform for real-to-simulation transfer. It evaluates a method by running its written program and scoring the output simulation with the original scorer of each benchmark. Moreover, Real2SimLab provides a pipeline to create training data and reward annotations for real-to-simulation methods. Our pipeline carefully avoids data contamination by filtering out the video recordings that exist in benchmarks. For each video recording, a program is first produced, and then our pipeline computes its reward with the same code that Real2SimLab uses for evaluation. So far, it supports 47 coding agents and 5 non-agent methods on 6 benchmarks. Both the platform and Awesome-Real2Sim, our research map of nearly 300 real-to-simulation papers, are open source, and we invite the Real2SimHub community to build them together.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34849) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34849)

## 23. Probing Visual Idiosyncrasies in Text-to-Image Models

- **作者**：Yongqian Peng, Hongjie Li, Yichao Zhou et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI

Text-to-Image (T2I) models turn short text descriptions into images, and the leading models now produce images of comparable visual quality. Whether their outputs still carry model-specific characteristics matters for attribution, for model comparison beyond aggregate quality, and for evaluations that assume anonymity. We ask whether images from frontier T2I models can still be classified by source, and which visual properties make this possible. Here we show that a standard ResNet-50 identifies the source of an image among HiDream-O1-Dev, FLUX.2-dev, HunyuanImage-3.0, and Qwen-Image-2512, the four highest-ranked open-source models on the Text-to-Image Arena, with 100.00% accuracy on held-out prompts. Human participants reach only 62.61% on the pairwise version of this task, so the distinguishing cues are not visually obvious. They are also not tied to a particular classifier and survive severe image corruptions and out-of-distribution prompts. We trace them to visual idiosyncrasies at three levels. Low-level texture statistics alone let a lightweight multi-layer perceptron match ResNet-50. Object layout, depth, and albedo estimated from the images each carry a source signal. Image captions reveal model-specific preferences in style and background that the prompts never specify. A one-sentence hint about these cues raises human accuracy by 9.03 percentage points. These idiosyncrasies make source classification a tool for measuring similarity between models and between real and generated images, and they caution against assuming anonymity in human preference evaluation of T2I models.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34843) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34843)

## 24. Diffusion Steering via Adaptive Reward Estimation and Resampling

- **作者**：Abinitha Gourabathina, Sana Tonekaboni, Marzyeh Ghassemi et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Text-to-image diffusion models can generate images that satisfy complex prompts, yet often produce images that omit requested objects, attributes, or relationships. We ask if models can more reliably generate text-aligned images by redistributing probability toward their more successful outcomes. We propose TrailMix, a training-free method that uses the base model's distribution of possible outcomes to adaptively guide generation toward better text alignment. During denoising, TrailMix generates inexpensive lookahead images from parallel trajectories (trails) and scores their alignment with the prompt using an external judge. The resulting distribution of reward scores allows us to estimate how strongly to steer generation toward a desired alignment level. We use these estimates to reweight and resample trajectories, favoring more promising outcomes. Across three text-to-image benchmarks, TrailMix outperforms existing baselines, with particularly strong gains on rare multi-object compositions. We further extend TrailMix to targeted image editing and introduce SEAM-Bench (Semantic Editing and Attribute Maintenance), a systematic benchmark evaluating whether semantic edits preserve other image content. Experiments on SEAM-Bench and existing benchmark PIE-Bench demonstrate the effectiveness of our approach for controlled image editing. Our results show how a model's own possible outcomes can inform reliable and controllable generation.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34839) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34839)

## 25. Beyond Feature Fusion: Sensor-Grounded Semantic-Spiking Learning for Robust Object Detection

- **作者**：Hanwen Liang, Wenxuan Liu, Wenxin Huang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Underexposure weakens object details, and overexposure obscures boundaries. Spike cameras encode light into high-frequency binary streams. Long-window spike counts give stable appearance cues, while short observations reveal changes in firing rates from sparse measurements. We propose SPIRE, a sensor-grounded semantic-spiking framework for illumination-robust object detection from a single spike stream. SPIRE builds an appearance view from the full packet through detection-oriented illumination adaptation and constructs a sequence of cumulative views from progressively longer prefixes. Each prefix supplies a rate map, an edge map, and a rate difference that tracks changes as spikes accumulate. At selected scales, ANN features and spike-derived initialization jointly set the initial membrane potentials and firing thresholds, allowing semantic cues to shape the responses during temporal encoding. Local spike-count contrasts supervise both branches during training. The residual between measured and ANN-predicted contrasts combines with the spiking features to produce a bounded correction to the fused feature pyramid. On simulated VOC and COCO, SPIRE-D outperforms the strongest baseline by 4.00-5.50 AP50 points across the four under- and overexposure settings. On real PKU-Vidar-DVS recordings, it achieves 67.07 AP50 and 40.10 AP75. SPIRE-D also uses 50.4% fewer MACs than SPFD.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34828) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34828)

## 26. Solve Vision with Code: Coding Agents Are Stronger Visual Reasoners Than Video Models

- **作者**：Dezhi Luo, Fengyuan Yu, Haotian Zhang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.LG, cs.MM

Video generation models are increasingly evaluated as visual reasoners: given a first frame and a natural-language prompt, can the model produce the video in which the specified task is carried out? The paradigm is considered advantageous because the reasoning is native to the visual medium, unfolding directly in the frames rather than through language or code. Whether this native route is in fact the more reliable path to visual reasoning has not been tested against a symbolic alternative under the same conditions. We therefore pose the same tasks, with identical inputs, output specification and evaluator, to both media: rather than denoising pixels, a coding agent writes a program that renders the answer video, which an unmodified rule-based evaluator scores. On VBVR-Pro-Bench (Xu et al., 2026), a 500-instance suite spanning five cognitive categories with symbolic ground truth, the agent receives the same first frame and prompt as the video model, with no access to ground truth, metadata or generation models, and one attempt per instance. Three closed-model agents (Codex with gpt-6-astra, Claude Code with Fable 5.1 and Gemini CLI with Gemini 3.1 Pro) score 0.923, 0.894 and 0.893, each more than 0.22 above the strongest video model. Among 23 runnable open-weight models, the best, GLM-5, reaches 0.572, above all video models except the RL-trained one. Together, we show that for tasks whose solutions can be computed from a first frame and a prompt, program synthesis is currently a substantially more reliable route to visual reasoning than video generation. We further distinguish depictive programs, which navigate the image to recover a transferable scheme, from propositional programs, which operate on hard-coded values, revealing how visual reasoning unfolds in agent-written programs and what it implies for its nature of reasoning in general.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34813) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34813)

## 27. HOG-WAM: Improving Visual Robustness through Future Gradient Prediction

- **作者**：Boxuan Dong, Yupeng Qian, Ying Wu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.RO
- **备注**：Code: https://github.com/iPPLab/HOG-WAM

World-action models jointly predict future observations and actions, yet visual corruption can disrupt the resulting policies. We introduce HOG-WAM, which adds future Histograms of Oriented Gradients (HOG) as structural supervision derived directly from demonstration images. An auxiliary HOG diffusion transformer (DiT) module reads RGB features through asymmetric attention, allowing its objective to shape the visual representation used for control. The module is used only during training and removed at inference, preserving FastWAM's RGB-to-action architecture. We evaluate four controlled observation corruptions through a visual-corruption extension of RoboTwin, termed RoboTwin-Plus; HOG-WAM achieves the highest success among the compared policies under all four. Mixed-noise success increases from 78.86% to 82.86% relative to FastWAM, with comparable performance on clean scenes. On LIBERO Plus, overall success increases from 74.49% to 75.26%, with the largest gain under sensor noise, from 74.14% to 78.89%. Across four Franka tasks, average success increases from 40% to 59% under clean observations and from 17% to 24% under noisy observations. A representation ablation favors combining RGB observations with auxiliary HOG prediction over replacing RGB with HOG. These findings support future-gradient prediction as complementary supervision for visually robust control, with larger absolute gains on physical tasks than in simulation. Code is available at https://github.com/iPPLab/HOG-WAM.

## 28. WorldExpand: Building Coherent 3D Worlds via Sparse Joint Panoramic Expansion

- **作者**：Yuyang Yin, Chenjie Cao, Xuhui Zuo et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Project Page: https://yuyangyin.github.io/WorldExpand_web/

Constructing complete 3D worlds from a single panorama is challenging because large portions of the scene remain unobserved. Existing panoramic-video methods expand spatial coverage through dense sequential generation, but neighboring frames contain substantial redundancy, long trajectories accumulate errors, and independently generated directions may be inconsistent. We present WorldExpand, which formulates single-panorama world expansion as the generation of sparse, complementary, and high-resolution panoramic observations. This representation concentrates the generation budget on informative scene regions and preserves the fine-grained details required for 3D construction. WorldExpand addresses two key challenges: where to observe and how to synthesize. Our Coverage-Guided View Selection identifies informative target locations using scene-adaptive quadrants and warp-based coverage scores, while our Joint Panoramic Diffusion uses Gaussian-splatting-based geometric warps to jointly synthesize four pose-grounded and cross-view-consistent panoramas. A diagnostic-guided global--local attention schedule retains global attention only in sensitive layers, reducing the training and inference costs of high-resolution joint generation. The resulting posed panoramas can be directly fed into an existing reconstruction pipeline to build an explicit 3D representation. Experiments show that WorldExpand recovers content behind occluders and around distant corners, improves both novel-view synthesis and downstream 3D reconstruction metrics across various datasets, and generalizes robustly to diverse in-the-wild scenes. WorldExpand runs significantly faster than the state-of-the-art panoramic-video method while producing higher-resolution observations with stronger cross-view consistency.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34810) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34810)

## 29. Guidance as Variance Matching

- **作者**：Ali Backour, Artem Lukoianov, Frank Permenter et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.LG

Guidance repels the prediction of a diffusion model away from a weaker reference prediction. It substantially improves sample quality, but its mechanism remains poorly understood. We first find that, at the scales used in practice, guidance does not improve the prediction of the posterior mean (the model's training target); it makes the prediction worse, while samples improve. Second, posterior-mean predictions have less variance than the data, and we find that guidance restores it. We introduce variance-matched guidance: a closed-form rule that picks the guidance scale by matching the variance of the guided predictions to that of the data. Without tuning, it recovers the hand-picked scales of eleven widely used models across image, video, audio, motion, and world generation; to our knowledge, no previous explanation of guidance predicts them. Applied per noise level, it yields a schedule without tuned hyperparameters that users prefer to hand-crafted ones on SD 3.5 Medium. Third, we offer an interpretation of the mechanism behind guidance: each model makes a random error in predicting the posterior mean, and guidance rescales the difference of these errors into a sample with the posterior's covariance, which improves sampling. This view also suggests two new hypotheses that our experiments support: guidance can work with negative scales, and guiding a model with its own checkpoint needs a larger scale than guiding it with an independently trained model.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34801) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34801)

## 30. CIRKDV2: Cross-Image Relational Knowledge Distillation with Contextual Modeling for Efficient Semantic Segmentation

- **作者**：Chuanguang Yang, Yu Wang, Chengqing Yu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：18 pages, Accepted by TPAMI

Existing Knowledge Distillation (KD) methods for semantic segmentation often guide the student to mimic the teacher's structured information generated from an individual image. However, they ignore the global semantic relations across various images that are valuable for KD. This paper proposes Cross-Image Relational KD V2 (CIRKDV2), which focuses on transferring structured pixel-to-pixel, pixel-to-region, and channel-to-channel relations modeled from global images in the dataset. A large teacher network often constructs a well-structured feature space in terms of global contextual dependencies. CIRKDV2 makes the student mimic the better structured semantic relations of the teacher, thus improving the feature representational capability. Beyond relational distillation, we further propose contextual feature distillation by using context aggregation to augment student pixel embeddings to match the semantic level with the teacher. This avoids the semantic gap problem between the teacher and student occurred in traditional feature distillation studies. Experimental results on Cityscapes, ADE20K, Pascal VOC, and COCO-Stuff-164K demonstrate that CIRKDV2 outperforms the state-of-the-art semantic segmentation distillation methods by large margins across various network architectures. Our code and pre-trained models are publicly available at https://github.com/winycg/CIRKD.

## 31. SceneDesigner3D: Agentic Evolutionary Search for Principled Rearrangement of 3D Indoor Scenes

- **作者**：Chandan Yeshwanth, Angela Dai
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Project page: http://cy94.github.io/scenedesigner3d, Video: TBD

Scene rearrangement is an essential task for 3D content creation applications, requiring generation of diverse, aesthetic and functional scene rearrangements. We present SceneDesigner3D, an agentic evolutionary search method for 3D indoor scene rearrangement that systematically explores the design space of 3D scenes and generates multiple solutions according to common interior design principles. Given an input scene and a text prompt, SceneDesigner3D represents the rearrangement as an editable program and evolves a population of candidate layouts. Starting from distinct design ideas, a VLM agent mutates the programs by refining promising candidates, exploring new rearrangement strategies or redesigning from scratch, while verifying the layouts for physical validity. Proposals are scored by a VLM judge for adherence to the input prompt and satisfaction of design principles such as balance, zoning and emphasis, as well as numerically computed principles such as circulation and clutter. An archive of top-performing, structurally varied candidates further increases diversity of rearrangements. Experiments on the SAGE-10k and SceneSmith datasets show that SceneDesigner3D improves prompt adherence and computed design principle scores over baselines by up to 5.1% and 15.2%, respectively. Code will be made publicly available.

## 32. Generation as Reverse Encoding

- **作者**：Cheng Shi, Sibei Yang, Hanzhong Guo et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：14 pages, 7 figures, 5 tables

Pretrained visual representations accelerate generative learning by abstracting away appearance and spatial detail. Yet the same abstraction makes the return to pixels far harder, shifting much of the burden onto a larger decoder. We ask why the representation-to-pixel path should be learned independently when the encoder already provides a learned pixel-to-semantics path (the semantic encoding path). We introduce RevFlow, based on the principle of generation as reverse encoding. RevFlow uses intermediate representations of a frozen encoder as successive generative targets, first generating deep semantic representations, then shallow representations that retain appearance and spatial detail, before decoding to pixels. To learn these transitions without training a separate generator for each stage, we develop HyC-DiT, a unified generative model with a standard DiT backbone shared across stages and stage-specific condition injection. We further show that native matching in semantic representation space enables few-step generation. RevFlow accelerates both learning and sampling. On ImageNet $256 \times 256$, RevFlow reaches 1.96 FID at 690M and 1.74 FID at 1.55B after only 5 training epochs. It also achieves 30.25 dB reconstruction PSNR with an 89M decoder. After a short native-matching adaptation phase, RevFlow-XL achieves 2.85 FID using a single sampling step in each stage. Our code repository is at https://github.com/ChengShiest/RevFlow

## 33. Comparative Study of Deep Learning Architectures for Brain Tumour Classification

- **作者**：Prisha Gupta, Pradeep Kumar Gupta
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.LG

Accurate and early classification of brain tumours from magnetic resonance imaging (MRI) is essential for treatment planning, yet many published deep learning studies are trained and tested on small, single-source datasets, reporting accuracies that may not hold outside these datasets. This study compares six deep learning architectures for four-class classification (healthy, meningioma, glioma and pituitary tumour): a baseline convolutional neural network (CNN), an attention-based CNN, ShuffleNet, and EfficientNet-B0, ResNet-50 and Xception augmented with the Convolutional Block Attention Module (CBAM). The models were trained on a corpus assembled from four publicly available MRI datasets. Because these datasets carry no patient identifiers, a leakage-aware pipeline was developed that removes exact duplicates using MD5 hashing (550 images) and near-duplicates using perceptual hashing (8,024 images), and assigns visually similar images to the same fold during 5-fold group cross-validation. A leave-one-dataset-out (LODO) test on a fully held-out dataset was used to measure cross-dataset generalisation. Under a conventional random split, ShuffleNet achieved 97.1% accuracy with 1.2M parameters. Under leakage-aware group K-fold cross-validation, ShuffleNetV2 (98.41%) and EfficientNet-B0 (98.23%) were statistically indistinguishable (paired t-test, p = 0.19), and both significantly outperformed the custom CNNs. On the held-out dataset, accuracy dropped for every model: EfficientNet-B0 generalised best (78.4%), while ShuffleNetV2 fell to 66.2%. Glioma-meningioma confusion was the dominant error mode, and Grad-CAM maps showed that predictions were driven by tumour regions. These results indicate that in-distribution accuracy alone overstates real-world performance and that leakage-aware and cross-dataset evaluation should be reported alongside it.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34781) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34781)

## 34. SOLVE: Sparse, Ordered Keyposes Make LLM Agents Versatile and Efficient Robot Manipulator

- **作者**：Xiang Li, Hongyu Zhou, Ya-Li Li et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.CV

LLM agents have made remarkable progress in robot manipulation, yet they suffer from expensive inference, dense step-by-step control, and tool bottlenecks. We introduce SOLVE, a training-free, versatile and efficient agent policy: a multimodal LLM agent writes a sparse, ordered sequence of 6-DoF end-effector keyposes per call, observes the result, and writes the next, while a motion planner interpolates between them. The agent decides the number and spacing of keyposes for both fast long-range motion and fine-grained manipulation. This granularity matches the sparsity of teleoperated manipulation: most demonstrations in DROID can be compressed into a few keyposes instead of dense actions. SOLVE grounds keyposes with sparse depth queries answered by noisy sources, such as multi-view triangulation, stereo, or monocular depth, and copes with that noise itself. It also improves from execution feedback, reflecting on its own rollouts and distilling a few rules per task. SOLVE reaches state-of-the-art performance of 74.5% on RoboDojo and 84.5% on RoboLab, with only $1.7 per episode on average, 12x less than previous dense-control agent policies. On a real robot, the same agent achieves an 88% success rate (75 of 85 episodes) over 16 challenging zero-shot tasks. Our project page is at https://serenos.github.io/solve-website.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34750) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34750)

## 35. Was the Model Given the Right Action? Auditing and Repairing Action Interfaces in Navigation World Models

- **作者**：Ajmain Istiak Apon, Md Mehedi Hasan, Sejuti Rahman
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.LG, cs.RO
- **备注**：26 pages, 8 figures, 10 tables

World models are evaluated by predicting future observations from actions. These evaluations assume that actions are prepared the same way as during training, but an unchecked mismatch can make a model appear less accurate than it is. We audit this assumption in Navigation World Models (NWM) and find three interacting differences between its training and rollout code. Correcting the inputs, without retraining, reduces mean visual prediction error by 42%, measured with DreamSim on the RECON navigation dataset. The correction also changes the ranking of NWM checkpoints in an exploratory analysis and removes or reduces two alternative models' measured advantages. Correcting only the normalization offset instead increases error, showing why interacting mismatches must be checked together. To test whether sensitivity to input preparation extends beyond NWM, we deliberately introduce encoding errors into two alternative models; both become less accurate. We introduce ActRosetta, a tool that checks whether action encoding matches training, identifies supported causes of disagreement, and repairs inputs when sufficient information is available. Repair helps little where predictions barely respond to actions, the tool's diagnosis has limited coverage, and improved control remains unproven. For any action-conditioned world model, the lesson is the same: before reporting rollout error, verify that evaluation encodes actions exactly as training did.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34739) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34739)

## 36. WorldInteract: Controllable Streaming Video Generation for Multi-Material Interactions

- **作者**：Ahmed Hatem, Caoliwen Wang, Robert Hoang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Video world models aim to create interactive physical worlds that capture how diverse materials move and interact. Yet material-dependent dynamics remain underexplored in controllable video generation, limiting reliable control in scenes with different materials. We present WorldInteract, a streaming video generation framework that decomposes cross-material interaction into material-dependent behavior generation and contact response computation. External pushes and contact impulses are expressed through a shared velocity-increment interface, allowing material behaviors learned from interaction-free videos to be reused in interacting scenes. A physics controller predicts contacts and computes velocity increments from estimated masses and restitution coefficients, while a causal generator executes these increments using learned material behaviors. This separation enables cross-material interaction without supervised contact videos spanning diverse material pairings and contact configurations. We train the generator on 30k simulated interaction-free videos spanning 22 material classes and introduce Material-Aware RL, using verifiable physical rewards to refine material behavior and control execution. WorldInteract substantially outperforms state-of-the-art methods on Physics-IQ and delivers more reliable push control on real-world images.

## 37. LiDAR Intensity Reconstruction with Limited Real Data: An Architecture Benchmark under Fixed-Epoch Training

- **作者**：Christopher M. Coovrey, Aniruddha Bora
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.LG

Physics-derived simulation can supplement scarce real-world LiDAR intensity measurements, but its effectiveness depends on the learning architecture and the amount of real training data available. We benchmark nine neural architectures for LiDAR intensity reconstruction from depth, semantic labels, incidence angle, and material reflectance, using real SemanticKITTI and synthetic VoxelScape range-image data. All models follow a fixed 20-epoch protocol; this controls the number of training passes, not the computational cost across architectures. We report mean absolute error (MAE), mean squared error (MSE), peak signal-to-noise ratio (PSNR), structural similarity (SSIM), inference timing, and spectral-energy agreement. We also evaluate four selected architectures with fixed-size training mixtures containing 0\%, 1\%, 5\%, 10\%, 20\%, 50\%, or 100\% real frames. With real-only training, UNet-V2 achieves the best reported reconstruction metrics (MAE 0.234, MSE 0.140, PSNR 8.64 dB, and SSIM 0.647), followed by a denoising diffusion model (DDPM). In several mixtures containing 1--10\% real data, DDPM achieves lower error and higher image-quality metrics than the evaluated deterministic alternatives, whereas UNet-V2 leads with 100\% real data. Spectral analysis additionally reveals architecture-dependent discrepancies across frequency bands. These findings characterize reconstruction trade-offs under the stated data mixtures and fixed-epoch protocol, without establishing compute-matched superiority or isolating the diffusion objective from backbone differences.

## 38. Reading, Not Adapting: Unlocking Frozen Visual Knowledge for AI-Generated Image Detection

- **作者**：Yuncheng Guo, Ying Dong, Xiaodong Gu
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：15 pages, 6 figures, and 9 tables

Generalization to unseen image generators has driven efforts to adapt pretrained visual representations for detection. Yet recent findings show that vision foundation models already encode transferable detection knowledge, while further adaptation can impair generalization. This motivates a shift from backbone adaptation to knowledge readout. Conventional linear probing offers a direct way to read this knowledge but is limited to final-layer RGB features. Multi-cue detectors broaden the evidence base, but their designs introduce other tradeoffs: adaptation may compromise pretrained transferability, heterogeneous encoders complicate feature integration, and late fusion precludes intermediate cross-view interaction. To overcome these limitations, we propose ReMoT, a Mixture-of-Transformers-inspired framework that reads complementary knowledge from spatially aligned RGB and wavelet views within a fully frozen visual foundation. ReMoT employs staged joint attention to enable controlled local and global cross-view interaction, while learnable fusion queries progressively read complementary evidence across Transformer depth without writeback. ReMoT achieves 91.2% average accuracy across eight benchmark groups with 3,074 trainable parameters, attaining the highest overall average among the evaluated methods. These results support structured knowledge readout as a promising alternative to backbone adaptation. Code is available at https://github.com/yunncheng/ReMoT.

## 39. Effect of class proportion shifts on classifier curvature

- **作者**：Maxim Bolshim, Alexander Kugaevskikh
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.LG, cs.CV
- **备注**：8 pages, 2 tables

Changing the class mix can alter mean loss without changing the classifier. To distinguish this reweighting from changes in the model itself, we studied ten-class CIFAR-10 classification in separate branches for two target classes, measuring the trace of the loss Hessian with respect to the output-layer parameters on fixed images of each target class. Across ten new initialisations, after 60 updates to the output layer with frozen features, the trace changed by -0.152% on average in branches where the target class share rose from 10% to 19%, and by +0.071% in branches where it fell to 1%, relative to each branch's initial trace. These changes do not isolate the effect of class share from that of the assigned training images. We developed a forecast based on initial class gradients after examining the first experiment's results and then tested it prospectively without modification. For direct increases from 10% to 19%, rather than increases after a deficit, its mean absolute error was 0.022 percentage points on the initial-trace scale, compared with 0.152 for a zero-change forecast and 0.104 for equally weighted gradients. Using the assigned class proportions improved the forecast of how much the trace would change.

## 40. Seeing the Right Context: Grounded On-Policy Distillation for Remote Sensing

- **作者**：Yichang Luo, Hourun Zhu, Shixiong Liu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：20 pages, including supplementary material

Remote sensing scene understanding requires multimodal large language models (MLLMs) to interpret queried targets in relation to nearby objects and surrounding structures, which serve as spatial references or define the scope of an answer. However, standard answers and bounding boxes often leave these contextual relationships implicit. Thus, we construct GeoContext-5K to make them explicit in 5,394 training pairs for visual question answering (VQA), with spatial cues, visual facts, and supporting regions. GeOPD uses these annotations for on-policy distillation, guiding a frozen teacher with region marks on the full image and a visual fact while preserving surrounding spatial references. It prioritizes teacher corrections that agree with the shift in the student's next token distribution when evidence is restored. A complementary objective weights the contrast between the student's scores on the two views by how much masking lowers the teacher's log probability for the same sampled token. Experiments on five remote sensing VQA benchmarks show that GeOPD outperforms the evaluated open-source MLLMs and matched post-training baselines, improving over the 4B and 9B base models by 5.81 and 2.97 percentage points on average. The dataset and code will be publicly released at https://github.com/YeeCharm/GeOPD.

## 41. ShowNow: Fine-Grained Speculative Streaming for Responsive World Models

- **作者**：Yiren Song, Guian Fang, Mike Zheng Shou
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：23 pages, including appendix. Video examples provided as ancillary files

Interactive world models must generate video continuously while responding promptly to user actions. Real-time throughput alone leaves input latency unresolved: coarse generation blocks delay action updates, and generation after input adds work to the response path. We introduce ShowNow, a fine-grained speculative streaming framework that uses computation available during playback to prepare action-conditioned futures before input arrives. Single-latent commitment enables frequent decisions, while configurable preparation covers one to eight actions. We construct a matched multi-future dataset to train alternative continuations from a common state; shared-history tree execution and fused commitment make their preparation efficient. On a single H100, our 5B model sustains 480p generation at 16 fps with eight prepared futures. In ViZDoom, the complete ShowNow deployment reduces median input-to-display latency from 620 to 208 ms relative to the native-block SolarWM-FT deployment. Matched-policy experiments reduce restart empty ticks from 1.56% to 0.09% (94%) and improve on-time responses to action changes. These results establish complementary roles for fine-grained commitment and advance preparation, with measured trade-offs in visual fidelity and long-horizon consistency. ShowNow turns playback-time computation into responsive visual interaction. Project: https://github.com/showlab/ShowNow.

## 42. Find the Clue, Not the Scene: Rethinking Reasoning for Entity Recognition in Vision-Language Models

- **作者**：Kyungmin Min, Soyeon Kim, Junseok Kim et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI

Answering a knowledge-based visual question requires first recognizing the entity in the image and then recalling knowledge about it. Thinking vision-language models (VLMs) reason for thousands of tokens before answering, yet it remains underexplored how well such thinking VLMs perform each of these two steps. We therefore analyze thinking VLMs on this task and make two key observations. (1) Recognition is the bottleneck, since 76.3% of errors across four models on ViQuAE stem from misrecognizing the entity, and 84.9% of these failures are resolved once the entity name is provided in the question. (2) Recognition is driven by entity-recall clues rather than by general descriptions of the image. An entity-recall clue is a distinctive visual detail that helps the model recall the entity, such as keys, a traditional attribute of Saint Peter. Based on these findings, we propose ClueRL, a reinforcement learning approach that extends Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) with an advantage localized to the entity-recall clue in the thinking trace. Rather than injecting new entity knowledge, ClueRL changes how VLMs think: it leads them to attend more closely to visual details and spot these clues, helping them use the knowledge they already have. Consequently, it outperforms both GRPO and a perception-reward baseline on three entity recognition benchmarks. Moreover, this clue-seeking behavior generalizes beyond entity recognition, the only task used for training. Since recognition is the bottleneck, the gains carry over to knowledge-based visual question answering. They even extend to general multimodal reasoning, raising Qwen3.5-4B by 4.4 and 4.0 points on the ScienceQA and MMMU subsets of MMStar, respectively.

## 43. Neural Rendering Compiler: Generative Video Models as Programmable Renderers

- **作者**：Yuxia Zhu, Xinglong Xu, Hao Liang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.CL

Generative video models are becoming powerful renderers, yet lack the predictable control interfaces of conventional systems such as HTML/CSS engines or Blender. Their capabilities emerge implicitly from training, making it difficult to determine what they can reliably execute. We present Neural Rendering Compiler (NeurCo), which automatically probes frozen video models to construct model-specific capability manuals describing which semantic requirements they can reliably satisfy. NeurCo represents user intent in a model-agnostic intermediate representation of objects, constraints, events, and cameras, then compiles it into model-specific execution strategies. Reliable requirements remain unchanged, while unreliable ones are selectively rewritten or compiled into alternative execution strategies. We introduce VComBench, a benchmark of 380 compositional, multi-requirement tasks spanning text-to-video, image-to-video, and reference-to-video generation. NeurCo improves requirement satisfaction from 46.8% to 52.3% on Wan and from 66.5% to 69.3% on Minimax H3, and achieves further gains when combined with prompt enhancers, including on Seedance 2.5. Our analysis reveals model-specific differences in control capabilities and trade-offs between controllability and photorealism, highlighting capability-guided compilation as a promising approach to programmable generative video rendering.

## 44. RoboEvo$^2$: Recursive Robot Self-Improvement via Harness-Policy Co-Evolution

- **作者**：Chenzhi Liu, Jiehong Lin, Wei Huang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.AI, cs.CV
- **备注**：Project page: https://kaiknower.github.io/RoboEvo2/

Robots should adapt to unfamiliar environments and acquire new skills through interaction, then execute those skills efficiently with practice. Vision-language-action policies provide efficient control, but often need additional human demonstrations to handle unfamiliar settings; reasoning agents can discover solutions using broad prior knowledge and robot-control APIs, but repeated deliberation and repair are costly. Inspired by human learning through reasoning and practice, we propose RoboEvo$^2$, a framework for recursive harness-policy co-evolution. In Policy-to-Harness (P2H) evolution, policy failures guide a coding agent to search complementary Code, Code+ICL, and Code+Policy routes, consolidating verified solutions into reusable skills, routing strategies, and experience memory. In Harness-to-Policy (H2P) evolution, verified trajectories provide supervised training, while Counterfactual Preference Distillation (CPD) teaches the policy to favor successful corrections over failures from the same starting state. The improved policy provides stronger motor skills for subsequent search, while its remaining failures redirect harness evolution, closing the reciprocal loop. Through recursive co-evolution without additional human demonstrations, RoboEvo$^2$ improves standalone success from 11.0% to 84.1% and hybrid success from 30.4% to 90.5% on LIBERO-PRO, with similar gains and reduced agentic costs on RoboTwin C2R. Further experiments demonstrate autonomous task acquisition, cross-embodiment harness transfer, and 95.0% hybrid success on the Unitree G1-D. Our models and code will be publicly released.

## 45. Counting Beyond Frames: Codec Motion Tells Frozen Diffusion VLMs How Many

- **作者**：Zhuoqin Li, Jiayu Ding, Ge Li
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Counting how many times something happens is among the simplest questions about a video and among the hardest for video-language models, autoregressive and diffusion alike. The difficulty lies between the frames: a model samples only a few of them, while each repetition unfolds as movement in between. Video codecs already record this movement densely as block displacements, and codec-based video models use it to shape their visual input. We are the first to show that this motion provides direct evidence for the count: a candidate count is a testable motion hypothesis, and unwinding the cycles it implies measures how strongly the observed directions support it. We present CoDiCount, a unified training-free framework that turns this test into a count potential and applies it while a frozen diffusion vision-language model (dVLM) denoises its answer. At every denoising step, structured count messages map each option letter, or each grammar-valid edit of an open integer, to the count it expresses and reweight the model's own probabilities within that set. Consistent gains over the official decoders on MotionBench, TOMATO, and SVCBench demonstrate the broad generalization of CoDiCount across diffusion models, datasets, and diverse counting tasks. With CoDiCount, a 7B dVLM outperforms GPT-5.1 and Gemini-2.5-Pro on MotionBench, surpasses GPT-4o and much larger open models on TOMATO, and matches GPT-5.4 on SVCBench.
