## 88. SemProxy: Semantic 3D Proxies for Controllable Agentic 3D Modeling

- **作者**：Shuyuan Zhang, Changjian Li
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI

Coding agents are increasingly capable of generating 3D models, yet enabling users to effectively control the modeling process remains challenging. Existing workflows rely primarily on text prompts and reference images, which often struggle to convey precise spatial intentions and require iterative corrections. We introduce SemProxy, a lightweight intermediate representation that bridges intuitive human interaction and agentic 3D modeling. Instantiated as labeled 3D bounding boxes, semantic 3D proxies allow users to directly specify object parts and spatial arrangements while leaving geometric details to coding agents. These proxies can be serialized into structured text and rendered as images, making them directly accessible to existing coding agents without additional training or modifications. We further develop ProxyStudio, a user interface that integrates text, image references, and interactive 3D proxy editing into agentic modeling workflows. We demonstrate the versatility of SemProxy across object- and scene-level modeling tasks with different coding agents, and evaluate its effectiveness in supporting controllable human-agent collaborative 3D modeling through a user study.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34414) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34414)

## 89. Strong Starts, Simple Recipes: Unified Multi-Teacher On-Policy Distillation from Multimodal Specialists

- **作者**：Wenzheng Chang, Taiqiang Wu, Yuqi Xu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.LG, cs.CL, cs.CV
- **备注**：Project page, code, and model weights: https://amberheart.github.io/Uni-MOPD/

Multi-teacher on-policy distillation (MOPD) integrates separately trained domain specialists into a single unified model. Existing methods focus on refining the distillation procedure while initializing the student from a base model or a multi-domain SFT checkpoint, which leaves capability integration largely to distillation itself. Instead, we treat the starting point as a design variable and propose Unified MOPD (Uni-MOPD), which pairs a strong start with a simple recipe: a training-free uniform merge of specialists from the same base model initializes the student, and short MOPD with uniform domain sampling and full-vocabulary reverse KL refines it. Across five multimodal domains and 19 benchmarks, our study establishes three findings. (1) The starting point matters more than subsequent training choices: before any training, the uniform merge already matches the score that the base model reaches after 200 MOPD updates, and after short distillation it matches higher-scoring, search-based starts. (2) Short MOPD recovers most of the capabilities the merge leaves unintegrated; it improves every domain, with the largest gains where the domain teacher still leads. (3) From a strong start, uniform domain sampling and full-vocabulary reverse KL match or exceed every evaluated sampling strategy and divergence, without schedules, per-token weighting, or truncation hyperparameters. Uni-MOPD outperforms Open-MOPD at the same training budget while training 3.3 times faster than its released code. We will release the training framework, specialist checkpoints, and unified student.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34397) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34397)

## 90. Plausible Is Not Proven: Testing Failure Explanations in Monocular Depth Estimation

- **作者**：Zeeshan Ali, Md Rayyan, Anshuman Chakravorty et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.LG
- **备注**：17 pages, 7 figures, 14 tables. Code, ratings and results: https://github.com/Md-Zeeshan-Ali/vision-error-observatory

Failure analyses of monocular depth models often name a cause for each error, such as an occluding boundary, a thin object or a reflective surface, but such labels are rarely tested. We present the Vision Error Observatory (VEO), an audit that finds regions failing the $δ_1$ criterion on sensor-measured pixels, labels each with a likely cause or abstains, and then subjects the labels to four tests: agreement with object annotations on data the label model never saw, agreement with blinded human raters, response to targeted image edits, and value as a training signal. We apply it to eight models on NYU Depth V2, DIODE and iBims-1, with labels also checked on Hypersim, and fix most decision rules before their results. The occluding-boundary label agrees with annotations (AUROC 0.85 on NYU, 0.81 on Hypersim, 0.69 on iBims-1), but a single depth-edge cue does as well, and most boundary failures of the strongest model vanish under a 2-pixel tolerance. The reflection label does not transfer to synthetic data, the textureless label is near chance against annotations, and only one of four edit tests, an exploratory one, supports its label. Evaluated against five blinded raters, VEO's labels matched the human consensus only at chance level (Cohen's $κ$ = 0.05). In addition, an increase in the weight on the diagnosed regions during fine-tuning led to an increase in the error on targeted pixels at every weight tested. The architecture-specific failure fingerprint claimed by an earlier version of this project also failed to replicate across the datasets. Plausible failure explanations, in short, need to be tested before they are trusted; we release the protocol, code, ratings and every result file.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34393) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34393)

## 91. MoCA4D: Monocular 3D Bounding Box Lifting and Tracking with Camera and Object Motion

- **作者**：Changwoo Jeon, Jongyoo Kim
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：20 pages, 5 figures, including supplementary material

Monocular 3D bounding box tracking must account for both object motion and changes in camera viewpoint. Framewise box lifting with temporal filtering leaves these motion components implicit, while static-scene fusion assumes stationary objects. We introduce MoCA4D, a framework for 3D box lifting and tracking from monocular video, associated 2D box prompts, and camera intrinsics. An observation branch estimates per-frame geometry, while a dynamic branch predicts a camera transformation shared across objects and a separate rigid transformation for each object. We propose Star Optimization to refine current depth and orientation using the observation and historical boxes propagated through these transformations. The refined box then guides subsequent propagation. Across all evaluated objects in Argoverse 2 and ADT, Star Optimization reduces center motion error by 38.1% and 38.7%, respectively, relative to Kalman filtering of the same observations. On MOVi-F, the dynamic branch achieves the lowest corner and rotation errors and the highest IoU among the evaluated methods over eight-transition motion windows. Real-data camera-only controls further link predicted object motion to improved localization on dynamic-object tracks.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34370) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34370)

## 92. One Patch, Many Policies: Object-Associated Physical Patch Attacks on Robotic Manipulation

- **作者**：Yiqian Liu, Yuqi Lin, Hui Lu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Vision-Language-Action (VLA) and World Action Models (WAMs) are vulnerable to adversarial visual interference, motivating the study of universal and transferable physical patch attacks. We propose Object-Associated Patch Attack (OAPA), a universal and transferable physical patch framework that enables an attacker to select which task object's manipulation to disrupt through patch placement, without re-optimizing the patch. Our framework combines object-associated placement, differentiable physical compositing, and final action-expert representation divergence. Physical compositing accounts for perspective and occlusion across camera views, while representation divergence guides patch optimization using either relative RMS or cosine distance. Experimental results demonstrate strong attack transferability across task suites and policies, together with the ability to selectively disrupt manipulation of an attacker-chosen task object through patch placement.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34360) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34360)

## 93. AIRIS: Benchmarking Social Embodied Intelligence in Egocentric Streaming Videos

- **作者**：Yuchen Zhou, Hongsheng Wu, Jiayu Tang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Embodied agents operating around people must not only recognize social cues, but also decide when the available evidence is sufficient, whether to intervene, and how to respond without violating social norms. Existing benchmarks typically evaluate these abilities in isolation or with access to completed videos, obscuring where an online social decision fails. We introduce AIRIS, a causal streaming benchmark that formulates social embodied intelligence as a time-anchored chain of Attention, Intention, Reaction, and Interaction (A-I-R-I). AIRIS contains 8,000 expert-curated questions grounded in 357 egocentric clips across 11 context categories and 36 capability dimensions. At each query, models receive only the observation prefix available at that time; decision-aware metrics reward calibrated waiting and complete reactions while penalizing premature commitment. Evaluating 45 proprietary and open-source general-purpose, embodied, streaming-video, and omni-modal models reveals fragmented and temporally miscalibrated competence. General-purpose VLMs excel at passive cue grounding and intent recognition, whereas embodied specialists lead on isolated reaction sub-decisions; yet across all families, models frequently guess intents before sufficient evidence emerges and fail to jointly align intervention timing, action domain, and concrete execution within the same episode. AIRIS thus provides a diagnostic foundation for bridging the gap between passive social perception and reliable embodied interaction.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34347) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34347)

## 94. SPARC: Semantic Calibration with Latent Multimodal Knowledge for Robust Image-Text Retrieval

- **作者**：Zhongkuan Mao, Wenzhuo Zhao, Haidong Chen et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Robust image-text retrieval aims to maintain accurate cross-modal matching when image or text queries are incomplete, supporting reliable information access in real-world applications. However, existing methods typically focus on adapting to distribution shifts, without adequately addressing candidate discrimination for incomplete queries and ranking instability. To this end, we propose semantic projection and alignment with relational constraints (SPARC), which elicits latent multimodal knowledge from frozen encoders to improve candidate discrimination and ranking stability. Specifically, we first devise a semantic calibration module on a product of spheres that refines incomplete visual and textual features through tangent-space updates, maintaining the unit-sphere structure used for cosine similarity. Second, we introduce a robust semantic guidance module that combines latent multimodal knowledge encoded in complete image-text features with cross-modal semantic anchors to promote semantic alignment of the calibrated features. Finally, we introduce a matching relation constraint module that fuses original query features with their calibrated counterparts and applies a soft ranking constraint to penalize reductions in initially positive matching margins, thereby limiting ranking perturbations. Extensive experiments on Flickr30K and COCO demonstrate that SPARC outperforms competing methods under various image occlusion and word deletion settings, while achieving consistent gains across different pretrained backbones.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34344) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34344)

## 95. PostMesh: Self-Rewarded Preference Post-Training for Flow-Based Artist Mesh Generation

- **作者**：Gihyun Kwon
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Artistic mesh generators turn a dense 3D object into an artist mesh: a compact, quad-dominant mesh that is easy to edit. Most of current models are auto-regressive methods which decode a mesh token by token, take tens to hundreds of seconds per mesh. Diffusion- and flow-based generators are much faster, but their meshes still fall short of artist quality. To our knowledge, PostMesh is the first post-training method for a diffusion- or flow-based artist mesh generator, and it needs no expensive artist-made ground truth: the generator is trained on its own samples, scored by a reward computed from the dense input and the output. We propose an artistic mesh reward that keeps the output consistent with the dense input while making the mesh structure into artistic. We also propose a multi-negative flow preference optimisation that transfers the reward to the generator. After a short LoRA post-training, PostMesh is comparable to or better than slow autoregressive generators on most artist-mesh quality metrics and in a blind VLM preference test, at a fraction of their time.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34319) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34319)

## 96. WebVibe: Multimodal Interactive Website Generation via Reverse Synthesis and Visual Self-Distillation

- **作者**：Tianxiang Jiang, Sheng Xia, Yilin Long et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI

Although multimodal large language models translate single screenshots into static HTML, generating interactive functional websites from design specifications still struggles with multi-page routing and stateful widget transitions. Examining existing training pipelines reveals two bottlenecks: (1) in forward data synthesis (X -> Y), writing the specification X before code execution leaves unimplemented or altered requirements in X as supervision noise; and (2) in reinforcement learning, scalar rewards discard the rich state transitions observed during replay. Based on these findings, we introduce WebVibe and Interactive-SDPO to train InteractiveCoder. WebVibe reverses synthesis into Y -> X by verifying cross-page and in-page interactions in the browser before deriving multimodal inputs from replay-confirmed states to build the 31,542-site WebVibe-32K corpus with reusable execution contracts. Interactive-SDPO then conditions a weight-sharing self-teacher on the replay trace and a 2x2 state-transition montage to provide token-level supervision at zero inference-time overhead. On the public Vision2Web L2, one-shot InteractiveCoder-9B nearly 3x its base model's functional score (20.2 to 55.2 FS) and matches a 1T-parameter coding agent in a single pass with 111x fewer total parameters and 78x fewer tokens. On our WebVibeBench, InteractiveCoder-4B and 9B further outperform all evaluated baselines by over 23 points.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34292) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34292)

## 97. StateWeaver: Multi-Agent Coordination of Story-State Transitions for Long-Horizon Video Generation

- **作者**：Modi Jin, Xuying Zhang, Yijing Guo et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI

Modern video foundation models generate high-quality short clips, but preserving long-video consistency and correctly depicting event-driven state changes remain challenging. Existing agentic workflows face these challenges because continual planning and repair accumulate context, while reference updates remain limited. We introduce StateWeaver, which combines Active States and Hierarchical Collaboration to connect review-driven visual reference updates with cross-shot state inheritance. Active States translate state discrepancies identified during review into concrete visual asset requirements that guide reference construction, validation, and regeneration, preserving identity and already-correct content while repairing target attributes. Confirmed generation results are linked to the corresponding asset states, story time, and reference versions for verification and reuse by subsequent shots. Hierarchical Collaboration isolates execution contexts by task, clip, and repair round, and uses hierarchical memory to organize this process across multiple sets. Across multiple benchmarks, StateWeaver outperforms existing methods and produces long videos with sustained cross-shot consistency under predefined and autonomously planned storyboards.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34290) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34290)

## 98. Rehearse in 3D, Render in 2D: Physically Consistent and Editable Robot World Models

- **作者**：Guangyu Liu, Hanzhang Wang, Huijia Zhang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.CV
- **备注**：Includes supplementary material

Embodied world models aim to predict future interactions that are both visually realistic and physically consistent. Yet explicit 3D motion prediction alone does not guarantee that object structure and contact relationships are preserved. Beyond motion prediction, 3D representations provide an explicit state space for imposing structural and physical constraints. Building on this insight, we introduce RehearsalWorld, an editable robot world model driven by 3D Interaction Rehearsal (3DIR). Given an initial RGB-D observation and robot actions, Interaction-Consistent Rehearsal (ICR) uses a pretrained 3D point-flow model as a motion prior. It refines the predicted trajectories through response-aware object grouping, persistent grasp memory, and contact structure refiner, preserving scene stability, object structure, and grasp attachments. Rehearsal-Aligned Video Generation (RAVG) then uses geometric projections of the rehearsal as temporal conditioning. We fine-tune a video generator on projection-video pairs to synthesize realistic interaction videos. Thanks to the decoupling of physical response from visual synthesis, RehearsalWorld also supports independent editing of material properties and camera viewpoints. Compared with PointWorld on real DROID grasping sequences, RehearsalWorld reduces gripper-relative offset by approximately 79% to 84% across deformable, articulated, and rigid objects and lowers reference motion error. For video prediction, it achieves a PSNR of 23.92 dB and an FVD of 149.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34288) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34288)

## 99. ReMeDi: Single-Image PBR Material Estimation via Reparameterization between Metal and Dielectric Hypotheses

- **作者**：Fan Fei, Xin Huang, Yixin Zhu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.GR
- **备注**：27 pages, 16 figures. Project page: https://costrice.github.io/remedi/

Physically based rendering (PBR) material estimation facilitates relighting, material editing, and 3D content creation. As the reflectance of a surface heavily depends on whether it is a metal or a dielectric, producing any map that determines albedo involves a decision on metalness, which we call the "intended metalness" of the map. Rendering maps with mismatched intended metalness yields physically implausible or misinterpreted materials. The metallic--roughness workflow makes metalness explicit and thus encourages base color to match it, but does not enforce this match, especially for automated methods directly predicting each map. Therefore, we propose ReMeDi, which enforces the match through two components. First, we reparameterize base color as a metalness-weighted blend of its two variants, estimated under the all-metal and all-dielectric hypothesis, respectively. Second, as available supervision cannot teach a single network both hypotheses at every pixel, we restrict each of two separate networks to one hypothesis, so that each variant is ensured to follow its hypothesis. Besides enforcing the match, this also improves each estimated material map. Experiments show that ReMeDi outperforms state-of-the-art methods in both material accuracy and relighting fidelity.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34284) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34284)

## 100. Semantic State Reading for Joint Image-Semantic Diffusion Transformers

- **作者**：Guanxi Lai, Zixuan Li, Zhiwei Li et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：28 pages, 14 figures, including supplementary material

Pretrained visual representations improve diffusion-transformer training, yet exploiting both global context and local spatial structure requires deciding which semantic states to model and how to access them. We introduce Semantic State Reading (SSR), which jointly denoises image latents, a global CLS token encoding image-level context, and a compact patch state preserving spatial correspondence. SSR couples this global-local modeling with distinct reading paths: local states are fused with corresponding image tokens at the input, while independent projections inject the current global state into every transformer block. Both semantic scopes thus remain accessible throughout denoising. Ablations show complementary benefits from local-state modeling and repeated global reading, and semantic-trajectory interventions alter image layout under a fixed class label. On ImageNet $256 \times 256$, SiT-XL/2 with SSR reaches or surpasses the reference FIDs of SiT, REPA, and Representation Entanglement for Generation (REG) with $100\times$, $40\times$, and $2\times$ fewer training iterations, respectively. SSR achieves unguided FID 1.78 at 2M iterations and FID 1.34 with classifier-free guidance alone. Combining classifier-free guidance with autoguidance yields FID 1.31 at 1M iterations, achieving the best FID in our main comparison table.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34279) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34279)

## 101. TORE: Token-Wise Taylor Order Selection via Decomposed Residual Error Estimation for Diffusion Acceleration

- **作者**：Lei Tang, Baoquan Zhang, Yakun Zhang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Diffusion models excel at image and video generation, but iterative denoising incurs substantial inference latency. Recent token-adaptive acceleration methods select Taylor prediction orders for the full-model residual using shallow-layer proxy scores. However, candidate orders are evaluated on these proxy features but used to predict the full-model residual, creating a proxy--target mismatch: the proxy-preferred order may not minimize residual prediction error. To address this mismatch, we propose \textbf{TORE} (\textbf{T}aylor \textbf{O}rder selection via \textbf{R}esidual \textbf{E}rror estimation), which selects token-wise Taylor orders based on estimated prediction errors for the full-model residual. Yet direct error evaluation requires future ground-truth residuals, which are unavailable during order selection. TORE therefore estimates prediction error by analyzing how errors arise in Taylor extrapolation. Specifically, it decomposes prediction error into truncation error and finite-difference derivative error, then estimates both contributions from cached residuals to preselect token-wise orders for upcoming skip steps. Extensive experiments show that TORE achieves $4.31\times$ speedup on FLUX.1-dev and $4.18\times$ on Wan2.1 while maintaining high generation quality and fidelity.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34261) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34261)

## 102. Detector Operating Behavior Inference via Data-Space Sampling

- **作者**：Stefan Becker, Simon Weiss, Wolfgang Hübner
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

A detector scores candidate detections, but the recall and false alarms at a given confidence threshold depend on the input distribution. We introduce Detector Operating Behavior Inference (DOBI), a second-level inference task: estimating a fixed detector's threshold-dependent, distribution-level operating response from the internal evidence of a single unlabeled image. DOBI treats the detector's threshold response, i.e. recall, false positives, and precision across thresholds, as a latent behavioral state under the current data condition. Controlled data-space sampling probes this state space offline, and a lightweight monitor learns the inverse map from one image to this state, whose single functional output supports multiple operating policies. In a controlled proof of concept with a fixed YOLOv10-Nano person detector and COCO-derived degradations, predicted PR behavior reduces mean absolute deviation from 0.128 for an image-independent mean-behavior prior to 0.054, and predicted functional distances track empirical regime distances (Spearman 0.96). From one unlabeled image, the inferred behavior selects operating points as well as a non-deployable lookup that knows the true regime (macro-regime $F_1$ 0.6604 for both, versus 0.6407 under pristine calibration; oracle 0.6620). On this discrete benchmark, regime-supervised curve lookups are strong controls. Pre-specified continuous and compositional probing shows where functional inference matters: on unseen combinations, the functional decoder has a lower mean curve error than a template lookup, increasingly with distance from the training conditions, whereas the lookup is more accurate for interpolation. These results establish the feasibility of single-image inference of distribution-level detector behavior over the probed conditions. Source code is available at https://github.com/beckerio/dobi

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34252) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34252)

## 103. Analemma $\infty$: A Temporal Benchmark for Geospatial Foundation Models

- **作者**：Alex López-Cifuentes, Laura Martínez-Ferrer, Juan Ignacio Bravo Pérez-Villar
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：30 pages, 13 figures, 17 tables. Includes supplementary material

Satellite time series capture how land surfaces change over time. Pixel-wise temporal foundation models read a year of satellite observations per pixel, yet public benchmarks lack a temporal axis or subsample it to a few dates. We introduce Analemma, a temporal benchmark of six datasets that carry every 10 and 20 m Sentinel-2 band and a time series spanning a growing season, completed where their releases omit geometry, dates, or cloud masks. One frozen-encoder protocol evaluates all models across label ratios and three seeds, with two baselines without pretraining. The TESSERA model ranks first overall. A from-scratch model on monthly composites outranks every foundation model except OlmoEarth v1.3-Base, suggesting that temporal input explains most of the performance. Three evaluation choices left unstated by the source publications each shift TESSERA v1 by 7.6 to 18.6 mIoU, against top-two gaps of 0.6 to 2.8. Data, harness and leaderboard will be publicly released soon.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34240) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34240)

## 104. VibeVideoAgent: Closing the Perceptual Loop for Code-Driven Video Generation

- **作者**：Xuanyu Zheng, Luoyuan Zhang, Yancheng Long et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Code-driven video generation asks a coding agent to write and run rendering programs. This gives exact control over typography, data visualizations, synchronized audio, and multi-shot scenes, and it leaves an editable project behind. A program that exits cleanly can still render overlapping labels, a camera aimed away from its subject, broken object interactions, or narration that drifts out of sync, and none of these raise a runtime error. Feeding rendered frames and audio back into the coding session bloats its long-horizon context, while critiquing only the finished full-resolution render turns early design mistakes into costly rewrites. We introduce VibeVideoAgent, a training-free framework that lets coding agents inspect their drafts throughout production. Multimodal perception runs in short-lived contexts kept apart from the coder and returns compact, grounded Findings. Seven primitives operate on a unified multi-clock representation of assets and drafts: they explore media from coarse to fine, verify quoted requirements with evidence from every required modality, compare a draft with a reference or an earlier revision on separate clocks, and measure stream signals locally. A lightweight staged protocol has the agent test its riskiest decision on a minimal preview first. On all 239 VibeVideoBench tasks, with matched agent runtimes and a fixed perception model, VibeVideoAgent improves holistic audiovisual quality (L3) for Claude Opus 5.5 (4.03 -> 4.43, while L2 rubric compliance rises from 98.87% to 99.35%), GPT-6 Astra (4.03 -> 4.27), and Claude Sonnet 5.5 (3.97 -> 4.10), with the largest gains in audio-visual synchronization (up to +0.56). Controlled ablations show that each component contributes, and trajectory audits show that the granularity of code edits governs the trade-off between rubric adherence and perceptual quality.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34237) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34237)

## 105. OS-Omni: Benchmarking Generalist Computer-Using Agents on Long-Horizon Workflows Across Platforms

- **作者**：Hui Shen, Yunta Hsieh, Jianing Ma et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：NeurIPS 2026

Computer-using agents are increasingly expected to complete realistic user goals rather than isolated GUI actions. However, existing benchmarks typically isolate web, mobile, or desktop environments, leaving it unclear whether a single generalist agent can execute long-horizon workflows that span multiple operating systems and persistent application states. We introduce OS-Omni, a live cross-platform benchmark and execution environment for evaluating computer-using agents across Windows, Ubuntu, macOS, iOS, Android, and Web environments. OS-Omni is released in versioned waves of 300 tasks each; this paper focuses on the first release, v1, which contains 48 tasks per platform and 12 cross-platform workflows that pair a desktop with an Android phone. OS-Omni provides reproducible initialization, platform-specific adapters, trajectory logging, and executable final-state evaluators, and includes controlled service-backed applications for workflows involving account, cart, budget, calendar, message, and other persistent state. Human studies show that OS-Omni tasks require 67.54 interaction steps, substantially exceeding OSWorld references of 15.0 steps. On v1, the strongest evaluated agent, Claude Opus 5.5, reaches 68.5% average success, trailing the 77.3% human reference by 8.8 points; the gap is uneven across platforms, with frontier models exceeding humans on Linux but trailing them by more than 25 points on iOS and macOS. These results indicate that current agents remain far from platform-robust, long-horizon computer use, especially when success requires sustained state tracking and durable final-state changes.

## 106. Weakly Conditioned Diffusion Model Augmentation for Prostate Segmentation

- **作者**：Chibike Ugbam, Muhammad Bilal, Jia Wu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：eess.IV, cs.AI, cs.CV
- **备注**：15 pages, preprint

Deep learning models for prostate MRI segmentation often struggle to generalize when trained on limited heterogeneous data, leading to boundary errors and over-segmentation. We present an augmentation framework using mask-conditioned synthetic and diverse T2-weighted MRI volumes to improve boundary precision and reduce over-segmentation. We employ a 2.5D weakly mask-conditioned denoising diffusion probabilistic model (DDPM) via channel concatenation. The diffusion model conditions on stacks of adjacent binary masks from training folds. The model generates T2 volumes consistent with the conditioning anatomy while diversifying tissue appearance. We evaluate the proposed approach on two cohorts of data: (i) the LUND probe cohort (n=432) for strategy selection and training nnU-Net from scratch with a 23% synthetic data mix, and (ii) the Medical Segmentation Decathlon (MSD) prostate cohort (n=32) evaluated with a 20% synthetic data mix. For the LUND cohort, we found that training an nnU-Net model from scratch with a 23% synthetic data mix reduced the mean false-positive voxels per case from 11,102 (baseline) to 10,757. On the MSD cohort, the augmented model with a 20% synthetic mix achieved a mean cross-validated Dice of 0.9094, improving over the baseline (0.8935) by 1.59 percentage points and reducing mean false-positive voxels by 23.1% (4,201 to 3,229). Experimental results on both these cohorts suggest that mask-conditioned DDPM augmentation can reduce over-segmentation errors and improve boundary adherence in data-constrained settings.

## 107. LiveRelight: Sub-Second Global Illumination Editing for Gaussian Interiors

- **作者**：Noah Rothenberger, Dor Verbin, Serge Belongie et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Relighting reconstructed 3D scenes remains challenging due to the complex nature of light transport: slow or approximate visual feedback restricts the editor's ability to fine-tune the results to satisfaction. We present a hybrid relighting approach that enables interactive, high-fidelity updates of direct and indirect indoor illumination without per-edit optimization. Our approach permits the editor to make a wide range of relighting edits at room-scale, with near-instant feedback. Our key insight is to precompute geometric light exchange rather than illumination responses, retaining an inexpensive transport solve that accommodates changes in emission and surface reflectance. We evaluate direct illumination on reconstructed 2D Gaussians using visibility-aware sampling of one-sided emitters, while propagating diffuse interreflection through sparse radiosity on a triangle proxy. A sparse projection fitted through Gaussian alpha compositing transfers the proxy solution to the reconstruction. Our formulation further supports fast fixture transformations and emitter insertion by recomputing light source injection. Across eight relighting conditions of our synthetic test suite, our sub-second edits improve relighting fidelity over prior path-traced editing by an average of 5\,dB PSNR, while reducing editing latency by up to three orders of magnitude. Qualitative results further demonstrate realistic, interactive relighting of real-world captures.

## 108. EgoMyo: Physical Hand State Estimation from Egocentric Video and Wrist Electromyography

- **作者**：Guanglong Sun, Kanglei Zhou, Ziteng Wang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Website: https://egomyo.github.io/

Physical hand interaction depends on configuration, contact, and force, yet nearly identical appearances can conceal different contact states and exerted forces. We therefore formulate physical hand state estimation from egocentric video and wrist surface electromyography (sEMG), combining visual geometry with neuromuscular evidence to jointly recover these coupled components. To support this task, we introduce EgoMyo, a benchmark spanning 5.75M video frames, 50 participants, 34 contact configurations, and repeated donnings, synchronizing these inputs with 3D hand pose and with contact and normal force registered onto the hand surface from bare-hand pressure. Controlled analysis reveals that vision resolves spatial structure while sEMG better estimates force magnitude, that sEMG's gains concentrate in regions where vision is least reliable, and that contact localization limits force accuracy. Guided by these findings, we propose MyoBridge, which makes the hand region the unit of vision-sEMG fusion, lets each region gate its own share of sEMG, and distributes regional force magnitudes only over predicted contact vertices at inference. On held-out trials, MyoBridge reduces pose and force error by 39.8% and 18.5% over the strongest baseline and achieves the lowest pose error on unseen sessions and subjects.

## 109. AnyCam4D: Ray-Based 4D Gaussian Rendering for Arbitrary Camera Models

- **作者**：Sascha Kirch, Rafael Pagés, Jan Ondřej et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.GR
- **备注**：Project page: https://sascha-kirch.github.io/anycam4d/

Real cameras are not pinholes, yet most dynamic Gaussian splatting assumes they are. Footage is therefore undistorted first, losing field of view or detail, especially with wide-angle and fisheye cameras. Static Gaussian splatting has largely solved this with ray-based rendering, which suits any camera. Native 4D Gaussians, which model motion in space-time, cannot use it directly: each Gaussian's orientation and size cannot be recovered reliably from its covariance. We present AnyCam4D, which trains native 4D Gaussians on original footage by ray-based rasterization, preserving field of view and resolution at about the per-step cost of standard projection. Our key contribution is a numerically stable way to obtain orientation and size: we condition a square root of the 4D covariance. Trained models render through other cameras, with rolling shutter, depth of field or chromatic aberration. With a synthetic 170-degree fisheye camera, training on original images improves peak signal-to-noise ratio (PSNR) by 3.0-8.1 dB. On real footage, AnyCam4D has lower perceptual error than the standard projection adapted to each camera, at comparable PSNR and without camera-specific derivations. With the synthetic camera rotated 60-80 degrees away from the capture rig's view, it leads by 6.2 dB against ground truth.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34223) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34223)

## 110. Variation-aware Temporal Correspondence for Self-Supervised Video Representation Learning

- **作者**：Yang Liu, Qianqian Xu, Peisong Wen et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Accepted by T-PAMI

Self-supervised video representation learning has achieved remarkable progress by leveraging large-scale unlabeled video data. Recent methods commonly extend Masked Image Modeling (MIM) to Masked Video Modeling (MVM), where video representations are learned by capturing semantic information from temporal contexts during masked content recovery. Unlike static images, videos exhibit inherent temporal variation, as visual objects continuously change their positions, appearances, and motion states across frames. Therefore, MVM needs to preserve semantic consistency while capturing temporal changes of corresponding regions, which raises two central challenges: 1) how to establish reliable temporal correspondence across frames, and 2) how to formulate an appropriate representation learning objective for modeling both semantic consistency and temporal variation. To address these challenges, we propose Variation-aware Temporal Correspondence for video Representation learning (VT-CoRe) framework. For challenge 1), VT-CoRe introduces a sandwich sampling strategy to construct two-sided temporal references and a Patch Registration Module (PRM) to establish reliable patch-level correspondence. For challenge 2), VT-CoRe imposes latent-space semantic alignment on registered representations through a self-distillation architecture and further proposes temporal variation modeling to constrain representation trajectories over time. Experiments on 8 downstream tasks across 16 benchmarks demonstrate the effectiveness and applicability of VT-CoRe across pixel-level, frame-level, video-level, and vision-based robotic learning scenarios. Complementary representation analysis shows that VT-CoRe learns a more structured latent space for variation-aware temporal correspondence learning.

## 111. GeoCAM: Self-Supervised Sonar-Optical Representation Learning via Geographic Overlap Supervision

- **作者**：Fadi Abdeladhim Zidi, Andrea De Salve, Giuseppe Celeste et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI
- **备注**：6 pages, 2 figures, 2 tables. Accepted at the IEEE International Conference on Advanced Visual and Signal-Based Systems (AVSS 2026)

Cross-modal self-supervised learning for paired side-scan sonar and underwater optical imagery is a promising direction for benthic habitat understanding, but it remains underexplored because existing methods do not fully exploit the one-to-many correspondences and geographic metadata available in real opti-acoustic surveys. This limits the ability to learn representations that are both semantically meaningful and geometrically consistent from abundant unlabeled marine data. To address this problem, we present GeoCAM, a geography-guided framework that learns a shared embedding space for sonar tiles and their associated optical views without semantic annotations. GeoCAM achieves its goal by combining three components: (i) a Set Attention Aggregator that adaptively summarizes multiple optical observations into a single tile-level representation; (ii) a geometry-weighted InfoNCE objective that converts GPS-derived footprint overlap into soft positive supervision; and (iii) an optional cross-modal masked reconstruction branch that encourages physically grounded cross-modal alignment. Experiments on the BenthiCat SSS-CAM dataset show that GeoCAM can be effectively exploited to learn which sonar and camera samples describe the same seabed patch, improving cross-modal retrieval and alignment over strong contrastive baselines. Code is available at https://github.com/FadiZidiDz/GeoCAM.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34218) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34218)

## 112. Beyond Logits: Coherent Hallucination Mitigation via Attention Contrastive Decoding

- **作者**：Yujia Chen, Rui Sun, Huayu Mai et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI

Large Vision-Language Models (LVLMs) demonstrate impressive multimodal capabilities, yet suffer from hallucination - generating factually inaccurate content. Contrastive Decoding (CD) mitigates this by contrasting amateur and expert branches at the logit level. However, our investigation reveals that such logit-level interventions fundamentally compromise generation coherence, necessitating restrictive penalty constraints unrelated to hallucination suppression. We introduce Attention Contrastive Decoding (ACD), a training-free plug-in that complements logit-level CD by relocating part of the contrastive operations to the attention mechanism. Operating at an earlier stage of the forward pass, ACD performs smooth semantic-preserving interventions through an Adaptive Subtraction Strategy (ASS), which attenuates hallucination-associated attention patterns while amplifying critical visual information. Extensive experiments demonstrate that combining ACD with existing CD methods (e.g., VCD+ACD) produces substantially more coherent outputs with further reduced hallucinations, eliminating restrictive penalties while enabling trustworthy multimodal generation.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34207) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34207)

## 113. Loop4D: Embodied World Modeling with 4D VAEs and Looped Diffusion Transformers

- **作者**：Gen Li, Fan Luo, Mengjun Lin et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Predicting the outcomes of robotic manipulation requires modeling both visual appearance and evolving 3D geometry. This poses two challenges: video compression can obscure fine-grained robot motion, while jointly predicting RGB and geometry demands sufficient generative capacity. We introduce Loop4D, a framework that addresses these challenges through motion-preserving 4D representations and recurrent generative refinement. Our 4D VAE encodes RGB and pointmap sequences into compact spatiotemporal latents while preserving motion details and geometric structure. Our RGB VAE combines geometry-guided supervision with predictive reconstruction to reduce compression-induced motion artifacts. Our geometry VAE uses lightweight adapters to adapt pretrained RGB representations while preserving their priors. Building on these representations, our Dual-Stream Looped Diffusion Transformer maintains separate appearance and geometry streams with shared Transformer weights and cross-modal connections. By reusing Transformer blocks across loops, we increase effective depth without replicating backbone parameters, enabling models with fewer parameters to improve joint RGB-geometry prediction through additional computation. Experiments demonstrate high-quality 4D generation and validate Loop4D in simulated robotic manipulation tasks.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34199) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34199)

## 114. Agentic-Level Adversarial Purification: Revisiting Diffusion Purifiers on Native-Resolution Agentic Inputs

- **作者**：Yan Lin, Zhongliang Guo, Jingyu Lin et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CR, cs.AI, cs.CV

Vision-language agents act on native-resolution renders of multi-element pages, where one perturbed photo or a few altered text strokes can redirect the decision. Any content provider can craft such a perturbation on the open model behind the agent, so a plug-in purifier is the natural first line of defense. Yet diffusion purifiers were designed and evaluated at the fixed resolution of the diffusion model on classifier or natural-image benchmarks, and defense evaluations for agents test JPEG, blur and noise but no diffusion purifier. We present the first study of adversarial purification at the agentic level, for an agent built on Qwen3-VL-8B: purifiers run at native resolution on multi-element inputs, face grey-box attackers who know the agent's model but not the defense, and are compared at matched clean cost. We show that treating agentic inputs as classifier inputs misleads both evaluation and design. Resizing the input to the resolution of the diffusion model, as existing purifiers do, raises the TextVQA clean cost of the Divide-and-Conquer purifier (DC) from 4.0 to 35.7 points. On web-shop pages, DC keeps 85.0% grey-box robust accuracy against web-hardened perturbations, where cheap input transformations at matched clean cost keep at most 40.0%, and this robustness comes from the global noise level: input-estimated masks and noise levels add none. These findings hold on a disjoint held-out set and on a second VLM and yield three requirements for defenses, we release the protocol.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34181) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34181)

## 115. Admoab-DETR: Resolution-Tiered Fusion for Infrared-Visible Object Detection with Missing Modalities

- **作者**：Wenchao Li, Haoran Jia, Shuyuan Wen et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Submitted to CVPR 2027

Infrared and visible images offer complementary cues for all-weather object detection, yet real-world sensor dropouts can leave one stream unavailable and degrade paired-input detectors. Meanwhile, existing modality-adaptive methods apply heavy cross-modal attention uniformly across scales, incurring prohibitive token overhead on high-resolution maps. We propose Admoab-DETR, a unified real-time detector supporting infrared-only, visible-only, and paired inputs with a single set of weights. Specifically, \emph{Resolution-Tiered Fusion} (RTF) allocates fusion capacity by spatial token density and feature granularity: a lightweight Spatial Modality Gate governs fine local fusion at the highest-resolution scale with zero-cost single-view pass-through, while channel-compressed deformable Admoab necks capture cross-modal correspondence at coarser semantic scales. Furthermore, \emph{Mode-Matched Feature Distillation} (MMFD) aligns multi-scale encoder features with an availability-synchronized full-fusion teacher, avoiding ill-posed cross-modal hallucination while cutting parameters by 18.1\% and paired latency by 39.6\% at zero inference overhead. We also construct Admoab, a strictly registered 20,000-pair infrared--visible benchmark across diverse scenes. Experiments show Admoab-DETR achieves state-of-the-art accuracy--efficiency trade-offs across missing-modality and paired benchmarks. Our code will be available at https://github.com/liwenchao0615/Admoab-DETR.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34163) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34163)

## 116. Modality-Shift Contrastive Decoding for Temporally Grounded Audio-Visual Understanding

- **作者**：Minyeong Jeong, Yeonju Kim, Yong Man Ro
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.SD

Audio-visual large language models (AV-LLMs) are designed to represent the temporal structure of audio and video, yet it remains unclear how effectively they use this temporal information to relate sounds to visual events. To examine this, we probe an AV-LLM on an audio-visual synchronization task by shifting the audio so that the queried sound overlaps a different visual event. We observe that the model still favors the originally aligned event over the newly aligned one, indicating limited sensitivity to changes in audio-visual temporal alignment. To address this, we propose Modality-Shift Contrastive Decoding (MSCD), a training-free decoding method that emphasizes audio-visual temporal alignment in model predictions. MSCD contrasts logits under preserved and disrupted temporal alignment to favor predictions supported by aligned audio and video. We disrupt this alignment by temporally shifting either the audio or the video, while keeping the other modality fixed. Extensive evaluations with two AV-LLMs across three benchmarks demonstrate the effectiveness of MSCD for temporally grounded audio-visual understanding. In particular, MSCD improves accuracy over the base models on Daily-Omni by 2.2 and 2.8 percentage points for OmniVinci and video-SALMONN 2+, respectively, suggesting that emphasizing temporal alignment can also benefit broader audio-visual understanding.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34162) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34162)

## 117. OceanFix: Reference-Guided Diffusion for Sparse-View Underwater 3D Reconstruction

- **作者**：Gen Li, Maoxin Chen, Yao Xiao et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Recent advances in Gaussian splatting have enabled efficient underwater reconstruction, but sparse observations, light attenuation, and backscatter still leave scene regions underconstrained and cause novel-view artifacts. We present OceanFix, a reference-guided framework for sparse-view underwater 3D reconstruction that combines diffusion-based view repair with Gaussian scene refinement. Our repair model follows a two-stage training strategy that separates underwater appearance adaptation from geometric refinement. The first stage learns underwater appearance from reference observations and estimated medium features. The second uses camera poses and reference depth to align reference features, with relative-depth supervision guiding structural repair without requiring target-view depth at inference. We further propose medium-aware distillation that transfers repaired views into the 3D scene according to geometric repair demand and channel-specific optical observability. Through iterative rendering, repair, and distillation, OceanFix refines underconstrained regions while retaining supervision from the observed views. Experiments on underwater datasets demonstrate that OceanFix improves rendering quality in sparse-view 3D reconstruction, reducing visible artifacts and recovering finer scene details.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34155) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34155)

## 118. HSTTA: Hierarchical Selective Test-Time Adaptation for Label-Free Cross-Subject EEG-to-Image Retrieval

- **作者**：Hongzhou Chen, Yuhao Wei, Lianghua He et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Cross-subject EEG-to-image retrieval suffers from substantial performance degradation on new subjects due to subject shift. Test-time adaptation (TTA) can mitigate this shift by adapting pretrained models with unlabeled target-subject EEG. However, most existing TTA methods struggle to match supervision granularity to each query's prediction confidence, leaving many queries without reliable pseudo-labels. To address this, we propose a novel framework named Hierarchical Selective Test-Time Adaptation (HSTTA). HSTTA builds a hierarchy over gallery image embeddings, uses hierarchical selective prediction to assign each EEG query a supervision granularity matching its current prediction confidence, and constructs adaptation objectives accordingly. We further propose Cross-Session Consistency Calibration (CSCC), an unsupervised method that calibrates the global confidence threshold to target a predefined accuracy level for hierarchical predictions. Moreover, we introduce Reciprocal Hierarchical Backoff (RHB), which moves queries lacking reverse-matching support to coarser nodes to mitigate error reinforcement. Experiments on two public benchmarks and ablation studies validate the effectiveness of HSTTA and its key components. On THINGS-EEG2, HSTTA outperforms the strongest EEG-specific TTA baseline by 17.9 (13.4) and 8.9 (3.7) percentage points in Top-1/Top-5 accuracy, respectively, under cosine (CSLS) ranking. Further analyses show that HSTTA yields more accurate pseudo-labels during adaptation, supporting more reliable model updates.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34153) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34153)

## 119. Lost in the Grid: Where Multimodal LLMs Fail Between Seeing and Planning

- **作者**：Luoxi Zhang, Tianyi Tan, Jiawei Liu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI
- **备注**：31 pages, 32 figures, 29 tables. Code: https://github.com/suat-llm/GridProbe

Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) excel at static visual understanding and are increasingly applied to spatial planning, where a model interprets a visual layout and outputs an action sequence to reach a goal. Grid games are a common testbed for this ability because their rules are explicit and a solver can verify every move. Yet existing benchmarks mostly report end-to-end success rates, which hide whether a failure stems from visual perception, rule application, or multi-step planning. We introduce GridProbe, a diagnostic benchmark that 1) locates the failing stage between visual perception and planning and 2) verifies the diagnosis by targeted fine-tuning. On Sokoban, GridProbe pairs end-to-end solving with four probes (recognition, localization, single-step prediction, and state tracking) and gives every board both as an image and as an equivalent text grid. Evaluating seven MLLMs on Sokoban and a maze game reveals distinct bottlenecks behind similar success rates: proprietary models are limited by multi-step planning, whereas open-source models are limited by rule application or by visual grid indexing, i.e., counting rows and columns. Planning failures also hide perception failures: when planning is easier, the visual cost of the same model grows from 7.6 to 37.7 points. The diagnosis identifies what to train: fine-tuning only the vision module of the perception-limited model on board reading raises its interactive Sokoban success 3.5-fold (5.8% to 20.4%) and transfers zero-shot to the unseen maze game (single-turn success 13.8% to 67.1%), whereas the same data does not help a rule-limited model and training on solver solutions brings no gain. We will release the benchmark and code at https://github.com/suat-llm/GridProbe.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34152) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34152)

## 120. Non-Linear Motion from Two Frames: Cubic Hermite Trajectories for Video Frame Interpolation

- **作者**：Md Rifat Ur Rahman, Md Abtahi Majeed Chowdhury, Md Sakib Hossain Shovon et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Two-frame video frame interpolation commonly resamples endpoint optical flow by the query time, which selects a constant-velocity straight-line path even though the same endpoint displacement is compatible with many intermediate trajectories. We present HermiteVFI, which replaces independent query-time motion prediction with one explicit continuous trajectory per pixel. The measured endpoint displacement fixes the two trajectory positions, while two latent endpoint derivatives learned from the frame pair control the interior path. A cubic Hermite curve is the minimum-degree polynomial that can independently satisfy these two position and two derivative boundary quantities. Its residual form contains linear motion exactly, structurally preserves the measured displacement endpoints for any network output, and has coefficients that are predicted once per frame pair and evaluated in closed form at arbitrary t. We further show that one interior query time gives a rank-deficient constraint on the two free trajectory coefficients, whereas any two distinct interior times are full rank at the representation level, motivating multi-time supervision. Appearance-derived directional cues help infer the latent derivatives that endpoint displacement alone cannot identify. On X4K1000FPS, SNU-FILM, and Vimeo90K-Septuplet, the largest gains occur for difficult non-uniform motion and high interpolation ratios. The amortization applies to trajectory-coefficient prediction; downstream reversal, refinement, and synthesis remain query dependent.

## 121. Spe3DUp: Faithful Trajectory Distillation for Fast Image-to-3D Generation

- **作者**：Liangchao Xu, Jiaye Fu, Youtian Lin et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Recent 3D generative models can produce high-quality meshes with complex structures and fine geometric details, with broad applications in games, film, and digital design. However, high-quality generation typically relies on long multi-step sampling processes that repeatedly evaluate large networks, leading to long inference times. At the same time, reference-driven tasks such as game-asset reconstruction and product digitization demand close correspondence to the input image: generated geometry must faithfully reconstruct the specific reference object. Acceleration must therefore reduce sampling computation while preserving the model's ability to reconstruct that object. To enable fast and faithful 3D generation, we introduce Spe3DUp, a coarse-to-fine framework based on Faithful Trajectory Distillation (FTD). FTD directly supervises few-step students with the teacher's input-conditioned denoising trajectories to preserve its generation behavior. We apply FTD separately to coarse geometry generation and geometric refinement, compressing both sampling processes while maintaining correspondence to the reference input. We further accelerate the complete mesh-generation pipeline with efficient geometry decoding and parallel remeshing. Experiments show that Spe3DUp maintains geometric quality and input correspondence close to those of the teacher, accelerates the coarse and refinement DiTs by 10.0x and 19.9x, respectively, and achieves a 7.7x end-to-end speedup over the original teacher pipeline.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34141) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34141)

## 122. Benefit of Structured Representations: When Equivariance Helps in Image Classification

- **作者**：Florian A. Hölzl, Daniel Rueckert, Georgios Kaissis
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：NeurIPS 2026 Workshop NeurReps Proceedings

Equivariant neural networks promise improved representation learning through structural guarantees. However, mainstream vision encoders rarely incorporate equivariance for most geometric symmetries. Images are a particularly interesting modality with many such symmetries and extensive datasets from which to learn this structure. In this work, we investigate when vision encoders benefit from geometric inductive biases. We study the discrete rotation group of quarter-turns (C4) which acts losslessly without interpolation or boundary artifacts. Combined with image classification as a controlled invariant objective, this allows for a principled empirical investigation into the role of structural guarantees. We introduce ConvNeXt-C4 as a rotation equivariant ConvNeXt with dimension-equivalent yet orientation-structured representations. Training from scratch, our experiments show, that the C4 variant outperforms its base model across all tasks while operating with 6x fewer parameters and 1.6x fewer FLOPs. Its performance advantage increases log-linearly with decreasing training set size, with pre-training providing a similar improvement across both model variants. Analyzing the classifier head reveals that orientation information from the representations' structure is actively used, improving performance by 4.90% across all datasets. Our results highlight the sample-efficiency benefits of equivariance and the value of retained orientation information, motivating further investigation beyond image classification. Code is available at https://github.com/hlzl/equivariant-convnext.

## 123. An Image Detectability Field as a Viewpoint Utility for 2D-3D Localization in Sparse Maps

- **作者**：Han Sun, Jun Li, Christy Jie Liang
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

In 2D-3D localization, a camera estimates its pose by matching an image to a 3D map. A camera held in a hand or mounted on a robot can often choose its viewpoint before it takes the image. In this work, we ask whether an image detectability field is a useful viewpoint utility when the planner reads only the geometry of the map. The field can be queried at any 3D point near a surface. From the local geometry around the point, it predicts how likely a 2D image detector is to fire at the projection of the point. We use the trained field as it is, without fine-tuning it for view selection. A fixed SuperPoint and EPnP-RANSAC localizer then estimates the pose from the chosen view. We answer in two settings, each with a test registered before the runs. On synthetic ShapeNet objects with sparse point maps, summing the field over the visible landmarks beats counting them by +2.0 points of localization success rate (object-clustered 95% interval [+0.7, +3.2]). It also beats summing Harris-3D ranks by +2.2 [+0.8, +3.5]. On real indoor scenes from SceneFun3D, our adaptation of the learned view scorer LWL falls below counting on the test rooms. We therefore add the field to a learned per-landmark planner. On the test rooms with sparse maps whose landmarks the field also selects, the planner with the field beats the same planner without it by +1.8 points. It beats the planner with Harris-3D or curvature in its place by +1.0 and +0.8 points. All three room-clustered intervals lie above zero. In both settings, the field helps in sparse maps and is on par with Harris-3D in dense maps.

## 124. A Calibration-Referenced Protocol for Measuring Decision Stability under Reference Bank Compression

- **作者**：Yiwen Chen, Shengming Yu
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：54 pages, including supplementary material (Tables S1-S11 and Figures S1-S4). Interval summaries and probe-sensitivity CSV files are included as ancillary material

Reference banks used for industrial image inspection can be compressed to fit a deployment budget, but the resulting anomaly decisions depend on both the compressed scores and the operating threshold. We present a four-step protocol that compares compression methods at matched deployment sizes, establishes a reference distribution from 200 recalibrations, evaluates disagreement against two calibration benchmarks, and measures changes by distance from the full-bank threshold. With WideResNet-50, budget-matched product quantization (PQ) at the 10% budget has the lowest decision disagreement among five matched methods, including int8 scalar quantization, on VisA and MVTec AD at both operating levels. Int8 scalar quantization gives lower disagreement than PQ at the other four budgets. On MVTec AD at the lower operating level, PQ meets the calibration median in 34/39 cells and the upper quantile in 39/39 cells. Category-equally weighted image-level AUROC loss at this budget ranges from 0.0003 to 0.0037 across the evaluated configurations. On VisA at the 1% budget and a 1% operating level, disagreement reaches 28.12% (95% CI 23.25--33.58%) among near-threshold anomalies and 1.30% (0.79--2.14%) across the normal holdout. The 10% budget-matched PQ setting retains the lowest disagreement among four compared methods with ResNet-18, with clean reference banks, and when thresholds are estimated from normal-directory probes. The protocol connects deployment size to a calibration-referenced decision measure and identifies the evaluation populations most informative for inspection.

## 125. FoundSurface: Feedforward Ray-Based 3D Scene Surface Reconstruction from Unposed Images

- **作者**：Shenxing Wei, Jinxi Li, Zihui Zhang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.CG, cs.GR, cs.RO
- **备注**：NeurIPS 2026. Project page: https://vlar-group.github.io/FoundSurface.html

Accurate 3D surface reconstruction from unposed images remains a fundamental challenge in computer vision. Recent feed-forward pointmap methods eliminate the need for camera poses but inherently produce discrete, sparse representations. In this paper, we present a generalizable pipeline that directly recovers continuous, high-fidelity 3D surfaces from unposed images by seamlessly integrating pointmap- and ray-based representations. Our approach first extracts explicit geometric priors using a pre-trained pointmap model. A query-view-centric selection module then identifies geometrically relevant reference views via a robust surfel voting mechanism. Next, a cross-attention regressor bridges target query rays with reference features to estimate a continuous coarse surface. Lastly, a raylet-based module aggregates local 3D features to carve out high-frequency details and analytical normals. Trained as a single foundation model on 9 diverse datasets, our method demonstrates exceptional zero-shot generalization on 6 unseen datasets, clearly surpassing state-of-the-art baselines in geometric accuracy and detail preservation.

## 126. Out of Sight, Not Out of Clues: Can Video LLMs Guess from Partial Evidence?

- **作者**：Luoxi Zhang, Jiawei Liu, Yanlin Luo et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.CL
- **备注**：27 pages, 21 figures, 23 tables. Code: https://github.com/suat-llm/ClueProbe

In real-world applications like egocentric recording, visual accessibility, and surveillance review, visual evidence for Video Large Language Models (Video LLMs) is often only partially captured. A reliable model should match its response to the evidence: answering when sufficient, guessing from visible clues when partial, and abstaining when absent. Yet existing benchmarks enforce a binary answerable-or-unanswerable split, leaving partial evidence unmeasured and obscuring whether failures arise from missing evidence or misjudging sufficiency. We introduce ClueProbe, a diagnostic benchmark to (1) separate perceiving evidence from judging sufficiency, and (2) validate our diagnosis via targeted fine-tuning. ClueProbe comprises 1,721 human-annotated questions across 598 natural videos, labeled Answerable, Guessable, or Unanswerable, alongside controlled perturbations altering wording or visual input. Evaluating 10 open-source and proprietary Video LLMs reveals that failures stem from judgment rather than perception: models perceive the evidence yet lack graded sufficiency awareness, answering or refusing outright while rarely guessing from partial clues. Targeted fine-tuning shows that this reflects missing supervision rather than insufficient capacity: supervising the guess level lifts appropriate guessing from 1.6% to 40.4% without extra false refusals, whereas conventional refusal tuning rejects 92% of guessable questions. Data and code will be released at https://github.com/suat-llm/ClueProbe.

## 127. A Simple Yet Effective Agent for Open-World Aerial Object Search

- **作者**：Jitai Hao, Liangxing Yang, Zhenyang Li et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.RO
- **备注**：20 pages, 10 figures

Open-world aerial object search methods often rely on persistent spatial maps, learned world models, or task-specific policy training. To examine how far a minimal, training-free design can go, we introduce SimpleUAV, an agent built around a frozen, off-the-shelf vision-language model (VLM). SimpleUAV separates current-view target assessment from history-conditioned action selection, combining fresh candidate evidence with lightweight search context. Local geometry converts visual selections into bounded straight-line movements, depth-based checks halt blocked segments, and measured execution feedback guides subsequent decisions. This closed loop sustains search without persistent spatial maps or task-specific training. SimpleUAV achieves 41.61% Success Rate (SR) on UAV-ON and 11.98% on AerialDojo-200K, compared with 23.80% and 3.20% for the strongest prior baselines, respectively. With limited benchmark-specific adaptation, the same core agent transfers to aerial Vision-and-Language Navigation (VLN) on TravelUAV, improving Full SR by at least 19.66% across all three splits under L1 assistance. These results establish a strong baseline for evaluating when additional architectural complexity benefits aerial search.

## 128. Detectable by Design: Learning a Continuous 3D Saliency Field from 2D Detector Responses

- **作者**：Han Sun, Jun Li, Christy Jie Liang
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

The 3D points kept in a map decide whether a camera can be localized against it, and this choice matters more as the map becomes sparser. Existing 3D keypoint detectors usually define saliency by semantics, repeatability, reconstruction or descriptor matchability. None of these definitions asks whether a point will actually be detected in an image, and as a result most of the points they select are not detected again in images. In this paper, we define 3D saliency as image detectability: the response of a 2D interest-point detector at a surface point, aggregated over all views in which the point is visible. We propose DetField, a continuous field over 3D space that takes a point cloud as input and predicts detectability at any location around it. The supervision is the response of a 2D detector, back-projected from images onto the point cloud. Multi-scale curvature invariants are passed to the decoder at every query point, bypassing the voxel pooling of the encoder. In a localization benchmark that isolates the choice of 3D points, DetField outperforms Harris-3D and five learned 3D keypoint methods on objects and on real indoor scenes. On unseen rooms, it improves localization success by 7.4 points over Harris-3D, the strongest baseline. On objects, it also leads under a 2D detector not used in training. On sparse maps, it stays ahead of Harris-3D on both objects and real scenes.

## 129. What Makes a Gavagai? Learning Visual Concepts from One Positive and Few Negatives

- **作者**：Katsuya Ogata, Kazuya Nishimura, Yuta Nakashima
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Humans can recognize novel concepts from only a few observations by identifying defining visual attributes and eliminating alternative interpretations. However, a single observation is consistent with multiple possible concepts, and accurate visual perception alone is insufficient to determine the intended interpretation. Negative evidence can reduce this ambiguity by eliminating competing hypotheses. Inspired by Quine's gavagai problem, we introduce the Gavagai Benchmark to evaluate whether vision-language models can identify the attributes defining a novel concept from positive and negative examples. The benchmark consists of synthetic images depicting novel combinations of visual attributes and real images of newly described species. Using these datasets, we examine how models use negative examples to identify novel concepts and analyze the respective roles of visual perception and reasoning. Several models struggle even when correct attributes are provided, while others show large gaps when ground-truth attributes are replaced with model predictions. These diagnostic comparisons highlight challenges in both attribute perception and inference from limited evidence.

## 130. When Position Favors the Wrong Part: Evaluating Left-Right Discrimination in Semantic Correspondence

- **作者**：Pingan Wang
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：9 figures & 11 tables

Overall part-discrimination accuracy can conceal failures when image position favors the wrong bilateral part. We examine whether dense descriptors distinguish the correct part under this conflict and whether their discrimination scores help select matching coordinates. We evaluate position agreement and conflict within the same target images and annotated part pairs, using a bounding-box-side score to define the conditions. We compare the original descriptors with their even and odd components under horizontal reflection, examine feature upsampling with the backbone and adapters fixed, and test candidate-selection rules based on odd scores. On SPair-71k, odd discrimination accuracy falls from 86.66% under agreement to 19.63% under conflict for frozen DINOv2, compared with 95.27% to 86.16% for MARCO. With MARCO's input geometry fixed, odd accuracy in box-side conflict was 34.12% on the native grid and 88.84% after learned upsampling and pooling back to that grid. Odd-score rules improve on the original hard-peak matchers overall for DINOv2 and DINOv3 and on conflict queries for MARCO. With MARCO's soft decoder fixed, reranking the cosine peaks changes mean per-pair PCK by +0.47 pp. For supervised GeoAware-SC, using the odd score to select the localization window center yields only 15 net additional correct matches, with 330 corrections and 315 introduced errors. Evaluating discrimination under position conflict reveals failures hidden by aggregate accuracy. Reporting corrections and introduced errors separately also shows whether a discriminative score produces useful matching decisions.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34100) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34100)

## 131. FoMNet: Adaptive Kernels Meet Contextual Matching for Cross-Modal Homography Estimation

- **作者**：Zhuoran Yang, Zhenyu Yi, Runmin Zhang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Cross-modal homography estimation requires reliable correspondences despite appearance differences between sensing modalities. Multi-scale refinement progressively reduces the search range, but its accuracy remains limited by the ability of matching features to distinguish nearby candidates. The vision foundation model (VFM) provides complementary representations, yet integrating these features through fixed interpolation leaves their spatial aggregation independent of image content and the correspondence objective. We propose the Foundation Model-Enhanced Matching Network (FoMNet), which combines learned spatial adaptation of VFM features with contextual correspondence estimation. Patch Feature Upsampling (PFU) progressively predicts location-specific kernels to aggregate neighboring features on upsampled grids, preparing VFM representations for fusion with CNN features at different matching scales. Contextual Correlation Refinement (CCR) then jointly processes source descriptors and local correlations with spatial context to refine candidate scores before each homography update. To reduce upsampling overhead, we develop FoMNet-D, which replaces PFU with bicubic interpolation and uses multi-scale feature-relation distillation to recover accuracy. Experiments on five cross-modal datasets show that FoMNet achieves the lowest mean average corner error (MACE) among the evaluated methods, reducing MACE by 10.6%-72.7% relative to MCNet. FoMNet-D retains competitive accuracy while reducing inference latency by 34.0% relative to FoMNet.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34096) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34096)

## 132. Exploring the Design Factors of Pixel-space Diffusion

- **作者**：Mang Ning, Le Zhang, Albert Ali Salah et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.LG
- **备注**：11 pages

In this paper, we introduce EiT by exploring the key design factors of pixel-space diffusion from three perspectives: model architecture, representation, and frequency domain. Inspired by Denoising Autoencoders (DAE) which perform denoising through a bottleneck, we construct a U-shaped pixel diffusion transformer that performs explicit compression along the channel dimension. In terms of representation, we find that both pixel and latent diffusion transformers exhibit a three-stage representation dynamics across layers and timesteps: denoising/local feature expansion, basis lift-up, and posterior projection. Moreover, earlier denoising and a longer basis lift-up stage are associated with better generation, which EiT naturally achieves through its channel bottleneck and increased network depth. From the frequency perspective, we establish the equivalence between pixel-space and frequency-space diffusion when keeping the network operating in pixel space. On ImageNet $256\times256$ and $512\times512$, EiT consistently outperforms JiT (Just image Transformers) in generation quality. Specifically, our EiT achieves better FID than JiT: 1.61 vs. 1.85 at $256\times256$, and 1.73 vs. 1.99 at $512\times512$. Remarkably, EiT can be seamlessly applied to video pixel diffusion by simply extending the patch size to temporal dimension and outperforms video latent diffusion in FVD evaluated on Kinetics-600 (resolution 256), under comparable total network parameters. Code is available at https://github.com/forever208/EiT

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34093) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34093)

## 133. From Diagnosing Alignment to Joint Supervision in Multimodal Draft Training

- **作者**：Haozhe Du, Runze Liu, Jiaming Fan et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：18 pages, 6 figures, 10 tables

Multimodal drafts learn from multimodal (MM) and text-only (TXT) supervision, yet their effects on alignment and how to combine them remain unclear. We diagnose draft-target alignment through paired predictions with and without visual input, decomposing distributional error into text-conditioned and visual-response components. Drafts with similar text-conditioned alignment can still differ markedly in visual-response risk and acceptance. Controlled continuation training shows that high-risk groups outperform random and low-risk groups, and visual-response risk guides MM training more effectively than aggregate risk. Building on these diagnostics, we propose MNRS-Joint, which configures joint supervision from a given draft's component-risk distributions, using modality-specific mean risks to determine the MM:TXT mixture. Across three warm-ups, its distinct mixtures fall in high-performing regions of the ratio sweeps. On LLaVA-1.5-7B, MNRS-Joint improves mean accepted length from 3.35 to 3.53 and mean speedup from $2.04\times$ to $2.16\times$ over composition-matched random training under greedy tree decoding. The gains also hold under stochastic chain decoding.
