## 134. TaCo: Rethinking Correlation for Visual Tracking on Edge Devices

- **作者**：Qingmao Wei, Fagui Liu, Quan Tang
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Lightweight trackers have replaced cross-correlation with attention, and on low-power edge hardware they pay for it, since memory-bound softmax, normalization and reshaping operators of attention cost far more time than their arithmetic suggests. Benchmarking efficient trackers on an edge Neural Processing Unit (NPU) and an Arm CPU under one numerically validated protocol, we find that correlation trackers are the fast ones, but they lag in accuracy unless they adopt a ViT backbone, which brings attention back. We revisit what correlation lacks. Template--search interaction serves two roles: conditioning the search features on the target and matching them against it spatially. Attention does both, whereas correlation only matches, and does so on features extracted without knowledge of the target. We present TaCo (Target-aligned Correlation), which supplies the missing conditioning without attention by letting the template enter the per-frame network only as operands computed once per video. A descriptor of the target scales and shifts the search features channel by channel inside the backbone, and kernels aligned with the target box, each with a weight predicted from the template, correlate with them in one matrix product. With the backbone fixed, our correlation remains essential while incurring negligible NPU overhead, offering a better speed-accuracy trade-off than the attention-based interactions we tested. TaCo-T achieves 62.3% LaSOT AUC at 184 FPS on the Rockchip RV1126B NPU and 96 FPS on a Raspberry Pi 5 CPU, 3.0x and 3.5x the NPU speed of HiT-Tiny and AsymTrack-T at higher accuracy. TaCo-B reaches 65.6% at 50 FPS on the NPU, more than twice the speed of HiT-Base, AsymTrack-B and LightFC-vit, again at higher accuracy. Code and models are available at https://github.com/TsingWei/TaCo-Tracker.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34084) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34084)

## 135. MulCLIP: A Multi-level Alignment Framework for Enhancing Fine-grained Long-context CLIP

- **作者**：Chau Truong, Hieu Ta Quang, Zhenzhen Liu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.IR, cs.CV

Vision-language models such as CLIP excel at image-text alignment but struggle with long, detailed descriptions due to training on short captions. Recent methods address this limitation using region proposals to align visual regions with sentences, but at substantial deployment cost. We present MulCLIP, an end-to-end multi-level alignment framework that directly exploits natural long-text structure without region proposals. Instead of simply stacking objectives, MulCLIP aligns different textual granularities through compatible training signals: global contrastive alignment for long captions and short summaries, Word-Patch Reconstruction over locally calibrated features for within-sample word-patch semantics, and Subcaption-Aggregated Patch alignment for context-rich subcaption grounding. Experiments on benchmarks spanning varying caption lengths show consistent gains, and ablations confirm that the proposed objectives are complementary and jointly improve the overall framework. Code will be released at https://github.com/truongchau2602/MulCLIP.

## 136. WorldBridge: Bridging Scattered Views for Coherent 3D World Generation

- **作者**：Weilong Yan, Jingyu Hu, Hongli Xu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI

Generating coherent and explorable 3D worlds from scattered images with little or no visual overlap is a fundamental and long-standing challenge in computer vision, which existing geometry-aware methods fail to address. Meanwhile, existing image- and video-based methods may generate unseen regions but produce inconsistent geometry across views. To bridge this gap, we present WorldBridge, a framework for coherent and controllable 3D world generation under scattered images with little or no visual overlap. WorldBridge encodes scattered references into a global scene context to guide geometry and appearance generation across views. Our temporal and spatial reference attention mechanism further enhances the fidelity of temporal references along the target trajectory and supports flexible spatial references outside it. A plug-and-play latent-reference consistency strategy improves reference fidelity through reprojection-guided test-time optimization. We also introduce the WorldBridge benchmark suite to evaluate 3D world generation from scattered, low-overlap references, which is rarely explored by current benchmarks. Both qualitative and quantitative results demonstrate that our method enables controllable and coherent 3D world generation, surpassing the existing state-of-the-art solutions. Our project page is available at https://davidyan2001.github.io/WorldBridge/.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34079) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34079)

## 137. Projector Subspace Optimization for Multimodal Continual Instruction Tuning

- **作者**：Hao Yu, Quan Cheng, Lijun Zhang
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：29 pages, 12 figures, including supplementary material

Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) achieve strong performance through instruction tuning, yet adapting them to sequential tasks requires Multimodal Continual Instruction Tuning (MCIT). Existing replay-free methods expand prompts or routed LoRA experts across multi-layer decoders, incurring heavy parameter and routing overhead while shifting internal feature distributions. Instead, we identify the final linear layer of the vision-language projector as a high-capacity, coordinate-stationary bridge, where sequential updates easily induce interface drift by displacing historical visual tokens. While low-rank subspace adaptation offers a compact remedy, selecting directions solely from historical statistics or task gradients sacrifices plasticity or stability, and unconstrained multi-step updates still accumulate large drift. To resolve both issues, we propose Projector Refinement through Optimized Update Directions (PROUD), a continual subspace optimization framework for the terminal projector layer. At task onset, PROUD combines retained input statistics with initial gradients to analytically extract a metric-orthonormal subspace maximizing first-order task gain under a drift budget. During training, it regularizes updates within this fixed subspace via an exact Frobenius penalty before losslessly merging them with zero inference or routing overhead. Experiments on UCIT and CoIN across MLLM backbones show that PROUD outperforms recent replay-free baselines in continual accuracy while curbing forgetting with a fraction of trainable parameters.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34078) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34078)

## 138. Hijacking 3D Generation: Targeted Adversarial Attacks against Image-to-3D Models via Prefix Trajectory Alignment

- **作者**：Yilong Yang, Jiale Teng, Yule Liu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI
- **备注**：18 pages, 10 figures

Online services that deploy Image-to-3D models enable users to generate high-quality 3D assets from a single image. However, the generated 3D geometry can violate content policies and cause broader security risks. To prevent such content, these services typically use policy-restricted inspectors to examine uploaded images before generation. Since the inspector examines only the visible content of the input, a visually benign image can still induce the generation of policy-restricted 3D geometry. However, turning this vulnerability into an effective attack is non-trivial in the black-box setting: global backpropagation through the long DiT generation trajectory is difficult to fit, while different image preprocessing pipelines across services can substantially reduce transferability. In this paper, we present PTA3D, a targeted adversarial attack against Image-to-3D models that addresses these challenges through Prefix Trajectory Alignment (PTA) and Preprocessing Aware Optimization (PAO). PTA progressively aligns the prefix DiT outputs of the adversarial trajectory with those of the target trajectory, where the core geometric structure is formed, allowing the final geometry to be controlled without optimizing through the complete generation trajectory. PAO exposes adversarial optimization to diverse image transformations rather than a single preprocessing pipeline, reducing sensitivity to the mismatch between surrogate and target services. We establish a prefix-to-output bound and a preprocessing-aware transfer bound to theoretically characterize the effectiveness of the two designs. Experiments on three open-source Image-to-3D models and three online services show that PTA3D achieves 41.5%-88.5% ASR on online services and 90.8%-94.6% ASR in white box settings, substantially outperforming existing attacks.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34075) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34075)

## 139. When to Respond, Where to Ground: Streaming 3D Affordance Grounding

- **作者**：Wenting Luan, Weilong Yan, Siqi Lu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：19 pages, 6 figures

Given a continuous RGB-D stream and a language request, streaming 3D affordance grounding requires an agent to decide when to respond and where to locate the requested functional part using only observations available so far. Existing 3D affordance grounding methods typically assume complete scene observations, while streaming vision-language models lack fine-grained 3D functional grounding. Our goal is to jointly determine response timing and functional-part localization during continuous scene observation. We propose StreamAff, an evidence-guided response strategy that makes both decisions from the grounding model's own visual evidence. The model scores the visual patches of the frames it reads: their combined support, weighed against a wait option, decides when to respond, and the highest-scoring patch selects the frame to ground in. A conditional look-back policy lets the model answer from an earlier observation at the moment of the request or wait for new evidence, and zoom-and-refine grounding segments the part named in the answer on the 3D points of the selected frame. We further introduce StreamFun3D, a benchmark derived from real-world 3D scenes that evaluates response timing and grounding across four evidence settings: Current, History, Future, and Unobserved, together with StreamIoU, a metric that credits a mask only when the response is timely and grounded in a valid source frame. We adapt offline 3D affordance grounding methods and build external-decider baselines that pair vision-language models with our mask decoder. StreamAff achieves 30.14% StreamIoU on 15-second streams, a 40.6% relative improvement over the strongest of these baselines.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34067) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34067)

## 140. SPARC: Geometry-Guided Attention Pruning for Long-Horizon Camera-Controlled Video Generation

- **作者**：Zesong Yang, Weikai Chen, Liyuan Cui et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

We present SPARC, a geometry-guided attention pruning framework that turns the native self-attention of pretrained video diffusion transformers into a unified mechanism for camera control and persistent scene memory. Our key insight is that attention connectivity can simultaneously serve as geometric conditioning for camera control and spatial addressing for historical memory retrieval. Motivated by the asymmetric contextual requirements of reconstructing observed content and synthesizing novel regions, SPARC restricts observed tokens to geometrically corresponding historical tokens and local spatiotemporal context, while preserving dense attention for unobserved regions. Correspondence-aware attention biasing and order-invariant historical positioning further enable stable retrieval across long temporal gaps. Without introducing additional learnable control or memory modules, SPARC achieves precise camera adherence and faithful scene revisitation while substantially reducing attention computation. Extensive experiments demonstrate the effectiveness of aggressive attention pruning in improving camera adherence, revisit consistency, and visual fidelity, with an inference speedup of over 50x when combined with four-step distillation. Project page is available at https://zju3dv.github.io/SPARC.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34064) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34064)

## 141. Surf-Style: Surface-Based Scene Stylization

- **作者**：Wanrong Hong, Ancheng Lin, Mukesh Prasad et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：10 pages, 6 figures

Consistent and controllable 3D stylization is critical for visual computing applications such as immersive visualization, augmented and virtual reality content creation, and artistic scene rendering. While 2D image stylization has advanced significantly, extending these methods to 3D remains challenging due to the need for cross-view consistency, where stylized renderings must align across all viewpoints. Applying 2D stylization independently to each rendered view often results in inconsistencies across perspectives. Existing methods attempt to improve consistency by leveraging implicit radiance field representations, which model scene appearance as a function of spatial locations. However, these methods struggle with ambiguity in updating colors, as the indirect dependence between pixel appearance and learnable scene parameters makes it difficult to enforce consistent stylization during optimization. This work presents Surface-based Scene Stylization (Surf-Style), which reconstructs object surfaces in a 3D scene and uses these surfaces to achieve geometric consistency and localized stylization. Surf-Style defines a learnable appearance model over 3D space, which is modified to replicate the visual style of a reference image. Compared to state-of-the-art methods, our method achieves superior consistency and offers greater flexibility in controllable stylization, including the ability to target specific surface regions and combine different styles.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34062) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34062)

## 142. Understanding Diffusion Acceleration: Methods, Trade-Offs, and Open Questions

- **作者**：Xiaofeng Mao, Fu-yun Wang, Le Zhuo et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Diffusion and flow-based generative models support high-quality image, audio, and video synthesis. Sampling can require dozens to hundreds of denoiser evaluations, with each pass becoming more expensive as resolution, video length, or model size increases. This cost affects latency and energy use in interactive and edge applications. We examine four approaches to accelerating inference: distillation, consistency training, sampling methods, and denoiser design. We express training objectives, transition maps, and numerical updates in a common notation to compare changes to the learning target, the sampling trajectory, and the work performed within each denoiser pass. Published image and video benchmarks illustrate the quality--cost trade-offs of one- and few-step generation, the interaction between solvers and timestep schedules, the effect of feature caching, and the difference between first-frame latency and sustained streaming throughput. We close with open problems in visual-native architectures and post-compression distillation, solver-aware few-step training, and semantic trajectory alignment with factorized distribution matching.

## 143. End-to-End Image-Goal Navigation for Agile Quadrotors via Recurrent State Distillation

- **作者**：Simone Nascivera, Ismail Geles, Davide Scaramuzza
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.CV

Image-goal navigation asks a robot to reach the viewpoint of a target photograph without a map or goal pose. In three dimensions, this requires not only recognizing the goal but also estimating 3D displacement and generating continuous control actions to approach and settle at the target view. This is difficult because the same visual difference between current and goal image can require very different actions depending on the robot's velocity, yet monocular images alone reveal neither metric scale nor body motion. As a result, existing image-goal policies are limited to planar action spaces and rely on a state estimator for flight control. Policies that map directly from images to low-level control commands have been demonstrated for agile flight, but not toward goal images in unseen environments. We present the first image-goal navigation policy that directly commands thrust and angular rates for agile quadrotor flight in unseen 3D environments, without relying on state estimation (e.g., SLAM, VO, or SfM) or a separate velocity controller. We train a recurrent vision-based policy through a four-stage pipeline that combines geometric and motion supervision with privileged-teacher distillation and end-to-end reinforcement learning fine-tuning. On 1000 held-out flights in unseen environments, the policy achieves an 82.3% success rate with a mean terminal position error of 0.10m across successful flights. Deployed zero-shot on a real quadrotor, the policy reaches an 84% success rate over 25 trials in an unseen indoor environment.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34046) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34046)

## 144. Counter-DECEIVR: Counterfactual Explanations for Deepfake Detectors using Detector-Guided Rectified Flow Inversion

- **作者**：Mariano V. Ntrougkas, Vasileios Mezaris, Ioannis Patras
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI
- **备注**：Under review

Deep-learning-generated media are increasingly passed off as real, driving an arms race between image generators and deepfake detectors. Trust in the latter is limited by their often black-box nature; and, while region-based explanations can sometimes be extracted, these become less useful as generation quality improves. Counterfactual examples, which show the minimal change that flips a detector's decision, reveal what evidence drives a verdict, helping analysts decide whether to trust it. This work introduces Counter-DECEIVR (DEtector-guided reCtifiEd flow InVeRsion for counterfactuals), a novel training-free framework that generates counterfactual examples. Counter-DECEIVR steers latents in the velocity field of a rectified flow model using gradients from off-the-shelf deepfake detectors, without training a generator or adapting the detector; unlike prior methods, it requires no per-instance or per-detector fitting and no diffusion training on the target data. It also leverages the detector's explanation maps to concentrate editing to salient regions, turning low-level feature importance into localised counterfactual edits. A single parameter controls the strength of the edits. Across two deepfake detectors and two widely used forensics datasets, Counter-DECEIVR outperforms prior counterfactual generation methods, including training-based ones. Code will be made available upon acceptance.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34032) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34032)

## 145. ContextHands: Completing Hand Motion with Learned Context and Joint-Specific Body Kinematics

- **作者**：Taewoo Kim, Seunghun Koh, Hyosup Shin et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：11 pages, 3 figures. Main paper only

Detailed hand motion contributes to the realism and expressiveness of generated human motion. Completing these details from body motion and language requires connecting the context of an action to the articulation of the hands. We introduce ContextHands, which completes hand motion from an observed body sequence and text descriptions assigned to hands and time intervals. Our approach learns probabilistic hand features from the descriptions and body motion, using auxiliary hand prediction to connect these features to hand articulation. A body encoder preserves joint and body-part features across multiple temporal scales, allowing each hand joint to query these features for joint-specific body information. The diffusion generator combines this joint-specific body information with the learned hand context. Both encoders and the generator are trained jointly, so the final generation objective also shapes the context features. Experiments on the OakInk2 dataset show that ContextHands generates more accurate hand motion and achieves lower Fréchet Hand Distance than existing hand-motion and general human-motion generation methods adapted to the task. In qualitative examples of grasping and tool use, ContextHands more closely reproduces the detailed hand poses in the reference motion. We will release our code and pretrained models to support further research.

## 146. Incorporating the Knowledge Distillation to Improve the EfficientNet Transfer Learning Capability

- **作者**：Tjeng Wawan Cenggoro
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI

Due to the Deep Learning requirement for a large training dataset, Transfer Learning has become a central method in the field of Computer Vision, which heavily used Deep Learning. Since the adoption of transfer learning in the field, the performance of the models in computer vision is significantly upgraded. The common transfer learning practice in computer vision research is to use state-of-the-art architectures in the ImageNet dataset as the backbone network. The best performing architecture in ImageNet is currently held by EfficientNet, which is a set of architectures that were progressively grown from a baseline architecture. By observing the EfficientNet development approach, it is natural to hypothesize that the smaller EfficientNet architecture is capable to contain the knowledge of the larger architecture. This hypothesis comes from the fact that the larger EfficientNet architecture was grown from the smaller architecture. Therefore, in this study, we experimented if it is beneficial to transfer the knowledge from the larger architecture to the smaller architecture of the EfficientNet. To achieve this goal, we proposed a transfer learning method that uses Knowledge Distillation as the knowledge transfer mechanism from the larger to smaller architecture. We found that the proposed method is able to upgrade the performance of each of the EfficientNet architecture. Several smaller architectures are even able to outperform the larger architecture that was trained using the standard transfer learning method.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34025) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34025)

## 147. MoSk: A Unified Motion-Skeleton Foundation Model for Human Motion Understanding and Generation

- **作者**：Yun Chen, Munchurl Kim, Jeonghyeok Do
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Please visit our project page at https://kaist-viclab.github.io/MoSk_site

Foundation models (FMs) have advanced visual understanding and generation by providing semantically rich representations. However, this role of FMs remains underexplored in human motion. Large motion datasets offer diverse movements at scale, but often lack action labels for systematically evaluating learned representations on understanding tasks. Skeleton datasets enable such evaluation through established recognition protocols, yet are typically smaller and focus on predefined actions captured in controlled environments. To combine these strengths, we present MoSk, to the best of our knowledge, the first Motion-Skeleton foundation model to integrate motion and skeleton datasets. MoSk learns shared representations through self-supervised masked motion modeling. We incorporate joint-name embeddings to provide a common anatomical reference across various skeleton formats. A cross-attention decoder reconstructs masked motion from the encoder's features, with joint-name embeddings specifying the target joints. Experiments demonstrate strong action-recognition performance across diverse settings, including linear evaluation and fine-tuning. MoSk also substantially improves motion generation across multiple backbones through representation alignment, reducing FID on MotionMillion by up to 9.75 for unconditional generation and 3.83 for text-to-motion generation.

## 148. Rethinking Unsupervised Cross-Modal Homography Estimation:Spatially Aware Representations and Geometric Consistency Learning

- **作者**：Zhuoran Yang, Junchen Yu, Runmin Zhang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Unsupervised cross-modal homography estimation is challenging because appearance differences obscure shared geometric structures. Separating modality transfer from homography estimation helps reduce these differences, but accurate alignment still requires effective geometric representations and consistency constraints on the two subtasks. We propose GeoCo (Geometry-Aware and Consistency-Constrained Homography Estimation), a framework that addresses these two requirements without cross-modal transformation labels. Its homography estimator, RVHomo, adapts hierarchical features from a frozen vision foundation model (VFM) and fuses them with local CNN features to capture structural context and spatial detail. Two geometric consistency constraints encourage modality transfer to preserve geometry and homography predictions to remain consistent under synthetic geometric transformations. Through two-stage progressive distillation, we further obtain GeoCo-D, which uses the lightweight RVHomo-D estimator. An intermediate model first learns to perform cross-modal matching without modality transfer, then transfers prediction and feature knowledge to the student, eliminating online VFM extraction. Experiments on GoogleMap, OPT-SAR, FSD, and VIS-IR show that GeoCo reduces mean average corner error by 17.4-46.7% relative to the best-performing baseline on each dataset. GeoCo-D also outperforms all compared baselines while using 98.5% fewer parameters and 70.4% fewer FLOPs than the complete GeoCo teacher.

## 149. Manga Page Parser: Comprehensive Visual Parsing through Local Story Understanding

- **作者**：Yingxuan Li, Jiafeng Mao, Arata Furukawa et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Understanding manga requires connecting who appears and who speaks with the events, character states, and relationships that unfold across panels. We study manga visual parsing through local story understanding: analyzing each double-page spread independently to capture and organize its story information in a reusable structured representation. We propose MangaParser, an agentic reading framework that jointly performs character clustering, speaker diarization, and speaker-to-character binding within a shared multimodal conversation, without additional training or fine-tuning. The agent uses structured story memory to guide character and speaker identification, inspecting relevant image regions when uncertain and refining its predictions and story records together as evidence accumulates. Quantitative evaluations show that our parser substantially outperforms manga-specific models across multiple backbones. Parsing examples illustrate how a unified representation links the character and speaker predictions with narrative content and supporting evidence. We further test reusability through story question answering using outputs generated before the questions are provided. The results confirm output reusability, offering a foundation for manga translation, content retrieval, and question answering.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34006) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34006)

## 150. ScaleCurve: From Constant Scale to Consistent Motion for World-Space Human Mesh Recovery

- **作者**：Sanghyeon Lee, Jong Taek Lee
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

World-space human mesh recovery can lose spatial consistency even when camera-space body estimates are accurate. A single sequence-wide scale resolves global scale ambiguity but cannot correct local scale distortion along a reconstructed camera path. We present ScaleCurve, a framework that estimates time-varying metric corrections from human-ground geometry. We construct scale observations by comparing predicted foot geometry with locally fitted ground planes. Joint temporal optimization downweights inconsistent observations and couples neighboring scale estimates, allowing calibration to adapt along the camera path without freely following measurement noise. We scale camera translation increments and integrate them into a calibrated trajectory. With this camera fixed, we refine the lifted body motion using 2D image evidence and pretrained 3D dynamics, while restricting global trajectory rescaling. ScaleCurve achieves state-of-the-art performance on most evaluated metrics on both EMDB-2 and SLOPER4D. Consistent gains across three HMR backbones demonstrate its compatibility with diverse body estimates without retraining. Code will be made publicly available.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34005) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34005)

## 151. QK-Bench: Benchmarking Quantitative Kinematic Understanding for Dynamic Spatial Intelligence

- **作者**：Lennox Wei, Jiaqi Yao, Shixin Sun et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.CV

Spatial intelligence concerns both scene structure and its temporal evolution. Yet a semantically correct description can conceal a quantitative error: recognizing that objects are approaching does not determine their closing speed or whether they will meet. Such errors are critical in collision avoidance, trajectory tracking, and reactive grasping, where actions depend on motion magnitude, timing, and relative geometry. We study quantitative kinematic understanding in multimodal large language models (MLLMs): resolving motion magnitude, rate, reference-frame dependence, and future spatial evolution from visual evidence. We introduce the Quantitative Kinematics Benchmark (QK-Bench), comprising 2,341 multiple-choice QA pairs from 379 videos and 17 tasks spanning Object-Centric Kinematics, Camera Ego-Motion Kinematics, and Multi-Object Kinematic Relations. Hidden geometric and temporal signals support trajectory construction and filtering, while fixed kinematic rules map physical quantities to verifiable answers, including categorical labels. Trajectory and question validation ensure target reliability and sufficient visual support in the sampled frames. All models receive a unified monocular RGB input. Evaluations of proprietary, open-source, and spatial MLLMs reveal substantial limitations: the strongest model achieves only 41.2\% overall accuracy, and spatial MLLMs show no consistent advantage. These findings indicate that current advances in spatial intelligence do not reliably transfer to quantitative kinematic understanding.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33991) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33991)

## 152. geo-eval-harness: An Evaluation Harness for Geospatial Foundation Models

- **作者**：Nils Lehmann, Caleb Robinson, Isaac Corley et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Geospatial foundation model (GFM) research papers are plagued by inconsistent evaluation settings (e.g., different pre-processing and probes), making it difficult to separate performance from evaluation choices. We propose geo-eval-harness}, an open-source framework for controlled model comparisons. We use this harness to evaluate 100 GFMs and general vision models on 6 classification and 14 segmentation datasets, using multiple probes on frozen or low-rank-adapted encoders. We show that under controlled evaluation, general vision backbones are competitive on RGB classification and high-resolution segmentation, but GFMs shine on multispectral tasks. When we compare any two of the top-ten classification models on a single dataset, their 95% confidence intervals overlap 73% of the time. We show that bigger models are not always better: inference FLOPs correlate weakly with segmentation performance. Moreover, translating inference compute into large-area mapping workloads shows how small accuracy differences have outsized deployment cost differences: with frozen encoders on SpaceNet2, a 0.9-point mIoU difference corresponds to 5.9x the compute and days of additional projected GPU time. Our findings stress the importance of comparing models under explicit and fixed settings, and reporting uncertainty and deployment compute alongside accuracy. Our geo-eval-harness makes controlled experiments easy, fast, and fully reproducible. We release the code, configurations, and results at https://github.com/torchgeo/geo-eval-harness.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33990) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33990)

## 153. FasterGRN: Reducing the Refinement Redundancy for Accelerating Generative Refinement Networks

- **作者**：Ziqian Zhang, Pengcheng Wang, Yulong Chen et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Departing from the paradigm of diffusion and autoregressive models, Generative Refinement Networks (GRN) enables high-quality image generation through iterative refinements. However, this repeated processing over the entire visual representations results in substantial computation overhead. Furthermore, the binary representations and global refinement mechanism in GRN disable the direct application of acceleration strategies developed for other generative models. To accelerate GRN model, we first observe several key properties by analyzing the entire generative process. That includes the stage characteristic, dual branch similarity and more importantly, the $\textbf{refinement redundancy}$. Motivated by the observations, we present $\textbf{FasterGRN}$, a training-free acceleration method for the Generative Refinement Network. $\textbf{FasterGRN}$ first divides the GRN into the semantic establishment stage and detail refinement stage according to its semantic establishment. For alleviating the refinement redundancy mainly resulting from the detail refinement stage, we propose Adaptive Retention Expansion ($\textbf{ARE}$) and Selective Token Freezing ($\textbf{STF}$) to shrink refinement steps and reduce token computations, respectively. In the experiments on GenEval and DPG-Bench benchmarks, our method $\textbf{FasterGRN}$ achieves a $2.03\times$ sampling speedup while largely preserving image generation performance. Our evaluation on COCO datasets additionally demonstrates that $\textbf{FasterGRN}$ even outperforms the original GRN in image fidelity.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33982) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33982)

## 154. Embodied-VGGT: Preserving Reliable Local Geometry for Long-Sequence Reconstruction

- **作者**：Yiheng Han, Ziang Chen, Zhuyong Fu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI
- **备注**：18 pages,8+3 ref+7 sup,6 figures

Long sequence reconstruction must correct accumulated drift while preserving locally consistent geometry. In robotics and autonomous driving, embodied acquisition provides recurring platform- environment geometry and repeated observations of scene structure and platform motion. We present Embodied-VGGT, which exploits these cues to establish reliable local relationships and selectively preserve them during global refinement. Memory-Augmented Grounded Chunks (MAGC) builds on Scal3R's global context memory and uses relative scale references derived from predicted ground geometry to regularize test-time memory updates, encouraging cross-chunk consistency while limiting changes to reliable local geometry. Superpoint Alignment (SA) and Cross-Loop Optimization (CL) jointly address the robust establishment and preservation of geometric relationships. SA balances ground and non-ground evidence across spatial regions to estimate inter-chunk translation. CL uses geometric and motion consistency across interleaved windows to select coherent chunk groups, each receiving a shared Sim(3) correction that preserves its internal relative transformations during global optimization. Experiments on five public driving benchmarks demonstrate consistent and substantial improvements in long sequence reconstruction. On KITTI Odometry, Embodied-VGGT achieves a mean absolute trajectory error of 7.30 m and a Chamfer distance of 2.67 m, reducing these errors by 46.8% and 23.3% compared with the state-of-the-art baseline under same evaluation protocol. To facilitate reproducibility, we released our code: https://github.com/Ang-Sheep/Embodied-VGGT.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33964) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33964)

## 155. Targeted Adaptation of World-Action Models via Interaction and Motion Heads

- **作者**：Yuze Zhu, Jacky Li, Yusen Luo et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.CV
- **备注**：Project page: https://headlight-wam.github.io/

World-Action Models (WAMs) are a promising new paradigm of robot manipulation policies that jointly generate future video and actions. Successful manipulation requires both identifying where to interact and modeling how the robot and objects move. Yet standard adaptation methods, such as full fine-tuning and LoRA, do not explicitly select parameters aligned with these manipulation-specific capabilities. Motivated by this challenge, we discover two functionally specialized families of attention heads: Next-Interaction Heads, which localize upcoming grasp-and-release targets, and Motion Heads, which track movement across frames. Furthermore, we introduce HeadLight, a parameter-efficient refinement method that jointly adapts Next-Interaction Heads and Motion Heads. On RoboTwin tasks unseen during fine-tuning, HeadLight achieves 62.28% success, compared with 57.89% for full fine-tuning and 58.66% for LoRA, while updating only 1.1% of parameters. Inference-time ablations further validate the functional importance of these heads: ablating Motion Heads reduces manipulation success from 57.89% to 0.94%, while ablating Next-Interaction Heads lowers it to 40.61%, both substantially more than ablating equally many random heads. Finally, we show that our approach is effective across multiple WAM architectures and real-robot manipulation settings.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33963) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33963)

## 156. Where Does Free-Text 3D Grounding Fail? An Error Analysis of Report-Sentence Segmentation in Chest CT

- **作者**：Abdullah Arif Sheikh, Kamalaker Dadi, Raju S. Bapi
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Free-text 3D grounding, segmenting in a CT volume the region described by one sentence of the radiology report, could link reports to images at scale, but it is unclear where current models fail. We analyse the publicly released VoxTell model on all 381 findings of the 200-scan ReXGroundingCT validation set, where it grounds 53.5% of findings. Its failures are rarely about which lesion or side is meant. No miss lies on the side opposite to the one named, and findings are almost never confused with one another. Instead, predictions are too large, land on a neighbouring lesion on the correct side, or are missing for small findings, and diffuse findings are rarely grounded. Removing location words halves performance, to the level of label-only prompting, and anatomical rules derived from the sentence never improve the full-sentence output. The same pattern holds on the hidden 300-scan test set. Further progress therefore depends on extent estimation and small-lesion sensitivity rather than on anatomical post-processing.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33958) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33958)

## 157. RefMix: Expanding Endpoint Proposals for Diversity-Preserving Reward Fine-Tuning

- **作者**：Jaemin Kim, Christian Simon, Dongseok Shim et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.LG
- **备注**：Project page: https://kjm981995.github.io/RefMix

Online forward matching aligns flow-based generative models by repeatedly generating, rewarding, and fitting model-produced endpoints. However, this behavior creates an on-policy feedback loop: as high-reward modes become increasingly favored, alternative modes become less likely to appear in subsequent training pools, progressively reducing visual diversity. We introduce RefMix, a reference-assisted endpoint proposal strategy that addresses this limitation by injecting alternative candidates into the training pool while retaining the original task reward and forward matching objective. In particular, RefMix uses a reference model during early high-noise steps to expose alternative visual modes, followed by the current reward-tuned model for endpoint completion. Across state-of-the-art online forward matching methods, RefMix consistently improves visual diversity while preserving task reward performance. Controlled inference experiments further demonstrate that training-time endpoint exposure is critical to improve reward--diversity trade-offs, beyond the impact of reference-assisted inference. By rethinking how training endpoints are proposed, RefMix achieves up to 44% higher diversity over forward matching baselines at comparable reward levels.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33957) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33957)

## 158. Where to Approximate: Understanding and Accelerating Looped Multi-View Reconstruction

- **作者**：Siyuan Zhang, Yuhan Chen, Yangyang Ning et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Looped multi-view reconstruction refines scene features by repeatedly applying a shared transformer block. Making these updates cheaper requires knowing how an approximation at one step affects the rest of the refinement. We study this with paired interventions in Déjà View and stacked reconstruction transformers, separating the error an approximation injects, how that error propagates, and how it affects the output. In Déjà View, approximation errors are attenuated more strongly than matched random perturbations under a scale-adjusted state-error measure, and a local Jacobian decomposition separates the contribution of channel gating from the response to spatial error structure. Spatial approximations made early benefit from subsequent refinement, whereas cross-step reuse depends on which quantity is cached and how reuse steps are spaced. At equal final norm, approximation errors change the predicted rotations 1.55 times as much as random errors. These findings guide our proposed method, which reduces spatial resolution and pools keys and values early in refinement, then interleaves attention-output reuse with full attention, and is adapted through lightweight self-distillation. Across five reconstruction benchmarks, the balanced configuration runs $1.63\times$ faster than the official 16-step Déjà View model with a 1.86-point reduction in average pose AUC@$3^\circ$. At matched latency, it exceeds the interpolated curve of a trained baseline using full refinement blocks by 4.73 points in AUC@$3^\circ$ and improves the corresponding geometry metrics.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33948) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33948)

## 159. A Computational Model of Continuous Recognition Memory for Natural Images

- **作者**：Sheng Zhao, Yijin Wang, Bei Xiao et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：18 pages

Recognizing an image we have seen before is a basic function of human visual memory. Understanding human continuous recognition memory (CRM) can help machine vision systems predict which images people are likely to remember and generate more memorable images. It can also inform the diagnosis of memory impairments and the development of interventions to improve memory. This paper proposes the first computational model for CRM. The model captures three key components of continuous recognition memory (CRM): image encoding, memory decay, and decision making. It encodes each image as a distinct random variable in a perceptual space. As the memory of an image fades, the distribution of its random variable drifts within that space. To decide whether the current image has been seen before, the observer retrieves samples from the distributions associated with previously encoded images and compares them with the current image. Our model predicts human observers' trial-by-trial responses in CRM tasks within the variability observed across human participants. Our model is parameter efficient, requiring only four shared parameters and two parameters per observer. It can accurately predict the memorability of an image no worse than prior methods that require millions of parameters.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33947) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33947)

## 160. TAT-Det: A Dataset for Testicular Appendage Torsion Detection and Segmentation in Ultrasound Images

- **作者**：Ying Jiang, Juntao Jiang, Linxuan Fan et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：17 pages, 9 figures

Background: Testicular appendage torsion (TAT) is the leading cause of acute scrotal pain in prepubertal children. Yet, its diagnosis remains challenging due to nonspecific clinical manifestations and the absence of standardized datasets for pediatric acute scrotal emergencies. Results: We present TAT-Det, an expert-annotated ultrasound dataset specifically designed for TAT. TAT-Det comprises 2,600 ultrasound images from 1,203 patients collected at the Children's Hospital, Zhejiang University School of Medicine. Each TAT-positive image is accompanied by lesion-level bounding boxes for detection and pixel-level segmentation masks for fine-grained delineation. Annotations were produced through a multi-stage workflow involving independent annotation by board-certified pediatric sonographers, consensus review, and refinement. Baseline benchmarks for object detection and semantic segmentation using representative deep learning models are further provided, with YOLO11m achieving the best detection performance and RWKV-UNet the best segmentation performance. Conclusions: TAT-Det addresses the lack of publicly available resources for pediatric emergency ultrasound and establishes a foundation for developing and evaluating artificial intelligence methods for automated TAT analysis.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33940) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33940)

## 161. BayesPath: Reliability-Aware Bayesian Reasoning for Whole-Slide Pathology Agents

- **作者**：Bryan Wong, Xun Xu, Jongwoo Kim et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Diagnosing whole-slide images (WSIs) requires hypothesis-driven reasoning: pathologists form a differential diagnosis, seek discriminative evidence, weigh its reliability, and stop once it suffices. However, existing training-free WSI agents lack an explicit framework for such reasoning. They typically use a large language model (LLM) to aggregate the region descriptions of a multimodal LLM (MLLM), without a belief over the candidates or regard for the descriptions' reliability. Consequently, a repeated model error counts as independent evidence for an incorrect diagnosis. We introduce BayesPath, a training-free WSI agent that casts the pathologist's Scan-Look-Weigh-Decide process as reliability-aware Bayesian reasoning. An explicit belief over the candidates is updated with each observation through a reliability-aware likelihood and guides where to look next and when to stop. This likelihood corrects for the models' answer bias, discounts correlated observations, and captures diagnostic confusions estimated without labels. The updates form an interpretable evidence trail of how each observation shapes the belief. On five MultiPathQA tasks spanning slide classification and slide VQA, BayesPath achieves the highest average accuracy among training-free agents, 58.6% against 34.1% for the next best, with large gains on slide classification. On identical observations, Bayesian updating outperforms LLM aggregation on average, even with a larger LLM. BayesPath's belief is also better calibrated than naive Bayes, so uncertain cases can be deferred to pathologists. Code is available at https://github.com/bryanwong17/BayesPath

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33935) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33935)

## 162. Towards 360$^\circ$ Embodied UAV Tracking Without Turning

- **作者**：Bingyu Li, Wu Du, Zhiyuan Zhao et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI

Conventional embodied UAV tracking couples target observability with heading control because targets may leave the field of view of forward-facing cameras. We investigate a complementary paradigm that uses full-spherical perception to follow designated targets without active heading adjustment. To support this setting, we introduce 360UAV-ETrack, a simulation benchmark comprising 2,652 sequences, over 1.73 million synchronized observations, eight tracking tasks, and 150 held-out closed-loop scenarios. We propose 360UAV-TVLA, a panoramic vision--language--action (VLA) framework integrating persistent visual referring (PVR), compressed temporal memory (CTM), and geometry-aligned trajectory supervision (GTS) for instance identification, motion reasoning, and three-dimensional control. Experiments across two VLA backbones show consistent gains over standard fine-tuning, with normalized average tracking frames increasing from 22.56\% to 35.28\% for $π_0$ and from 25.68\% to 33.64\% for $π_{0.5}$. Our benchmark further reveals a substantial gap between offline imitation accuracy and closed-loop tracking reliability, establishing a challenging testbed for future research https://libingyu01.github.io/360UAV-ETrack_360UAV-TVLA/

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33934) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33934)

## 163. iMFPO: Improving Mean Flow Policy Optimization with Drift-Aware Velocity Alignment for Embodied Control

- **作者**：Lisiyu Pan, Ting Huang, Zeyu Zhang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.CV

Flow-based generative policies offer expressive action modeling for embodied control, yet achieving efficient one-step generation under reward-driven policy optimization remains challenging. In particular, evolving action distributions during online reinforcement learning may introduce inconsistencies between instantaneous-velocity learning and interval-averaged action generation. To address this challenge, we propose iMFPO, a one-step generative policy optimization framework with decoupled velocity learning and drift-aware velocity alignment. A shared interval-conditioned actor supports instantaneous-velocity supervision and interval-averaged action generation. JVP-corrected velocity regression is jointly optimized with reward-driven policy objectives, with action-level guidance from a frozen PPO teacher during training. At inference, iMFPO directly generates actions through interval-averaged velocity prediction, achieving one-step control without iterative flow integration. Across Go2, G1, H1, and Spot, zero-latent evaluation yields mean returns of 35.69, 34.44, 416.23, and 29.86. On Go2, action-generation latency drops from 15.57 ms to 0.41 ms relative to 64-step RFPO. Code: https://github.com/AIGeeksGroup/iMFPO. Website: https://aigeeksgroup.github.io/iMFPO.

## 164. UPP-Gauss: Ultrasound Propagation Physics-informed Gaussian Splatting for B-mode Novel-View Synthesis

- **作者**：Guil Jung, Seok-Hwan Oh, Myeong-Gee Kim et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：5 pages, 3 figures

Accurate novel-view synthesis of B-mode ultrasound images requires modeling acoustic propagation through tissue and the resulting appearance variations across probe poses. We propose Ultrasound Propagation Physics-informed Gaussian Splatting (UPP-Gauss), which integrates these acoustic interactions into an explicit Gaussian representation. UPP-Gauss constructs acoustic maps by sampling three 3D Gaussian fields using the probe pose and acoustic beam propagation direction, then combines these maps according to ultrasound propagation to synthesize B-mode images. Across three acoustically diverse datasets, UPP-Gauss achieves the best reconstruction quality with efficient rendering, improving PSNR over the best baseline on each dataset by 1.43 dB on average. These results demonstrate accurate and efficient B-mode novel-view synthesis through explicit Gaussian modeling of ultrasound propagation.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33920) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33920)

## 165. Event-WAM: Learning to Act with Event-Derived Futures

- **作者**：Jongseong Bae, Hyunwoo Kim, Yeongin Lee et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

World Action Models (WAMs) leverage future visual modeling for robotic action learning, with recent studies exploring alternative or complementary objectives beyond conventional RGB prediction. We introduce Event-WAM, which incorporates event-derived visual changes as auxiliary future supervision alongside RGB. Event-WAM constructs temporally structured event representations from RGB demonstrations and encodes them using a frozen pretrained RGB video VAE, without training an event-specific VAE. Joint RGB--Event latent flow matching provides appearance- and change-oriented supervision without requiring event observations or explicit future generation at inference. Across simulated benchmarks, Event-WAM maintains comparable performance under standard conditions, while robustness gains under perturbations are benchmark-dependent, including improvements on LIBERO-Plus. Real-world evaluations further demonstrate improved manipulation success under both standard and unseen perturbation conditions. Further analyses show that policy improvements are not consistently accompanied by higher future prediction fidelity, suggesting the potential of change-oriented supervision for action learning.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33917) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33917)

## 166. SEEK: Self-Evolution through Execution Knowledge for Agentic Robot Manipulation

- **作者**：Zeyang Guan, Qihao Zhao, Rui Gong et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.CV
- **备注**：17 pages

General-purpose multimodal foundation models enable robots to adapt during deployment from observations and execution feedback, without task-specific demonstrations or training. However, the robot retains what it learns only in the model's working context. Once the task ends, it loses this execution knowledge, such as how to carry a bowl between two grippers. On the next attempt at the task, the robot may take just as long and repeat the same mistakes. To avoid this repetition, robots must retain execution knowledge across attempts. Yet replaying past actions is insufficient, because their effects depend on the current conditions. Procedures that determine actions from the current scene address this, but when they meet conditions that earlier attempts did not cover, revising them requires the measured effects of earlier actions. We therefore propose SEEK, an agentic framework that retains both, enabling robots to improve across attempts while keeping the underlying model fixed. To adapt retained knowledge to the current scene, SEEK adopts a Self-Evolving Programmatic Policy (SEPP) that encodes manipulation as executable procedures and determines each action from current observations. As the robot executes these procedures, SEEK records the measured effects of its actions, including those from failed attempts, in a Persistent Action-Effect Bank (PAEB). Where a retained procedure applies, it acts without a model request; where it meets a condition it does not cover, this evidence guides its revision. On RoboDojo, knowledge retained over ten attempts raises SEEK's average task score from 27.0 to 54.4 and its success rate from 19.7% to 48.0% across all 42 tasks. On 34 of these tasks with GPT-6.2 Astra, SEEK reaches 80.1 after five attempts, against 49.6 for the same model acting directly, and uses 77.6% fewer model requests. https://zeyangguan.github.io/SEEK-Agentic-Robot-Manipulation/

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33915) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33915)

## 167. Tortho-Gaussian++: Suppressing Geometric Drift and Floaters for Robust Gravity-Aligned TDOM Generation in Large-Scale Aerial Scenes

- **作者**：Xiang Wang, Xin Wang, Zongqian Zhan
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) has demonstrated remarkable performance in aerial scene reconstruction, motivating its recent extension to true digital orthophoto maps (TDOMs) generation. We propose Tortho-Gaussian++, a novel 3DGS training framework that suppresses geometric drift and floaters and automatically prunes unreliable peripheral regions, coupled with a robust gravity-aligned TDOM rendering solution. First, we explore geometric support via viewing-ray-based geometric observability analysis, quantitatively characterizing the reliability for each Gaussian and subsequently pruning peripheral regions with particularly weak geometric support. Second, the optimizer state initialization is refined for newly created Gaussians during densification, intrinsically suppressing drift and floaters without external priors. Lastly, a robust gravity direction estimation solution is designed, combining image-derived gravity cues, camera-distribution priors and nadir views, to achieve a gravity-aligned orthogonal projection reference for TDOM rendering. Extensive experiments on multiple large-scale UAV (Unmanned Aerial Vehicle) datasets demonstrate that our Tortho-Gaussian++ outperforms vanilla 3DGS by 0.5-3.5 dB in average PSNR and yields comparable or superior performance to prior-guided 3DGS methods. Notably, it reduces the training time, VRAM and Gaussian number by 45.5%, 47.2%, and 58.6%, respectively. Qualitative evaluations confirm that our method effectively suppresses drift and floaters, while robust gravity direction estimation reduces tilt-induced facade artifacts in TDOMs.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33905) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33905)

## 168. RelPRM: Grounding Visual Process Reward Models in Relational Evidence

- **作者**：Zhaoxing Zhang, Ziqiu Gui, Cheng Chen
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Visual process reward models (PRMs) can detect calculation errors yet assign high scores to steps that misread visual relations. Supervision based on subsequent answer success does not directly establish whether a step's relational claim holds. To strengthen visual-relation verification, we first build an automated pipeline that programmatically verifies each claim and identifies the required objects, relations, and image regions. It produces RelProcessTrain and RelProcessBench (RPB), which tests whether scores respond appropriately to relational evidence through controlled image edits and step rephrasings. Building on this pipeline, we further introduce RelPRM, a visual-relation-oriented method for process reward modeling with two training mechanisms. Relation-evidence self-distillation constructs views preserving necessary objects, relations, and dependencies, reducing the burden of finding decisive evidence in complex scenes and transferring reliable evidence-assisted judgments to full images. Relation-evidence-driven consistency training encourages appropriate score changes when relation edits reverse a step's truth, stability under irrelevant edits, and similar score changes across paraphrases. At inference, RelPRM directly scores the current step using only the original image and reasoning context. RelPRM achieves 66.2% on VisualProcessBench (VPB) and 68.4% on RPB, gains of 4.2 and 9.9 percentage points over VisualPRM-8B. These results show that training for visual relations improves both relational verification and step assessment across broader multimodal reasoning tasks.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33903) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33903)

## 169. TWIST: Teacher-Weighted Initial-Noise Spherical Transform for Quality-Preserving Diversity in Distilled Diffusion Models

- **作者**：Shayan Baghayi-Nejad, Jeongsol Kim, Jong Chul Ye
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Timestep-distilled diffusion models generate high-quality images in one or a few steps, but often suffer from reduced mode coverage and limited diversity. Recent methods improve diversity by optimizing the initial noise at inference time, requiring costly backpropagation through the denoiser. Moreover, their diversity objectives typically repel generated samples without accounting for how the corresponding teacher model distributes its outputs. We introduce Teacher-Weighted Initial-noise Spherical Transform (TWIST), a lightweight network that transforms the low-frequency components of initial noise to improve diversity without inference-time optimization. TWIST uses a teacher-guided determinantal point process (DPP) objective to align the feature-space diversity of student samples with that of the teacher. By amortizing noise optimization into a single network, TWIST eliminates inference-time backpropagation while preserving the efficiency of few-step generation. On SDXL-Turbo, TWIST matches the diversity of MoNO at less than 25% of its inference cost, while achieving higher recall and better PickScore, ImageReward, and CLIPScore under matched compute budgets. On SDXL-Lightning, it achieves comparable recall at lower cost without the ImageReward degradation observed with MoNO.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33895) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33895)

## 170. SIA: Selective Image Analogy

- **作者**：Amirhossein Alimohammadi, Kian Izadpanah, Bardiya Kariminia et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.GR
- **备注**：SIGGRAPH Asia 2026 (Conference Papers). 28 pages including supplementary material. Project page: https://selective-image-analogy.github.io/ Code: https://github.com/alimohammadiamirhossein/selective-image-analogy Dataset: https://huggingface.co/datasets/selective-image-analogy/SIA-Multi-Edit-Analogy

Image analogy transfers a demonstrated transformation from a before/after pair (A, A') to a new query image B. Existing methods assume all changes in (A, A') should be fully transferred, limiting flexibility when users want only a subset of the demonstrated edits. We introduce Selective Image Analogy (SIA), a new setting where a single demonstration pair encodes multiple edits, and the user selects at inference time which edits to apply to B. To support this, we curate a multi-edit analogy dataset with full-edit and partial-edit targets. We then train a model that first learns to transfer the complete demonstrated transformation, and then gains selective control through a mask-aligned suppression mechanism: a token-gated LoRA that restricts adapter updates to suppressed edit tokens during denoising, paired with a concept suppression vector that weakens the selected tokens in the text embeddings before denoising. Together, these components allow SIA to suppress unwanted edits that remain visible in A', while preserving the user-selected ones, all without requiring a new demonstration pair for each edit combination. We perform extensive quantitative and qualitative evaluations along with user studies to demonstrate the efficacy of our approach.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33894) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33894)

## 171. DeltaRAE: Derivative Tokenization for Long Video Generation

- **作者**：Mingzhuo Yu, Shengcao Cao, Chengzhi Mao et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Latent video generators are trained on clips of a few seconds, and much of this limit lies in their tokenizers, which spend as many tokens on a static frame as on a moving one and need future frames that do not exist during generation. We introduce DeltaRAE, a causal representation autoencoder for video. Its derivative tokenization encodes periodic anchor frames in full and all remaining frames with a few tokens each, and its causal encoder and decoder read a frozen pretrained image encoder. On UCF-101 and Kinetics-600, a standard diffusion transformer trained on DeltaRAE latents is competitive with state-of-the-art video tokenizers. DeltaRAE halves the tokenizer GFLOPs on 16-frame clips relative to keeping the same number of patch tokens for every frame, and it represents a minute of video with 18.8x fewer tokens. The tokenizer trains end to end on one-minute videos, and in video prediction a generator on its latents produces a full minute of video. The same generator collapses within 15 seconds in most videos when the tokenizer has the same token budget but no pretrained encoder.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33886) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33886)

## 172. LayoutRecall: Preserving Appearance in Multi-Round 3D Layout-Controlled Image Editing

- **作者**：Yankuan Chi, Xin Lin, Baihan Yang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

3D layout-controlled image editing gives users explicit control over object placement and camera viewpoint. In a sequence of edits, however, the latest image can lose the appearance of an object when it is occluded or leaves view. The target layout specifies where the object should reappear, while its appearance must be recovered from earlier observations. We introduce LayoutRecall, a multi-round editor that stores and reuses appearance in the local coordinate systems of 3D boxes. Box-Surface Latent Memory retains image latents on box faces and retrieves them along rays in the target view. Geometry-Aligned Memory Conditioning supplies the retrieved tokens to a FLUX.2 editor using target-view positions, shared box-local embeddings, and object-wise attention. A correspondence loss encourages the editor to attend to matching observations during denoising. We also introduce LayoutLoop, a multi-round 3D layout-controlled image editing benchmark with calibrated layouts and designed revisits. We conduct extensive experiments on LayoutLoop and real-scene data to verify the ability of our method. The project page is at https://ykchizs.github.io/layoutrecall.github.io/.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33885) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33885)

## 173. Off the Driven Path: Transferable Road Understanding in Driving VLAs

- **作者**：Lena Wild, Christian Helten, Katie Z Luo et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

End-to-end driving policies increasingly operate without explicit HD maps at inference, raising the question of what road knowledge they actually internalize. To probe this knowledge through model outputs, we introduce Road-KSU, a framework that automatically converts HD maps and actor annotations into a road-centric scene graph. From this, we derive road-focused question answering and reasoning targets, complemented by map-based trajectory compliance metrics. Pairing Road-KSU with geographically disjoint splits yields a $2 \times 2$ evaluation crossing output modality (language vs. action) with place familiarity (seen vs. unseen roads). On NAVSIM, 95% of test samples lie within 10m of a training sample, and scores of 13 published planners on turns rise steeply with the number of training drives through the test location, pointing to memorized road geometry. Retraining on geographically disjoint splits and probing with Road-KSU confirm that action-space performance degrades most on unfamiliar turns, with failures dominated by geometric compliance. Language accuracy, however, drops on road-centric VQA on straight and curved roads alike, and remains stable on dynamic scene content. Scaling the training data further reveals a double dissociation: more drives through the same places improve driving on unseen roads but leave road question answering unchanged, whereas spreading a fixed data budget over more places increases road question answering with little change in driving performance. These findings show that improved driving scores need not imply broader road understanding on unseen roads, and identify geographic diversity as key to transferable road knowledge.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33878) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33878)

## 174. X-Matte: VAE-Free Pixel-Space Flow Matching for Single-Step Image Matting

- **作者**：Zanyi Wang, Jiaang Li, Dengyang Jiang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Pre-trained generative models offer rich visual priors for image matting, but their adaptation often relies on variational autoencoders (VAEs), whose spatial compression can degrade fine alpha details and introduce additional inference overhead. To address these limitations, we propose X-Matte, a VAE-free pixel-space flow matching framework for single-step image matting. By eliminating latent compression, X-Matte formulates the RGB-to-alpha transition as a continuous flow directly in pixel space. We adopt trajectory-wide x-prediction with transition-aware alpha supervision, enabling the model to learn from intermediate states while directly predicting the target alpha matte in a single forward pass. This design avoids both VAE encoding and decoding and iterative sampling. Extensive experiments demonstrate performance competitive with state-of-the-art trimap-free matting methods on P3M benchmarks, together with promising zero-shot generalization to RWP-636 and AIM-500. Moreover, X-Matte achieves up to a 9.4x inference speedup over a constructed single-step latent counterpart at high resolution, demonstrating the effectiveness of VAE-free pixel-space modeling for accurate and efficient image matting.

## 175. Concept Generalization in Visual Brain Decoding via Encoder-Generated fMRI

- **作者**：Peter Yongho Kim, Juhyeon Park, Dongsheng Li et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：33 pages, 7 figures, 20 tables

Visual brain decoders are typically trained and evaluated on stimuli drawn from similar semantic distributions, leaving their ability to reconstruct unseen concepts poorly understood. We study this concept generalization problem by withholding semantic concepts from a target subject's training data. We find that three recent fMRI-to-image decoders degrade substantially for the held-out concepts, even when the concept remains available for multi-subject models. In contrast, brain encoders that map a pretrained visual representation directly to voxels are markedly more robust to the same semantic holdout. Motivated by this asymmetry, we use such an encoder to generate synthetic fMRI responses for new images and add the resulting image-fMRI pairs to decoder training. Across three decoders, four subjects, and twelve semantic concepts, synthetic data recovers a substantial fraction of the performance lost on unseen concepts without requiring additional, time-consuming fMRI acquisition. We further show that the same approach improves general decoding performance across a wide range of data budgets. Analyses of encoder training conditions, encoder designs, and synthetic-data controls show that the gains depend on both encoder generalization and the semantic content of the synthetic supervision, rather than merely increasing the amount of training data. Together, our results show that encoder-generated fMRI can transfer semantic generalization to decoders.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33870) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33870)

## 176. MAFNet-UR$^3$: Mask-Aware Fusion Network for Unified Raindrop and Reflection Removal

- **作者**：Yeongjin Jeong, Hyunjin Cho, Minsang Kim et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Accepted at the ECCV 2026 LoViF Workshop. Received the Outstanding Challenge Paper Runner-Up Award

Raindrops and reflections frequently co-occur on windshields and jointly obscure scene content, making their unified removal, referred to as UR$^3$, a highly ill-posed problem. Existing approaches are mainly based on diffusion models and thus can produce visually plausible restorations. However, they may generate scene-inconsistent structures in severely corrupted regions. To address this limitation, we propose MAFNet-UR$^3$, which adopts a three-stage framework to exploit multiple observations for scene-consistent unified raindrop and reflection removal. Specifically, Stage I suppresses raindrops while preserving the reflection layer, and Stage II performs prior-guided reflection removal followed by image-space refinement. Stage III estimates degradation masks from intermediate restoration results to provide degradation cues and performs mask-aware multi-scale fusion across observations to preserve scene structure and fine details. As a result, our proposed MAFNet-UR$^3$-L achieves state-of-the-art performance on the RDRF-testing benchmark, outperforming DiffUR$^3$ by 2.33 dB in PSNR and 2.7634 in the challenge score. Our method ranked third in the LoViF 2026 UR$^3$ Challenge, achieving a final score of 41.1416.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33866) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33866)

## 177. Measuring the Value of One Shared Image at Robot Handoffs in Vision-and-Language Navigation

- **作者**：Yuze Lu
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.CV

When the lead robot cannot continue, what should it send the teammate that must finish the route? We replay a frozen receiver at each contact under alternative messages. Across 3,920 autonomous departures in 106 environments, adding the view before the lead's last action block to its executed actions raised success by 7.51 percentage points (pp) per RxR handoff and 8.54 pp per SAGE handoff. Per departure the gains were 2.23 and 1.44 pp, respectively. The lead continuing in simulation defines the empirical full-history ceiling (Solo). At contact, the lead had accumulated a median of 41 frames. Sending one frame when available recovered 24% of the full-history advantage over actions alone in SAGE and 30% in RxR. Over an estimated progress cue in SAGE, the image rescued 100 routes and reversed 54 successes. Near-goal receivers finished outside less often (63.1% to 51.7%, post hoc). In secondary comparisons the earlier view outperformed the contact view by 6-7 pp per handoff. Exact-route specificity and image gains at controlled starts with mostly unknown progress or outside the goal region remained unresolved. Gains were detectable from about 7 kB per handoff. Random transmission matched within size bins nearly equaled the learned sender's team success. At a required handoff, a view of the approach to the last maneuver helps the receiver finish.

## 178. OmniMeld: Enhancing Audio-Visual Understanding via Multi-Teacher On-Policy Distillation

- **作者**：Daili Hua, Yifan Dai, Bohan Zeng et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Audio-visual understanding requires perceptual grounding, temporal understanding, and semantic inference. Multi-teacher distillation can consolidate these capabilities, but direct probability matching does not explicitly capture teacher prediction changes across audio-visual conditions. We introduce OmniMeld, a multi-teacher on-policy distillation method that integrates domain expertise through modality-response-guided distribution matching. We train four teachers covering grounding and correspondence, temporal dynamics and event structure, causal and inferential reasoning, and contextual and holistic understanding. Each sample is routed to its corresponding teacher for supervision conditioned on student-generated answer prefixes. We derive transport costs from teacher prediction changes over shared candidate tokens across audio-visual conditions, using optimal transport to match student predictions to those of the teacher under complete audio-visual input. We also construct OmniMeld-20K for domain-specialized teacher training. The dataset spans four capability domains, with responses structured into evidence, reasoning, and answer sections and verified through consistency checks and media-based review. Experiments on audio-visual and general video understanding benchmarks demonstrate competitive teacher performance among similarly sized open-source models. The student improves over its base model across all evaluated benchmarks and surpasses the teachers' base model on several benchmarks.

## 179. Structured Noise Diffusion for Echocardiography Haze Removal

- **作者**：Zike Luo, Hao Li, Mengxiao Wang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Echocardiographic images are frequently degraded by spatially structured haze artifacts that obscure anatomical boundaries and hinder clinical analysis. Conventional diffusion models typically employ pixel-independent Gaussian noise, limiting their ability to represent localized ultrasound haze. In this study, we propose a structure-informed diffusion framework that explicitly models ultrasound haze as an accumulation of localized Gaussian components constrained by the ultrasound fan geometry. The forward process generates spatially correlated corruption by sampling component locations and scales, providing a formulation that better reflects the spatial characteristics of real echo artifacts. During reverse restoration, a dual-head network predicts an anchor probability map and Gaussian scale parameters to progressively remove structured artifacts while reducing the center bias associated with direct coordinate regression. We evaluated the framework on the public DehazingEcho2025 benchmark and two private clinical datasets, without retraining or fine-tuning on either private cohort. Our method achieved the highest mean generalized contrast-to-noise ratio across all three datasets, reaching $0.715 \pm 0.090$ on the public benchmark while preserving tissue edges.
