## 225. Interpreting Speckle Effects Through Neuron Interventions

- **作者**：Shubham K. Singh, Hare Krishna
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：eess.IV, cs.CV, cs.LG
- **备注**：17 Pages 7 figures

Speckle, the multiplicative noise inherent to synthetic aperture radar (SAR) imaging, reduces the performance of pretrained SAR models. We use interpretability methods to examine its effects inside pretrained vision models. We identify neurons that respond selectively to speckle and test whether correcting their activations improves reconstruction. We study SARMAE, an Earth observation model pretrained on a million-scale SAR dataset. Synthetic scenes with known reflectivity allow us to measure recovery precisely. The model contains 38 speckle-selective neurons that track speckle's value distribution. Restoring these neurons to their clean values does not improve reconstruction. Activation patching across layers shows that the first MLP has the largest restoration and injection effects. Within this layer, a different set of 64 neurons recovers 44.1\% of the correlation loss on held-out scenes. When the speckle-selective neurons are held at their noisy values, 96.5\% of this gain remains, while holding the values of three decoder heads removes 37.4\%. In an ImageNet-pretrained MAE, restoration is most effective in the third MLP block. On manipulated Sentinel-1 and Capella images, the same 64 neurons also improve reconstruction, whereas for land-cover classification, only restoring the entire first MLP yields a clear gain. The neurons that respond most to speckle are therefore not the ones the model uses to recover the scene.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33632) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33632)

## 226. Greedy Decoding Hides Good Plans: Sample-and-Verify Planning for Vision-Language Driving Models

- **作者**：Zhipeng Bao, Wenjie Zhao, Yiping Li et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.CV

Vision-language models (VLMs) are increasingly adopted for end-to-end driving planning, yet their performance is typically evaluated based on a single greedily decoded trajectory. However, greedy decoding may overlook better trajectories within the model's output distribution. Experiments on WOD-E2E and NAVSIM reveal a consistent performance gap between greedily decoded trajectories and the best sampled candidates, showing that planning errors can arise from trajectory selection, rather than trajectory generation alone. On WOD-E2E, when the ego vehicle should start moving, the model often assigns probability to different possible start times, causing greedy decoding to repeatedly favor waiting and produce a stationary plan even though moving trajectories are available. Motivated by these findings, we propose Sample-and-Verify (SaV), which keeps the VLM planner frozen and samples multiple candidate trajectories. A lightweight verifier is trained from ground-truth futures to estimate the relative quality of these candidates. It uses trajectory kinematics, candidate-set agreement and likelihood, and ego-motion features to select the final trajectory. On WOD-E2E, SaV reduces ADE and FDE by approximately 15% across three VLM planners on the validation set. Changes in RFS are smaller: two planners show slight decreases on val-456, while Alpamayo-1.5 improves from 7.74 to 7.81 RFS on the official test set as ADE@5s decreases by 11%. On NAVSIM, SaV reduces ADE@4s by 10-24% across three VLM planners, while PDMS changes are less consistent. These results show that trajectory selection can substantially improve displacement accuracy without additional planner training. The code is publicly available at https://github.com/hzxbzp/sample-and-verify.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33621) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33621)

## 227. Learning from Natural Images Untangles Number Sense

- **作者**：Yechan Cho, Hyeonsu Lee, Emma Deneuville et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.AI, cs.CG, cs.CV, cs.LG, q-bio.NC

Numerical cognition is a simple yet fundamental ability that remains challenging for modern vision-language models. Meanwhile, number sense is already present in numerically naïve animals, suggesting that it can emerge without explicit numerical learning. Deep neural networks provide a related observation, with numerosity-selective units appearing after training on unrelated natural-image tasks and even before any training. However, these findings leave unresolved whether visual learning contributes beyond the spontaneous emergence of numerical selectivity. Human development provides a clue, as children become better at making judgments in which numerosity and proportion conflict. Here, we introduce a geometric framework to trace how representations of these two quantities change across learning. Although untrained and pretrained networks exhibited similar unit-level numerosity selectivity and basic comparison performance, natural-image pretraining increased the angle between numerosity and proportion subspaces while reducing their overlap, supporting more robust readout under conflict. Direct manipulation of these geometric properties further demonstrated their functional contributions to decoding accuracy and functional independence. Importantly, conventional supervised pretraining was not essential for quantity separation, which also emerged through self-supervised learning without category labels and synthetic-image training without natural-image exposure. Together, our findings suggest that structured visual learning can refine spontaneously emerging number sense into robust and functionally independent quantity representations without explicit numerical training.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33615) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33615)

## 228. VivaWorld: Long-Horizon Video World Model With Compositional 3D Priors

- **作者**：Ying Jiang, Donglai Xiang, Anita Hu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：17 figures, 10 tables, 26 pages

Long-horizon video generation is key to interactive world modeling, physical intelligence, and spatial reasoning, yet maintaining geometric consistency and accurate camera control remains challenging without 3D grounding. We introduce \textbf{VivaWorld}, a framework for generating geometry- and view-consistent long videos from an image. We first construct a compositional hybrid 3D representation that combines reconstructed point clouds, which preserve observed scene geometry and appearance, with generated textured meshes, which provide complete object geometry across $360^\circ$ novel views. We also introduce a connected-region filter removing inconsistent floating components for robust cross-view reconstruction and propose a hybrid camera embedding that combines global and object-anchored Plücker rays to capture both scene-level camera motion and camera-object relationships, and inject it together with the visual guidance into a video generation model via a control adapter. Extensive experiments show VivaWorld achieves state-of-the-art visual quality and camera control, while supporting controllable novel-view synthesis, long-horizon scene exploration, and physical AI. Project page is available at: https://research.nvidia.com/labs/sil/projects/vivaworld/

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33591) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33591)

## 229. SEEL: Towards End-to-End 3D Sign Generation with Language Models

- **作者**：Ronglai Zuo, Fei Ni, Junpeng Jing et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.CL

3D sign language generation aims to produce signing motion from written text. Motivated by the linguistic nature of sign language, recent methods combine a motion tokenizer with a language model to predict motion tokens from text. However, both components are optimized through a two-stage pipeline with mismatched objectives: the tokenizer minimizes reconstruction error in isolation, while the generator learns from rigid one-hot reference tokens. A straightforward end-to-end extension bridges discrete token selection with straight-through estimators (STE), suffering from biased gradients and codebook collapse. To address these limitations, we propose SEEL, an end-to-end framework that aligns tokenization and generation with motion quality through two complementary mechanisms. First, to overcome rigid one-hot supervision, reconstruction-aware generation decodes candidate tokens in context and converts their 3D motion errors into soft targets, guiding the generator with physical motion quality. Second, to propagate generation feedback without STE, generation-aware tokenization passes continuous codebook expectations through the frozen generator, routing generated-motion gradients to the tokenizer to prevent codebook collapse. Combining both mechanisms enables the tokenizer and generator to co-evolve, achieving state-of-the-art performance on CSL-Daily, How2Sign, and Phoenix-2014T. Code and models will be released to facilitate future research.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33588) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33588)

## 230. LLMVC: Large Language Model-Guided Generative Video Compression

- **作者**：Lebin Zhou, Zhenghao Chen, Nam Ling et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Recent generative video methods achieve strong perceptual compression by encoding videos into compact latent representations and reconstructing them with powerful generative decoders. However, visual encoders must compress both high-level semantics and dense visual details into a unified latent space, which complicates entropy coding and decoding and may cause semantic drift under aggressive compression. In this work, we propose Large Language Model-Guided Video Compression (LLMVC), which leverages the summarization capability of frozen video large language models (Video-LLMs) to generate compact semantic tokens aligned with temporal latent slices for guiding video compression. Specifically, Hybrid Bidirectional Bimodal Attention (HBBA) enables bidirectional interactions between visual and semantic features during encoding and decoding. Semantic-Conditioned Cross-Attention (SCCA) incorporates semantic features into probability modeling during entropy coding. These semantic features provide high-level guidance throughout the pipeline, improving representation quality and encouraging semantic consistency through a transmitted text side channel. Experiments on UVG, MCL-JCV, and HEVC show that LLMVC consistently improves compression performance over traditional and recent neural video compression methods in terms of LPIPS and DISTS, while also achieving better fidelity than recent perceptual codecs.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33580) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33580)

## 231. DoseSteer: Steering diffusion-based dose prediction models towards clinical targets via Dual-Response Guidance

- **作者**：Shupeng Cheng, Jing Jia, Jincheng Wu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

AI dose-prediction models generate patient-specific 3D dose distributions from patient anatomy. Yet these predictions can still miss structure-level clinical dose targets. Once deployed, most existing predictors offer no direct mechanism to correct such deviations, deferring target enforcement to downstream plan optimization. We introduce DoseSteer, an inference-time framework that steers frozen diffusion-based dose prediction models towards specified clinical targets without retraining. To meet clinical targets while preserving overall dose prediction quality, we propose Dual-Response Guidance (DRG), a structure-centric algorithm that probes the model's responses to perturbations in each structure's noisy dose and context and jointly solves for per-structure steering strengths. Across five dataset-model configurations spanning three diffusion architectures and three datasets, DRG outperforms 12 training-free baselines under identical reference-derived target proxies, removing at least 97% of the target error, reducing the official dose error by 7.8% on average, and improving dose fidelity outside the steered structures. DoseSteer reconciles learned anatomical dose priors with explicit structure-level targets at test time, establishing a principled, training-free mechanism to refine AI dose predictions prior to plan optimization. Code is available at https://github.com/csp2002/DoseSteer.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33577) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33577)

## 232. ProPolar: Progressive Polar Decomposition for Implicit Neural Representations

- **作者**：Pureum Kim, Younggeon Ryu, Dongyoon Lee et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Implicit neural representations (INRs) capture global low-frequency structure during the early stage of training and then refine localized high-frequency details, afterwards. However, standard optimizers are agnostic to the coarse-to-fine learning behavior of INRs. Such optimizers accumulate a gradient matrix in momentum buffer and retain components misaligned with the dominant low-frequency directions in the early stage. We propose a progressively growing rank scheduler and a rank-aware learning-rate scheduler for enhancing a subspace-based momentum optimization, where a momentum matrix is updated within a low-rank subspace. The rank schedule expands the subspace during training, and the scheduler holds the peak learning rate through rank growth so that the added subspace contributes to fine-detail recovery. Experiments on image fitting, single-image super-resolution (SISR), and neural radiance field optimization show that the proposed method improves convergence and peak reconstruction quality over competitive optimizers. The largest gains appear in the high-frequency refinement stage, mirroring the coarse-to-fine progression that the rank schedule targets. A significant study on hyperparameter optimization (HPO) on NeRF further confirms that these gains persist under matched search budgets.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33567) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33567)

## 233. EgoCARBS: Egocentric Video Meets Active Acoustics for Kitchen Action Understanding

- **作者**：Saif Mahmud, Vineet Parikh, Bharath Hariharan et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-25 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.HC
- **备注**：36 pages (12 main paper and references, 24 supplementary material). Project page: https://saif-mahmud.github.io/projects/ego-carbs

Egocentric video records what a person looks at, but often misses what their body does: the hands leave the frame, and arm motion goes unseen. An exocentric camera recovers this context, but it must be installed wherever the activity happens. Active acoustic sensing can recover it from the wearables themselves: a device's speaker emits inaudible chirps, and its microphone senses the motion of the hands, arms, and nearby objects from their echoes. Yet no public dataset pairs it with egocentric video. We present EgoCARBS, with two deliverables. The first is a multimodal dataset and benchmark: 15 participants cook unscripted meals in their own kitchens wearing camera glasses and an off-the-shelf smartwatch that both sense with active acoustics, synchronized with egocentric and exocentric video and the watch's audio and IMU, with 4,019 verb-noun action annotations in 11.4 hours. The second is a study of what the wearables add to egocentric video. On verb recognition, they add at least as much as an exocentric camera for participants seen in training, and even more for new participants, for whom the exocentric camera does not help. Active acoustics alone provides most of this gain, and helps most where the camera does not see the hands. Trimmed action recognition, detection, and anticipation remain open. We will release the data, our data collection and annotation tools, and pre-trained models.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33565) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33565)

## 234. Frame geometry, fascicle extrapolation and leaderboard overfitting in muscle ultrasound

- **作者**：Soheil Kooklan
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, eess.IV
- **备注**：38 pages (20 main text, 18 supplement), 13 figures, 21 tables. Code, trained weights, notebooks and derived data are public on Kaggle (user soheilkooklan) and in the ancillary files

Automated readings of pennation angle, fascicle length and muscle thickness from B-mode ultrasound depend on the geometry of the stored frame and on how fascicles that leave the image are extended, and challenge entries add settings chosen on a small public leaderboard. This study measures all three in one entry of the UMUD Challenge, which scored 309 frames from six screen layouts, 103 on a public and 206 on a private leaderboard. The pipeline applies layout-specific values of scale and pixel aspect, resamples each frame to square pixels, segments aponeuroses and fascicle fragments with U-Nets and measures the three readings geometrically; the submitted entry blended its angle and length with public notebooks. Because the score is additive, 65 single-change contrasts from 13 base entries measured each decision on both splits. The primary selected entry scored 0.27608 on the private split, the 69th reported position of 690 teams. Reading every frame with square pixels cost 0.029 public and 0.037 private. Of 20 late-stage corrections, 9 kept the sign of their effect on the private split. An audit of the UMUD expert benchmark found that 26 of its 35 frames share their tissue with training images of both DL_Track_US and the challenge. On the 9 frames with no detected match in either set or orientation, the recipe scored 0.213, two experts against two others 0.218 and DL_Track_US 0.263 (0.209, 0.196 and 0.259 when both raters of a reference pair are required), with no difference established. Frame geometry and the extension rule moved readings as much as the choice of model, and small corrections tuned on about 100 public frames did not carry over.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33539) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33539)

## 235. AVERT: Anomalous Value Erasure with Response Testing against Visual Prompt Injection

- **作者**：Newt Hue-Nam K. Nguyen, Jui-Yu Ho, Wei-Han Chen et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：21 pages, 13 figures, 15 tables

Vision Language Models (VLMs) are vulnerable to visual prompt injection, in which an adversarially perturbed image forces the model to respond with the attacker's chosen content, such as malicious tool calls, website links, or advertisements, sometimes regardless of the user's question. Existing defenses, such as diffusion-based purification and image-token masking, mitigate these attacks at a substantial cost to the model's utility on benign inputs. To address this challenge, we propose AVERT, which removes adversarial feature directions only when the model's answer depends on them. Our key procedures involve identifying anomalous value subspaces and detecting answer dependence. To identify the anomalous value subspace, we compare the statistics of each input's attention-head outputs with those of clean images and extract the directions along which the input deviates the most. We then project out these directions to remove the injected content. Because the same directions also carry benign visual information, we apply the removal only to answers that depend on them, namely answers whose likelihood collapses once the directions are removed or that are as likely under unrelated questions as under the user's question. Against white-box attacks on four VLMs (Qwen2-VL, Qwen2.5-VL, LLaVA, InternVL2.5) and four injection benchmarks, AVERT lowers average attack success from 86-90% to at most 0.6% while retaining at least 96% of undefended utility averaged over thirteen benchmarks. Its defense-utility F-score ($F_{DU}$), the harmonic mean of attacks stopped and utility retained, is 98.0-99.1 and the highest among all compared defenses on every model.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33532) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33532)

## 236. ArtLang-4D: Articulation-Aware Dynamic Language Fields from Monocular Videos

- **作者**：Sylvia Yuan, Dan Wang, Ravi Ramamoorthi et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Language that describes an articulated object is often about its state: the drawer pulled out the furthest, the door opened halfway, push the drawer all the way in. Existing 3D language fields attach one feature to each point and cannot tell these descriptions apart, and 4D language fields condition on video time, which has no kinematic meaning and cannot be set by a user. We present ArtLang-4D, which reconstructs an articulated object from a single monocular RGB-D video and equips it with a dynamic language field indexed by articulation time t, the normalized state of its joints, instead of video time. The geometry is a canonical triangle field whose triangles follow typed screw joints, discovered from the video without any joint initialization. The language field is supervised by a t-tagged caption bank that describes each part with comparative state phrases in a whitened open-vocabulary text space. A free-form query is answered by maximizing part relevance jointly over parts and states, which returns both the part and the articulation state it describes; a command is then executed by moving only that part along its recovered joint. On the 100 objects of Articulate-100, ArtLang-4D recovers more accurate joints and part geometry than reconstruction and articulation baselines, retrieves the queried state far more reliably than a state-agnostic language field, and executes language commands with lower motion error.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33511) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33511)

## 237. Separating Environment and Sensor Shift from Acquisition Protocol in Physical Vision Benchmarks

- **作者**：Yuyang Yuan, Jason Wu, Mohammadali Khodabandehlou et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

In real-world deployments, vision systems must operate under varying environmental conditions, such as illumination changes. Recent work has explored adaptive sensing strategies that select camera parameters according to the current environment and downstream task to improve task performance. Reliable physical benchmarks are therefore essential for evaluating such strategies. We study ImageNet-ES-Diverse, a physical benchmark that evaluates the adaptive exposure control strategy Lens by recapturing printed ImageNet images under varying illumination conditions. We construct four controlled acquisition pipelines that vary printed-image scale and camera zoom, and conduct a pilot study with directly captured physical objects. We find that the acquisition choices substantially change benchmark difficulty on all downstream baselines. Moreover, the average classification-accuracy advantage of Lens over auto exposure observed on the original benchmark does not persist in either our collected pipelines or real-object pilot study. Oracle exposure selection nevertheless retains substantial gains, indicating remaining potential for sensor control. These findings highlight acquisition protocol as an important factor in physical benchmark evaluation and motivate explicit protocol reporting and evaluation across multiple acquisition setups when assessing adaptive-sensing benefits.

## 238. A 3D Simulator for Passive SPAD Imaging

- **作者**：Elia Mergaert, Rong Zou, Roberto Pellerito et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.RO

Passive single-photon avalanche diode (SPAD) cameras provide short-exposure observations for low-light perception, but collecting task annotations for these sensors remains difficult, and annotated datasets are scarce. We present a 3D simulation pipeline that generates realistic photon detections together with camera-motion and semantic labels, enabling perception models to be trained on synthetic SPAD observations and applied directly to real-world data. The pipeline couples controllable scene geometry and motion with a sensor-informed observation model that accounts for photon shot noise, dark counts, and spatial crosstalk. We evaluate the pipeline through calibrated photon-count comparisons and zero-shot sim-to-real transfer across three downstream perception tasks. We show that classifiers trained only on synthetic SPAD data achieve high accuracy on real low-light recordings at illumination levels less than 2 lux, without real-data fine-tuning. Similarly, we apply models trained entirely on simulated camera sweeps to real-world camera-rotation estimation and achieve mean absolute rotation errors of less than 1 degree on real 20-degree and 30-degree sweeps at 0.5 and 5.0 lux, when deployed zero-shot. We additionally train semantic segmentation models on synthetic data with automatically generated dense labels and perform direct inference on real-world low-light recordings; the results further demonstrate the usefulness of synthetic dense supervision for real low-light perception. Our simulator thus provides a practical source of annotated training data for real SPAD perception.

## 239. EvOrderBench: When do sampled frames establish event order?

- **作者**：Xiaoqin Feng, Yuqi Li, Xinyuan Li et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Vision-language models (VLMs) are often asked which of two actions in a video happened first, given only a few sampled frames. Such frames can show that both actions occurred without showing which came first, so a reliable model should state an order only when the frames establish it. Forced-choice temporal benchmarks treat every input as answerable and reward lucky guesses, while answerability benchmarks do not separate frames that miss an action from frames that show both actions but not their order. We introduce EvOrderBench, a real-video benchmark of 579 event families. Each family fixes an episode and a question and varies only the frames: of two four-frame inputs, only one establishes the order, and two six-frame inputs extend the other one with either the frames that resolve the order or frames that do not. Every input is labeled for whether its frames establish each action and their order. Across sixteen open-weight configurations and three closed-source references, the best open models order nearly as accurately as the references when the frames establish the order, but withhold correctly on at most 83% of the inputs where they do not, against at least 90% for every reference; forced-choice accuracy cannot reveal this gap. Open models withhold mainly when the frames show neither action; once the frames show one or both, most of them commit to an order. Thinking narrows the gap, even when it is switched on within a single checkpoint, mainly by separating sufficient from insufficient evidence rather than by making models more cautious overall. Accurate ordering thus does not imply knowing when the frames fall short, and benchmarks should reward withholding alongside ordering.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33492) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33492)

## 240. Memory as the Backbone: A Dual-Store Associative Memory Architecture for Visual Recognition

- **作者**：Ruizhi Wang, Xiangtao Wang, Bayar Menzat et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.LG

We investigate whether explicit memory can serve as the organizing principle of a general-purpose visual backbone. Inspired by the interaction between working and long-term memory in human cognition, we introduce DuSAM (Dual-Store Associative Memory), a hierarchical memory-native visual backbone coupling image-local working memory (WM) with a fixed-capacity persistent semantic memory (PM). WM organizes local visual evidence into compact, spatially anchored states that are updated and transported across stages, then read back to refine dense features. PM consolidates semantic evidence from training images and annotated regions into prototypes, whose retrieved content enriches the same working states. With these memory operations in place of self-attention or state-space blocks, DuSAM achieves competitive performance relative to representative, similarly sized backbones on ImageNet-1K classification, ADE20K semantic segmentation, and COCO object detection and instance segmentation. Ablations show contributions from both stores, with PM particularly benefiting class groups with fewer annotated training pixels. At $2048\times2048$, the complete DuSAM-based UPerNet segmentor achieves $2.36\times$ the throughput of the evaluated DeiT-S/16 counterpart while using $59.5\%$ less GPU memory, and $1.24\times$ the throughput of the linear-complexity VMamba-T. Memory analyses reveal spatially selective WM access and semantic associations between retrieved prototypes and training regions. Class-targeted removal and restoration further demonstrate that category-specific experience can be replenished through state updates without changing network parameters. These results establish explicit memory as a viable organizing principle for visual backbones and provide a foundation for jointly designing contextual computation and addressable semantic memory.

## 241. Streaming Tax: Can Streaming VLMs Replace Their Offline Parents?

- **作者**：Zhi Yang, Junzhou Chen, Jindong Wang
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Most vision-language models (VLMs) specialize in offline video understanding; in contrast, their post-trained versions, streaming VLMs, can perform live interactions. We investigate whether streaming VLMs can replace their offline parent models on offline tasks. Such replacement could reduce deployment costs and improve efficiency. We propose Streaming Tax to systematically evaluate streaming VLMs on offline tasks. Our exploration spans three core dimensions: understanding, trustworthiness, and generalization across 15 benchmarks. Our findings reveal a critical gap between offline capability preservation and practical replaceability. This gap emerges at two stages: streaming adaptation can alter trustworthy behavior even when offline understanding is largely preserved, while the native streaming protocol can introduce further performance losses. Our analyses show that some of these losses arise from restricted access to historical visual evidence and failures to deliver usable responses. Importantly, regarding safety, streaming models often leave both harmful and legitimate requests unanswered rather than genuinely refusing unsafe ones. We then propose Stream++, a training-free plugin to improve streaming VLMs on offline tasks.

## 242. SplatWAM: Splatting World Action Models for Real-Time 3D Imagination

- **作者**：Frank Tsu-Fang Lu, Karthik Sundar Senthilkumar, Christopher Thirgood et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.LG, cs.RO
- **备注**：21 pages, 15 figures, 13 tables

World models typically predict future observations in pixel or latent space, limiting their ability to directly represent true 3D scene evolution. We present SplatWAM (Splatting World Action Model), a generative world model that learns action-conditioned dynamics directly over compact 3D Gaussian primitives. A tokenizer SplatWAM-T first encodes a variable number of unposed views into a fixed budget of Gaussian tokens. A dynamics model SplatWAM-D then predicts future Gaussian states in a single forward pass per action, using log-Euclidean covariance coordinates and differentiable rendering supervision to facilitate learning in an explicit 3D space. Each predicted state can be rasterized from arbitrary viewpoints, enforcing a shared 3D representation across views by construction. On OGBench Scene, SplatWAM renders novel views 12x faster than video world models (~80 FPS on an RTX 5090) with up to 2.0 dB higher PSNR. We further introduce SplatWAM-P, a lightweight flow-matching policy head jointly trained with the dynamics model. Across 8 task suites, model-free Gaussian-token policies outperform pixel-based counterparts by up to 24.8 percentage points. SplatWAM-P improves over Latent Particle World Model (LPWM) by 12 percentage points and matches HIQL without a learned value function. On LIBERO, our ~1B model outperforms WorldVLA (~7B) and the robot-pretrained OpenVLA (~7B), while matching OpenVLA-OFT when both are trained without large-scale robot pretraining. Code and checkpoints will be released.

## 243. OrdiHand-GS: Ordinal-Guided Tracking and Hand-Refined Gaussian Avatars for Digital Signers

- **作者**：Mingxuan Ma, Ronglai Zuo
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：18 pages, 6 figures, 15 tables. Project page: https://mmxmichelle.github.io/OrdiHand-GS/

Digital signers, also known as signing avatars, convey sign language through animated virtual humans, where visual fidelity and linguistic intelligibility hinge primarily on the hands. When one hand crosses over the other, two failure modes dominate. First, monocular expressive-human tracking suffers from ordinal depth ambiguity: either hand can satisfy the same 2D landmarks, and the baseline tracker orders wrists at chance level on 365 annotated overlapping frames from How2Sign. Second, hand appearance remains blurred in neural rendering even after geometry is corrected. We propose OrdiHand-GS to address these errors separately. Relative depth from a video depth estimator and apparent hand size vote, with confidence weighting and abstention, for a front-back relation, which enters expressive-human tracking as a hinge on median hand-surface depth. A source-conditioned residual head then refines the local positions of hand-associated UV Gaussians with the avatar backbone and renderer frozen. We instantiate the framework with GUAVA. Against matched unconstrained refinement, wrist-order accuracy rises from 32.9% to 77.3% at a cost of 0.39 pixels of reprojection error, while a random-sign placebo gives no balanced-accuracy gain. A 2x2 factorial evaluation attributes most of the interaction-rendering improvement to tracking-stage refinement, with a smaller additional benefit from the residual.

## 244. FoamSurf: A Voronoi-Surfel Representation for Differentiable Rendering

- **作者**：Grzegorz Wilczyński, Julia Farganus, Krzysztof Byrski et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Scenes inside a scattering medium contain solid surfaces, such as the seabed or rocks, and a semi-transparent medium, such as water, fog, or smoke. Recent Gaussian-based methods produce realistic images of scenes in scattering media, but traces of the medium can remain visible in surface-only renders. Neural fields separate surfaces and medium more accurately, but they are slow to train and difficult to edit. Both approaches evaluate the medium only along camera rays, so its effects along reflected and refracted rays are not modeled. These limitations prevent reliable surface recovery, independent editing of surfaces and the medium, and consistent rendering of inserted objects. We present FoamSurf, a hybrid representation that models surfaces with planar Gaussian surfels and the medium with space-filling Voronoi cells. A single differentiable ray tracer composites both along arbitrary rays, while training priors keep them separate without segmentation masks. Across real underwater scenes, synthetic scenes, and dense fog, FoamSurf achieves image quality on par with the state of the art and the most accurate surface-medium separation among the compared methods. The recovered components can be removed, edited, or stylized independently, and the same reconstruction supports inserted mesh objects with attenuation and scattering along both direct and reflected or refracted rays. Together, these capabilities enable scene restoration, editing, and composition within a single reconstructed model. Project page: https://grzegorzwilczynski.com/foamsurf/

## 245. Playfulness as Feedback for Agentic Game Development

- **作者**：Weijia Mao, Kevin Qinghong Lin, Zhen Fang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：29 pages

Coding agents can generate runnable games from natural-language descriptions. However, a runnable game does not guarantee an excellent gameplay experience for players. In our view, a playful game should provide 1) reliable core functionality, 2) appealing visual design and assets, and 3) rich gameplay that supports exploration and progression. How agents can improve these aspects of playfulness remains underexplored. In our work, we propose Play2Evolve, a recursive game development framework with three stages: Initialization, Refinement, and Evolution. Using playfulness as feedback, agents playtest the game and diagnose issues in its functionality, visual presentation, and gameplay. Their feedback guides bug fixes and asset improvements. It also helps agents propose new gameplay and aesthetic goals for continued game evolution. To enrich game materials, we combine a video generation model with code to create appealing static and dynamic assets and integrate them into interactive gameplay. Finally, Play2Evolve achieves state-of-the-art performance among the evaluated methods, scoring 60.05 on GameCraft-Bench compared with 50.81 after Game Initialization and 52.46 for Play2Code. Human overall-quality ratings reach 3.67, compared with 2.38 and 2.43, respectively. Our code will be released at https://github.com/showlab/Play2Evolve.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33457) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33457)

## 246. Recovering What Matters, Transferring Only What Is Reliable: Toward Precise Reference-Based Super-Resolution

- **作者**：Sihan Ji, Wenxin Chen, Yue Shi et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：15 pages, 8 figures, 7 tables

Reference-based super-resolution (RefSR) aims to recover missing details from high-quality references. However, existing methods face two fundamental challenges. First, perceptual plausibility does not guarantee content fidelity. Existing methods often produce visually convincing details without faithfully recovering reference-specific information, such as text, codes, and identity cues. Second, faithful reference reading does not guarantee reliable information transfer. Increasing reference utilization also exposes the restoration process to misleading content, as global semantic relevance does not necessarily imply local correspondence consistency. To address these challenges, we propose PreciseRef, a reference-aware generative framework for faithful and reliable RefSR. At its core, Asymmetric Twin-Attention (ATA) allows target and reference images to participate in a unified attention space while the reference keeps its own shape. By letting their image tokens interact directly through joint attention, ATA facilitates fine-grained reference information retrieval without explicit image alignment. To ensure reliable information transfer, we introduce Dual-Scale Reference Gating (DRG), which combines Semantic Relevance Gating (SRG) to filter globally irrelevant reference context and Bidirectional Consistency Gating (BCG) to suppress unreliable local correspondences. Furthermore, we establish VeriRef, a benchmark evaluating exact reference-dependent content recovery and robustness to mismatched references. Extensive experiments show that our method achieves 85.6% character accuracy and 76.3% exact code decoding, compared with 17.4% and 0% for existing methods, while effectively suppressing erroneous reference transfer. It also achieves state-of-the-art perceptual quality on CUFED5 and WR-SR.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33455) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33455)

## 247. HumblePRM: Improving VLM Abstention in Ambiguous Situations through Process Reward Modeling

- **作者**：Saket Reddy, Ke Yang, ChengXiang Zhai
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.CL, cs.LG

Reliable deployment of Vision Language Models (VLMs) requires that a model's expressed confidence remain grounded in what it can actually see, and that it recognize when the visual evidence is too ambiguous to commit to a confident response. Standard post-training uses outcome reward models (ORMs) that supervise only the final decision, rewarding a confident answer regardless of whether the image supports one, while existing methods either tend to detect uncertainty post-hoc or assume an objective verdict always exists. We instead split multimodal reasoning into two stages: perception, where the model must factually describe the scene, and calibration, where it must judge whether that evidence is sufficient to commit to a conclusion. We introduce HumblePRM, a lightweight process reward model (PRM) that scores each stage independently, rewarding agreement between evidence and expressed confidence rather than outcome correctness alone. Training with HumblePRM increases abstention due to uncertainty on ambiguous inputs considerably while leaving accuracy on clear images roughly unchanged. We also find that the improvement transfers to Gemma 4 E4B, a larger, popular edge model. Our findings show that process reward supervision is a promising route to VLMs with better calibrated abstention, ensuring more reliability rather than unpredictable behavior.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33452) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33452)

## 248. OnCue: Generating Safety-Critical Driving Scenarios under Occlusion with Temporal Control

- **作者**：Kailang Ma, Li Song, Weining Ren et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.CV

Occluded hazards challenge autonomous driving because visibility timing determines available avoidance actions. Existing scenario generators control hazard motion without jointly specifying exposure and reference-contact times. We propose OnCue, a framework that generates safety-critical encounters in real driving logs with these temporal conditions. Scene geometry provides hidden starts and timed waypoints for pedestrian, cyclist, and vehicle trajectory generation. An offline response oracle labels encounters as Reactive (avoidable after exposure) or Anticipatory (avoidable only with earlier visibility) and stores safe maneuvers as training demonstrations. On the Waymo Open Motion Dataset, 90.1-96.6% of valid trajectories meet both requested times within +/-0.2 s. OnCue matches the requested reaction window between them in 92.8% of shared vehicle requests versus at most 42.1% for adapted adversarial generators. Across matched cyclist encounters, longer windows lower PDM-Closed collision from 59.1% to 5.5%, while the learned planner LatentDriver shows the opposite trend. OnCue demonstrations reduce LatentDriver target collision by 54.8 points on held-out Reactive tests, but reduce overall no-target progress by 12.7 m. These results highlight the value of temporal control for evaluating and improving planners under occlusion.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33439) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33439)

## 249. ViCARD: Visual Credit Assignment with Reward-Gated Distillation for Multimodal Reasoning

- **作者**：Zijian Huang, Jiasi Chen
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.LG
- **备注**：23 pages, 12 figures

Visual reasoning requires models to identify relevant visual evidence and use it to derive correct answers. Outcomebased reinforcement learning (RL) faces two key challenges in this regime: it yields no learning signal when perceptual errors cause all sampled responses to fail, and it broadcasts a uniform advantage across every token within a response. Conversely, while visual self-distillation offers dense guidance from evidence-centered image crops, student learning from token distributions alone often fails to elicit correct final answers. Motivated by an audit of training rollouts that exposes these complementary failure modes, we introduce ViCARD (Visual Credit Assignment with RewardGated Distillation), which integrates per-token visual supervision with verified correctness rewards. For each rollout group, we leverage a teacher with its input image cropped to the relevant visual area, and use this to provide per-token supervision to the student while also valuing correctness. If all sampled rollouts fail, ViCARD samples an additional crop-teacher answer and activates on-policy distillation only if that answer is correct. Together, these mechanisms provide fine-grained visual credit within informative reward groups and a learning signal for otherwise uninformative all-fail groups. Extensive comparisons across model scales demonstrate that ViCARD improves fine-grained visual reasoning performance over RL and distillation baselines, while retaining broad visual reasoning capabilities.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33437) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33437)

## 250. Anatomy-Aware Pseudo-Labels and Homotopy-Annealed Hausdorff Supervision for Semi-Supervised Pancreas Segmentation

- **作者**：Alireza Kheyrkhah, Amir Hossein Saleknia, Reza Azad et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：10 pages, 4 figures, 5 tables. Accepted to WACV 2027 Workshops (FABRIC). \c{opyright} 2027 IEEE

Confidence-gated co-training assumes that pseudo-label errors are uncertain and concentrated at organ boundaries. We argue that for pancreas CT, where the target organ is a single connected structure and detached fragments are anatomically implausible, the dominant failures arise at three levels: where the organ is, what its boundary should look like, and how a correct boundary is penalized. Anatomical Rectification relabels implausible fragments of a teacher prediction as confident background, without adding any loss or parameter; an audit confirms that its rejections are overwhelmingly detached hallucinations and that thin, tail-like slices are not eroded. Boundary Grafting transplants labelled surface regions with their annotation into unlabeled volumes, replacing unreliable boundary pseudo-labels with ground truth. The residual error is coherent surface displacement, weakly penalized by permutation-invariant Dice and cross-entropy losses. Homotopy-Annealed Hausdorff (HAH) supervision anneals a distance-weighted penalty from an annotation-anchored objective that is convex in the prediction to a Hausdorff surrogate, admitting the prediction-dependent distance term only after the predicted surface becomes reliable. On NIH pancreas CT, under the standard semi-supervised split, the full method reaches 84.60% Dice and 4.97 voxels 95HD, improving all four metrics over compared methods; on MSD Task07, 78.90% Dice and 4.19 voxels 95HD. We further contribute PanTS-SSL, a semi-supervised evaluation protocol derived from the public 9,901-case PanTS corpus, with a curated 1,015-volume main pool, labelled tiers of 50, 100 and 250 volumes, an 80-case tumor track, shift probes for acquisition and anatomy change, and a fixed 901-volume test set. On it, the method exceeds the co-training baseline at 100 labels, scales to 85.29% Dice at 250 labels, and reaches 85.50% Dice at 100 labels with HAH.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33425) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33425)

## 251. Beyond Random Starts: Amortized Initialization for Adversarial Attacks on Vision-Language Models

- **作者**：Haz Sameen Shahgir, Ali Asif Khan, K. M. Mehemud Azad et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Gradient-based transferable adversarial attacks achieve state-of-the-art attack success rates against black-box vision-language models (VLMs). However, they require hundreds of costly optimization steps for each image pair. One possible reason is that these attacks typically start from random perturbations that contain no information about the source or target image. In this work, we study whether amortized adversarial generation can provide better initializations for gradient-based attacks. By learning across many image pairs, amortization allows a generator to produce adversarial perturbations for new inputs without optimizing each perturbation from scratch. We introduce SPARK, a lightweight looped perturbation generator conditioned on both the source and target images. SPARK repeatedly applies the same network to refine a perturbation, producing effective adversarial attacks in a few forward passes without requiring gradients through surrogate vision encoders at inference time. We show that these perturbations provide effective warm starts for existing gradient-based attacks, substantially improving attack success rates while reducing the number of optimization steps required. These improvements generalize across different attack methods, VLMs, and unseen image categories. SPARK also produces effective standalone attacks at a fraction of the computational cost of gradient-based optimization. Code and model weights are available at https://github.com/Patchwork53/SPARK.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33414) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33414)

## 252. A Semantics-First Approach to Multimodal Video Captioning with Frozen Encoders and Stabilized Reinforcement Learning

- **作者**：Albert Chiwara, Erhan Guven
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.CL, cs.LG
- **备注**：13 pages, 6 figures, 3 tables. Preprint correcting minor typographical errors and clarifying baseline evaluation conditions from the SPIE Defense + Security 2026 proceedings

We propose the Audio-Visual Query Transformer (AVQFormer), a highly efficient architecture built upon a 2.7B parameter LLM featuring a fixed-length $K=16$ semantic alignment module, stabilized via Adaptive Self-Critical Sequence Training (ASCST) with Entropy Regularization. Multimodal Large Language Models (LLMs) often rely on global audio representations to compensate for inefficient, frame-based visual encoders. While our Phase 1 prompted evaluations demonstrate that the Multimodal Base Model achieves strong deep semantic alignment (scoring 0.889 on BERTScore F1), optimizing this architecture for legacy metrics reveals a performance gap. We explore this gap as a hypothesis we term temporal alignment friction: a conflict where global audio representations disrupt the chronological sequence of visual frames, leading to penalties under visually-focused evaluation metrics. Consequently, by stripping away the audio, our Video-Only ablation conforms more closely to this rigid syntax, achieving 63.0 CIDEr. While trailing larger models such as Qwen2.5-VL (67.4 CIDEr), this establishes a strong baseline for sub-3B parameter LLM-based models. This indicates that CIDEr, as applied to datasets such as MSR-VTT, may exhibit a bias toward visually grounded syntax, limiting its ability to fully capture multimodal semantic richness.

## 253. ArtKit: Motion-Driven Re-Segmentation and Link Re-Generation for Articulated Objects from RGB-D Video

- **作者**：Sylvia Yuan, Dan Wang, Vivian Yu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

An RGB-D camera that films an object being moved, often from one viewpoint, sees one side of it and how its parts move. We present ArtKit, which takes such a video as input to create an articulated model with complete parts, a kinematic tree, and typed joints. Our central idea is generating the hidden geometry and verifying it against what the camera sees. A generative part prior proposes complete, part-decomposed geometry based on the first frame. Point tracks, grouped into rigid trajectories, split and merge the generated parts into links. We then fit revolute and prismatic joints, with a visibility-aware energy ensuring that the articulated model explains the depth observed across the video. A rule checks against the video to choose between synchronous group fits and per-frame chain fits, so that one pipeline serves a diverse range of articulated objects, such as household items and robotic hands. Finally, we regenerate each link of the chosen fit as one part and keep it wherever it agrees better with the observations. We evaluate on Articulate-100 and on HArt, a new benchmark of 60 PartNet-Mobility objects and 8 robotic hands and grippers rendered as RGB-D videos with ground-truth parts and joints. ArtKit reduces the axis and part motion errors of the strongest baseline by more than half on the objects, as well as the axis error on the hands. ArtKit also runs on real recordings and generated videos.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33403) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33403)

## 254. Sequentially Acquiring Concept Knowledge to Guide Continual Learning

- **作者**：Shivanand Kundargi, Kowshik Thopalli, Tejas Gokhale
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.LG, cs.AI, cs.CV
- **备注**：Published at fifth conference on lifelong learning agents

The goal of continual learning (CL) is to adapt to new data (plasticity) while retaining the knowledge acquired from old data (stability). Existing methods are explicitly designed to focus on balancing stability and plasticity to mitigate the challenge of catastrophic forgetting while promoting learning. However, the impact of order and nature of samples from the new data that the network is trained on remains an under explored factor. We argue that a CL algorithm should have the ability to rank incoming samples in terms of their relationship with prior data and their effect on the learning process. In this work, we investigate if scoring and prioritizing incoming data based on their semantic relationships with the model's current knowledge can benefit CL. We propose SACK, short for Sequentially Acquiring Concept Knowledge, a plug and play technique for continual learning methods. SACK dissects categorical knowledge of the model into fine-grained concepts, computes the relationships between previously learned concepts and new concepts in each experience, and uses this relationship knowledge for prioritizing new samples. Experiments across several types of CL methods (regularization, replay, and prompt based) in class incremental and task incremental settings demonstrate that integration of SACK not only improves accuracy and reduces forgetting but also handles long tail distribution, helps focus on semantically interpretable regions, and yields better calibrated and robust models compared to prior approaches.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33399) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33399)

## 255. BreathSplat: Grounding One Smooth Motion in the Breathing Phase for 4D Cone-Beam CT

- **作者**：Sujong Shin, Sang-Il Choi, Kwang Moo Yi
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：15 pages, 7 figures

Four-dimensional cone-beam computed tomography (4D-CBCT) images the anatomy of a breathing patient, but sees each breathing phase from only a few angles. Breathing, however, is a smooth motion that repeats; it has a phase. Existing methods exploit this either by reconstructing the anatomy as a function of the breathing phase, often through bins, or by estimating smooth dynamic motions in time. Yet none of these grounds one smooth motion in the phase. We thus propose to share projections across breathing phases with splines. Specifically, we represent the 4D volume with dynamic Gaussians whose motion uses B-splines in space and time. We ground the motion state in the respiratory phase, which we read from well-studied conventional surrogates. Projections at the same phase in different breathing cycles then constrain the same anatomy and motion. On simulated lung scans, our method, BreathSplat, outperforms recent dynamic methods in reconstruction and motion accuracy, including those given the ground-truth phase. We further show qualitatively, on two real patient scans, that our method provides clearer reconstructions than the state of the art.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33392) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33392)

## 256. Measuring and Predicting Image-Token Repair Boundaries in Vision-Language Models

- **作者**：Vishal Bhutani, Prasang Gupta, Sumanth Chundru et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.CL
- **备注**：16 pages, 7 figures, 17 tables; supplementary material included

Degraded images make vision-language models answer wrongly. Using synthetic paired blur, noise, and low light, we trace which clean image states can still repair those answers from the vision encoder through the language model. In four encoder models, 12 to 23 ranked tokens recover half of the full-patch effect, whereas random orders require four to fifteen times as many; encoder-free Gemma needs 22.2 versus 53.1. Full-block repair expires at a model-specific language-model crossing. The matched reverse-direction intervention is algebraically identical to text-token restoration, so its gap from image restoration measures a redundancy band rather than an independent predictor. A clean-image gray-ablation crossing independently predicts held-out repair with 1.53-layer error, against 3.65 for shared depth. Gemma MMBench has the only large attention-to-repair point gap; Gemma GQA does not. Restormer provides a non-oracle consistency check on two models.

## 257. Synchronizing Cameras via 3D Object Relationships

- **作者**：Sujong Shin, Sang-Il Choi, Kwang Moo Yi
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：15 pages, 4 figures

In this paper, we synchronize multiple videos of the same dynamic event through object relationships. Driven by dynamic reconstruction and novel view synthesis, many recent synchronization methods rely on pixel-level evidence, such as point correspondences across views or the photometric losses of a reconstructed scene. In dynamic scenes, however, fast motion and occlusions make the moving regions that carry much of the timing information hard to match across views. We thus propose to bring back the traditional wisdom that objects are reliable cues for global reasoning about cameras. Measured in 3D, the relationships between objects, such as the distance between two people, are stable across all views up to scale; they can serve as cues for alignment. To compare these relationships across cameras, we only need the identity of each object in every view, as the cues are relational. We obtain these identities by giving a vision-language model a few frames of every camera and prompting it to identify the objects in the scene. We then segment and track each identified object through every video with an open-vocabulary segmentor. To obtain the 3D locations of objects, we use a pretrained depth estimator. Where depth or segmentation fails, we complement these 3D cues with 2D motion from point tracks. Finally, we synchronize the cameras by finding the temporal offsets at which the cues of different cameras agree best, first for each camera pair and then for all cameras jointly with robust optimization. We evaluate our method on four standard benchmarks, outperforming the state of the art.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33387) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33387)

## 258. ArtTet: Watertight Part Meshes of Articulated Objects from Rigid-Body Rules and Partitioned Marching Tetrahedra

- **作者**：Sylvia Yuan, Dan Wang, Ravi Ramamoorthi et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

We present ArtTet, a method that reconstructs an articulated object from multi-view observations of two articulation states and outputs one watertight mesh per rigid part together with the joints that move them. Mesh splatting gives explicit triangles, but it treats the object as one scene. It extracts a single global surface and cannot keep the triangles of different parts apart; the mesh it exports is layered, self-intersecting, and full of holes. ArtTet follows a divide-and-conquer strategy instead. We first divide the object into rigid parts with dynamic- and geometric-constrained articulation and ownership inference. A multibody registration of the two states recovers the parts and their screw joints. Rigid-body rules on attachment, contact, and swept motion then correct joint types and part labels without any part annotation. We then divide space into part cells with an articulated tetrahedral partition, and conquer each cell with its own differentiable signed distance field on a shared tetrahedral grid. In these partitioned marching tetrahedra the fields are mutually exclusive by construction, so the extracted part meshes are closed and never overlap. All part meshes are optimized jointly from the images of both states through the recovered joints. On Articulate-100, ArtTet gives the most accurate axis directions, part motions, and part geometry over all objects among methods that use no part masks, and its part meshes have virtually no open boundary edges. We also show reconstructions of real captures from the SplArt dataset.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33382) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33382)

## 259. DreamTour: Layout Steering for Training-Free World Exploration

- **作者**：Srikumar Sastry, Meher Gitika Karumuri, Chenqi Li et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：20 pages, 12 figures

Video diffusion and world models generate plausible videos but do not reliably match the requested camera direction and travel distance. We present DreamTour, a training-free framework that generates camera tours from a single image with more accurate control over camera motion. DreamTour first uses a frozen image inpainting prior to extend the photograph beyond its original field of view while preserving the input pixels. Depth-based rendering of this extended scene along the requested camera path produces approximate target views that serve as spatial guidance. We introduce Layout Steering, which aligns early video latents with these views through decaying low-frequency corrections, leaving later denoising to refine appearance and dynamics. For longer tours, we autoregressively generate the video segments, repeating guidance construction from each segment's final frame to maintain camera control across the full trajectory. DreamTour improves directional accuracy and travel calibration on VBench while maintaining competitive video quality. On WorldScore, it strengthens camera control and scene consistency across chained movements. Together, these results demonstrate that training-free spatial guidance enables more accurate and consistent camera control in pretrained video diffusion and world models.

## 260. VersionGS: Compact and Motion-Robust Dynamic Gaussian Splatting via Versioned Gaussians

- **作者**：Shihang Wei, Yichi Zhang, Fengqing Zhu
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：16 pages, 6 figures, 12 tables, including supplementary material

Recent dynamic Gaussian splatting methods encode temporal change, either as smooth functions of time or as constrained updates between frames. We observe that their reconstruction quality degrades significantly under fast motion. In challenging dance sequences, their PSNR drops by 1.1-4.0 dB on high-motion frames compared to low-motion frames. To overcome this limitation, we propose VersionGS, a method that directly encodes per-frame Gaussian states and uses motion estimation only to guide reconstruction. Within each group of frames, unchanged Gaussians keep their current versions in a shared dictionary, while modified Gaussians are appended as new versions. To maximize version reuse, we introduce selective sequential construction, which refines the Gaussians selected by optical flow, photograph differences or rendering errors and leaves the others unchanged. The dictionary is then pruned, fine-tuned with simulated quantization and encoded as images. Adjusting the pruning ratio trades off storage and quality without rebuilding the dictionary. On the fast dance sequences of DanceNet3D, VersionGS improves foreground PSNR by 2.7 dB over the best baseline, and its PSNR decreases by less than 0.3 dB on the highest-motion frames. On N3DV and Technicolor, it requires 6.0x and 5.7x less storage than TAD-GS, the strongest baseline overall, while achieving even higher PSNR. Finally, playback is fast and requires no motion model or neural network, only a 3DGS rasterizer.

## 261. MotifFlow: Training-Free, Self-Contained Style Transfer from Color to Motif in Rectified Flow Transformers

- **作者**：Chun Xie, Itaru Kitahara
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Expressive style transfer must reproduce a painting's palette, brushwork, and characteristic motifs while retaining the content image's structure. Attention injection is effective in U-Net-based diffusion models, but adapting it to FLUX often transfers color without capturing the style's distinctive brushwork. Our analysis across backbones links this gap to how injected features are integrated across neighboring spatial locations. We introduce MotifFlow, a training-free, self-contained framework that combines style injection with style-alignment guidance in FLUX. Injection supplies style features during generation but does not explicitly encourage the model's native attention output to align with them. We therefore use the discrepancy between native and injected attention outputs to guide latent-space updates, encouraging the evolving image to develop the style's distinctive brushwork and motifs. Style encoding, guidance, and synthesis all use the same frozen backbone, without auxiliary image encoders or trained adapters. A single strength parameter controls the transition from color transfer to pronounced brushwork and motifs, with additional support for regional stylization and style mixing. Experiments on 1,000 content-style pairs show that MotifFlow better matches the local textures of the style image than baseline methods and produces more consistent stylization than injection alone. The same design extends to FLUX.2.

## 262. Benchmarking Segmentation Models for Scalable Mangrove Mapping with Sentinel-1/2 Imagery

- **作者**：Damba Koné, Sara Mobsite, Joris Guérin et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI
- **备注**：Accepted at the Tackling Climate Change with Machine Learning (TCCML) Workshop at NeurIPS 2026, Sydney, Australia

Mangroves are dynamic coastal ecosystems that contribute to carbon storage, coastal protection and biodiversity conservation, but are vulnerable to climate change. Very-high-resolution imagery such as Pléiades enables detailed mangrove mapping but it is costly and have a low revisit rate. Large-scale monitoring requires free and regularly available data such as Sentinel-1 and Sentinel-2 but their spatial resolution poses challenges for detailed mangrove mapping. In this work, we benchmark deep learning approaches for mangrove mapping from Sentinel-1 radar and Sentinel-2 optical imagery using reference maps derived from very-high-resolution Pléiades imagery. We compare four segmentation configurations based on CROMA and BigEarthNet-pretrained ResNet50 representations with different segmentation decoders. We construct a mangrove mapping dataset covering French Guiana, Suriname, and Amapá. CROMA + UPerNet achieves the best performance, with an F1-score of 0.9218 and an IoU of 0.8549. These results highlight the potential of pretrained multimodal Earth observation representations and open Sentinel data to support mangrove resilience through scalable monitoring.
