## 263. Searching Ultra-Long Videos with Decision Models

- **作者**：Ce Zhang, Baiqi Li, Seongsu Ha et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Our code is available at https://github.com/CeeZh/DeLoVi

Understanding ultra-long videos requires retrieving sparse, query-relevant evidence from extensive video histories. Existing similarity-based retrieval methods are efficient, but they encode the question and the video content independently and thus often miss evidence whose relevance requires reasoning over both. Generative large language models (LLMs) can perform such reasoning, but they typically generate text over multiple rounds of search, which makes each query slow and costly. In this work, we introduce DeLoVi (Decision Models for Long Video), a framework that leverages pretrained decision models to retrieve evidence from ultra-long videos. Like an LLM, a decision model reads the question together with the candidate evidence, so it can reason over both. Unlike an LLM, it directly outputs a relevance score without generating text, which makes it fast and inexpensive. We first present DeLoVi-S, a simple method that generates captions for short video clips and uses a decision model to rank those captions by their relevance to a question. A generative LLM then answers the question using the top-ranked captions. Despite its simplicity, DeLoVi-S achieves state-of-the-art performance on three ultra-long video understanding benchmarks, EgoLifeQA, EgoMemReason, and MM-Lifelong, and generalizes effectively to long-video understanding benchmarks, VideoMME-L and LVBench. However, DeLoVi-S scores every caption for every question, so its cost grows linearly with video length. To reduce this cost, we propose an automated policy evolution framework that iteratively refines the memory construction and retrieval policies around a fixed pretrained decision model. The resulting method, DeLoVi-E, maintains comparable performance while reducing input tokens by 34x on average compared with DeLoVi-S. It also runs over 3x faster than the best prior method on EgoLifeQA while achieving higher accuracy.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33368) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33368)

## 264. DDxBasis: Explaining Frozen Foundation Encoder-based Medical Classification by Exact Differential Diagnosis

- **作者**：Luojie Huang, Gregory N. McKay, Nicholas J. Durr
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, q-bio.QM
- **备注**：Project page: durrlab.github.io/DDxBasis

In differential diagnosis (DDx), a clinician explains a diagnosis by the few findings that separate it from the alternatives. Medical image classifiers cannot yet explain themselves this way. Existing explanations are either faithful but spread over hundreds of concepts, or compact but leave part of each decision unexplained. We present DDxBasis, which explains a classifier on a frozen foundation encoder as a differential diagnosis in three steps. First, a concept bottleneck reads all patch tokens at every encoder layer using gated, second-order slot attention. A concept basis then regroups the concepts into two-pole axes, each a differential between groups of diagnoses. Finally, we read a differential diagnosis from each axis through the typical presentation of its poles, the pertinent findings of the case, and counterfactual edits of borderline cases. Across five medical datasets, DDxBasis is the most accurate frozen-encoder model and close to the best published domain-specific results. Because every concept adds its own evidence, its reproducible concept basis splits each decision exactly, at no cost in accuracy. Without ordered stage or concept labels, the axes recover cell lineages and rare subtypes, agree with dermatologists' structure annotations, and expose planted and real shortcuts.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33358) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33358)

## 265. LGCount: Layout-Guided Diffusion for Zero-Shot Object Counting

- **作者**：Adriano D'Alessandro, Ali Mahdavi-Amiri, Ghassan Hamarneh
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Class-agnostic counting requires the user to specify what to count, usually with a class name or a few annotated boxes. We propose a richer text-based specification: a structured layout that names the target, the region that holds it, the distractors to ignore. A prompt upsampler writes this layout from the image and the requested category alone, and an image-editing diffusion model, adapted into a single-step counter, turns it into a density map. Because counting datasets pair each image with a single valid density map, a counter trained on it learns to ignore the layout. We therefore train on synthetic counterfactuals in which one image admits several valid density maps, so that the layout cannot be ignored. On FSC-147 we improve on the previous zero-shot state of the art and we set the best reported result on LOOKALIKES while being told the least about each image. A single photograph can be counted by category, by region, or by granularity without retraining.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33344) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33344)

## 266. Temporal Weight Surgery: Repairing Video Grounding with LLM Editors

- **作者**：Ali Asgarov, Chris Thomas
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.CL
- **备注**：35 pages, 18 figures, 23 tables, including supplementary material

A surgeon first finds what is wrong, then fixes only that part. We use this idea for video grounding, where video-language models often find the right moment in a long video but write its time wrong. In Temporal Weight Surgery (TWS), a general-purpose LLM reads the model's weights and activations, finds where the error arises and repairs it there, without gradients. Separate sessions find the same cause, and this diagnosis of where and why the model fails is our main contribution. Three GPT-6 Astra sessions trace a dropped digit in TimeLens-8B to one MLP layer, and the largest edit gains 7.9 mIoU on 1,215 unseen questions and 18.3 on videos of 30 minutes or more. Two Claude Opus 5.5 sessions trace minutes written as seconds in Qwen3-VL-8B to the separator and gain 2.9 and 1.3. Before any edit, its answers often have the right minute and the wrong separator, and fixing only the separator lifts their mIoU from 13.1 to 19.2. Every improving edit beats a random edit of its size, and the edited models run as fast as before.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33339) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33339)

## 267. Do Not Let the Prior Heal the Anomaly: Selective Anchoring for Detection from Incomplete Measurements

- **作者**：Nicolas Tricard, Stefan Brandstätter, Jason Chen et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.LG, physics.med-ph
- **备注**：26 pages, 7 figures, 23 tables. Includes supplementary material

Generative priors can recover anatomical detail from incomplete medical measurements, but may erase the very lesions that imaging aims to detect, even when the measurements contain evidence of them. We introduce selective anchoring that protects reliably measured image components from prior-driven changes, while allowing expressive generative models to reconstruct unresolved structure. Acquisition uncertainty in the protected components is either added deterministically to the downstream test or carried by the ensemble through stochastic perturbations of the readings. We implement this principle as a wrapper for pretrained diffusion and flow samplers and as AnchoredFM, a fast constrained flow model. Under a linear-Gaussian measurement model, the protected coefficients are unbiased in expectation and achieve nominal interval coverage across repeated acquisitions, independently of the learned prior. Across sparse-view CT, accelerated brain MRI, and photoacoustic tomography, selective anchoring improves lesion contrast preservation and uncertainty reliability, and can reduce reconstruction error within lesions. For tumor detection in accelerated MRI, with a healthy reference estimated from the measurements alone, deterministic anchoring raises mean AUROC across eight samplers from 78.4% to 84.6% and lesion sensitivity at 0.25 false-positive marks per slice from 38.8% to 47.5%, with gains in all four predefined tumor-size groups. At thresholds frozen on healthy validation data, mean sensitivity rises from 20.2% to 44.1% (37.6% with stochastic anchoring). These findings show how expressive generative priors can reconstruct unresolved anatomy without healing away the abnormalities that the measurements support.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33331) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33331)

## 268. StyleID++: Position-Decoupled and Structure-Coherent Style Retrieval across Diffusion Architectures

- **作者**：Chun Xie, Itaru Kitahara
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Attention injection offers a remarkably simple route to training-free style transfer: content queries retrieve style keys and values inside a pretrained diffusion model. We show that this operation also creates an overlooked failure mode. Position signals that are useful within one image become a spurious shared coordinate system across two spatially unrelated images, causing content tokens to favor style tokens at similar coordinates. This cross-image positional leakage appears in both convolutional U-Nets and diffusion transformers (DiTs), despite their different position mechanisms. Removing the positional shortcut exposes a second problem: unconstrained global retrieval lets related content tokens select incompatible style regions, producing fragmented appearance. We present StyleID++, a training-free framework that addresses the two problems jointly. Position-Decoupled Retrieval reduces coordinate-dependent matching through circular padding in U-Nets and injection-only RoPE removal in DiTs, while preserving native position modeling elsewhere. Affinity-Coherent Refinement then regularizes style assignments with the content self-attention graph, encouraging structurally related tokens to retrieve compatible style evidence. We quantify the leakage with a coordinate--centroid correlation and a permutation test on the style reference. The permutation error can be driven to zero in both backbones, which identifies the position mechanism as the carrier of the leakage, and StyleID++ obtains the best ArtFID of all methods we compare.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33323) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33323)

## 269. What Does RL Post-Training Teach Text-to-Image Models? A Skill-Wise Analysis of Flow-GRPO

- **作者**：Cheng Zhang, Jiesong Liu
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.LG
- **备注**：19 pages, 8 figures, 25 tables

Reinforcement learning (RL) post-training has become a standard way to improve text-to-image flow models: Flow-GRPO lifts the GenEval score of SD3.5-M from 0.63 to 0.95, a gain read as the model having learned compositional generation. We ask how much of that gain the base model's own samples already contained, by measuring pass@k over shared noise seeds, one skill at a time, before and after RL, on prompts never used in training. The answer differs by skill. On four of GenEval's six skills, where 32 base samples already solve almost every prompt, the gain is sharpening: one post-trained sample is worth 4 to 10 base samples, and pass@32 barely moves. On spatial relations it is unlocking: pass@32 rises from 0.69 to 1.00, 128 base samples do not match one post-trained sample, and prompts the base model never solved in 32 samples are solved 93% of the time, as often as those it solved. The benchmark average, 31 base samples per post-trained sample, describes no skill. Relations carry 35% of the gain; of the other 65%, selecting among 8 base samples with the judge behind the reward recovers nine tenths. We propose an account of when each occurs: within a prompt, a group-relative update re-weights only the outcomes its samples contain, and a flow policy cannot widen the noise that produces them; across prompts, shared parameters let successes on some prompts repair a deficit common to many. The same anatomy applies to a text-rendering checkpoint. The account's predictions are tested with a checkpoint trained without KL, within-prompt diversity, and controlled training that holds object classes out, where sharpening comes first and transfers, and unlocking follows, on practised objects before unseen ones.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33316) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33316)

## 270. Large-scale Model Merging via Recovery-aware Task Weighting

- **作者**：Kaiyang Li, Shaobo Han, Boming Chen et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.LG
- **备注**：24 pages, including appendix

Model merging combines multiple task-specific expert models into a single model. However, merging preserves expert capabilities unevenly across tasks. This imbalance is particularly pronounced in large-scale settings, where some tasks can suffer severe capability losses even when average accuracy remains high. To address this imbalance, we introduce Recovery-aware Task Weighting (RaTW), a model merging framework that assigns larger weights to tasks whose expert capabilities are poorly retained by an initial merged model. We quantify these recovery opportunities as the fraction of a task's labeled validation samples that its expert predicts correctly but the initial merged model gets wrong, and use the resulting task weights to re-merge the experts. To evaluate merging at large scale, we construct CLIPMerge-100, a 100-task benchmark with task-specific experts on CLIP ViT-L/14 and ViT-B/32. Across eleven merging methods with 20, 50, and 100 tasks on two CLIP backbones, RaTW improves both mean accuracy and tail20 (the mean expert-normalized accuracy of the 20% worst-retained tasks) in all 33 ViT-L/14 settings and 32 of 33 ViT-B/32 settings. We further introduce ReForge++, which combines RaTW with iterative model refinement and outperforms all evaluated baselines, including a matched-budget control, in both mean accuracy and tail20 across all settings.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33315) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33315)

## 271. NeuroRefine: Six-Head MLP Content Conditioning and Qwen Refinement for Faithful fMRI Decoding

- **作者**：Runqi Xu, Vince D. Calhoun
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：eess.IV, cs.CV
- **备注**：22 pages, 13 figures, 1 table

Reconstructing seen images from fMRI has advanced through diffusion models, but the current state-of-the-art Brain-IT pipeline still has a lossy bottleneck: its low-level branch outlines the figure well, yet the diffusion model that follows frequently mispaints colour, object identity and layout. We move one step backward and condition the refiner directly on the fMRI-derived low-level image, using six one-hidden-layer MLP heads (scene, main-subject category, count, colour, object, layout) to predict the content prompt that guides a frozen Qwen-Image-Edit-2511 model. On the 930 shared NSD concepts, scored with Brain-IT's own metric code on the same ground truth, NeuroRefine reaches a CLIP image cosine of 0.755 against 0.735 for Brain-IT enhanced and produces markedly sharper images, while the saturated 2-way identification is unchanged. The low-level image supplies an explicit spatial prior rather than a lossless layout: refinement trades some layout agreement (0.541 against 0.638) for semantic fidelity and texture. We then show that the standard panel is multi-factor: a high PixCorr can coexist with completely different content, so we read the low-level and semantic factors separately and quantify where they disagree. Finally, we trace the cortical sources of the prompt heads across subjects and report their causal lesion profile and the fMRI noise ceiling. Direction errors are left to future work.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33309) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33309)

## 272. Change Partners at Every Layer: Balanced Random Regrouping for Model Merging

- **作者**：Kaiyang Li, Shaobo Han, Shihao Ji
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.LG, cs.CV
- **备注**：26 pages, including appendix

Model merging combines fine-tuned experts into one model that serves many tasks without retraining, but merging many experts this way substantially reduces accuracy. Grouping offers a middle ground: at every layer or weight matrix, the $N$ experts are divided into $K<N$ groups, the experts in a group are merged into one shared module, and a task uses the module merged from its expert's group. Natural choices are to keep the same partners across layers or to group similar experts. Surprisingly, we find that randomly changing partners at every layer outperforms both. We call this Balanced Random Regrouping (BRR): it independently partitions the experts into $K$ equal-sized random groups at each layer (BRR-layer) or weight matrix (BRR-matrix) and merges each group with an existing method. With validation-selected merge scales, both BRR variants outperform reusing one grouping across layers in all 24 CLIP settings, by 1.0-6.8 points, and BRR-matrix also outperforms the similarity-based FlexMerge in every setting. A first-order analysis explains this gain. At each layer, merging a task's expert with its partners shifts the task's output features; when the partners are the same at every layer, these shifts tend to point in similar directions and add up, whereas with new partners at every layer they vary independently and add up less on average. A series of experiments supports this analysis, which further guides a grouping search that can outperform BRR.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33308) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33308)

## 273. The Platonic Representation Hypothesis in Jacobian-Space: Language and Vision Models Agree Approximately Up to a Rotation

- **作者**：Ross Murphy, Gabriel Brostow
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.AI, cs.CL, cs.CV

The Platonic Representation Hypothesis (PRH) posits that AI models converge toward a shared representation of the world, even when trained on different data modalities. Previous work has found evidence for the PRH in the activations of vision and language models. In this work, we ask whether vision and language models also converge in how they act on these representations: precisely, in how these internal representations affect the models' output. At a chosen layer of a transformer, the Jacobian lens gives every word in the vocabulary (or class in an image classifier) a vector in the hidden state: the vector that most increases the model's tendency to generate that word, at that position or later, or to predict that class. Collectively, these vectors make up what is referred to as the model's J-space. We find evidence of the PRH in J-space across vision and language models, and furthermore, we find that J-spaces mostly converge up to a rotation. That is, a single rotation, fitted on a subset of words, approximately maps one model's J-space onto another's, and so applies to words and classes it was not fitted on. A word's vector, rotated from a text-only language model's J-space into the visual patch tokens of a vision-language model, changes the object the vision-language model says is in the image (and only in the image region where it is added). Rotated into an image generator's text conditioning, a word's vector changes the object in the generated image. Rotated into an ImageNet classifier (trained without text), it raises that object's class score above almost all others, and vice versa. This suggests that models converge not only in how they represent the world, but also in how they act on it.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33304) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33304)

## 274. CoSMo: A Compact Spectral Space of Body Motion in Video

- **作者**：Maham Tanveer, Hao Zhang
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Project page: https://cosmospectral.github.io/cosmo/

We introduce CoSMo, a compact spectral space of body motion in video, in which each motion is a single code that can be compared and edited without training, transferred to a new subject, interpolated, composed, scaled in intensity and detail, or sampled anew. Our encoder expresses how a clip's DINOv2 features change over time in a fixed temporal basis, the discrete cosine transform (DCT), and compresses this change into 384 numbers. Because each frequency band is the same cosine pattern in every code, codes are aligned in time, ordered from coarse to fine, and can be blended or added without matching frames. What stays constant over a clip cancels, and keeping fewer frequencies leaves less of the performer and scene. The encoder is trained on real videos and on synthetic motions of several characters, and lightweight adapters let a frozen image-to-video model render any code with any subject. On unseen videos, our code ranks the same motion on other bodies higher than DisMo, a per-frame representation, while its nearest neighbors show the same performer about 40% less often with a seven times more compact code. In generation, without per-motion training, CoSMo transfers motion more faithfully than FlexiAct and a text-prompt baseline, at least as faithfully as DisMo on automatic metrics and clearly better between humanoid bodies, and its blends keep much more of both source motions than DisMo's. Users rank CoSMo first nearly twice as often as DisMo for motion transfer (59% vs. 30%), place it top-two 94% of the time, and prefer it in 76% of the interpolation comparisons.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33296) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33296)

## 275. JetDiffusion: Pushing the Speed Limits of Video Generation with NVFP4 Sparse Attention

- **作者**：Yulun Wu, Zihan Min, Minshen Zhang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.LG

Attention over long sequences is a major computational bottleneck in video generation, which can be alleviated through sparsity and low-bit quantization. We propose JetDiffusion, a video generation system built on trainable Video Sparse Quantized Attention (VSQA), with custom inference kernels that combine native NVFP4 computation and kernel fusion. At 90% sparsity, VSQA accelerates attention by 8-29x compared with FlashAttention on different hardwares. To further accelerate video generation, we combine VSQA with NVFP4 linear quantization and step distillation. Although each technique can individually preserve generation quality, jointly adapting to all three remains challenging, particularly under aggressive NVFP4 quantization. Through empirical studies, we develop an effective staged training recipe that enables stable adaptation to quantization, sparsity, and few-step denoising. On Wan2.1-14B, the resulting model achieves 273x end-to-end video generation speedup on the RTX 5090 while maintaining comparable video quality.

## 276. Same Tokenizer, Better Tokens: Choosing Image Tokens by MAP Inference

- **作者**：Piotr Kubaty, Jacek Tabor, Łukasz Struski
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.LG

Discrete visual tokens encode images for reconstruction and serve as training targets for autoregressive image generators. Vector-quantized tokenizers typically assign each encoder feature its nearest code independently. The decoder uses these codes jointly, so a sequence of individually nearest codes can reconstruct an image less faithfully than an alternative assignment. A local model of the reconstruction loss yields a closest-vector problem with a decoder-dependent target and metric. Reconstruction-only search improves PSNR across the frozen tokenizers we test. Different code sequences can achieve similar reconstruction quality, leaving room to favour more predictable ones. We introduce RecaP (Reconstruction and Predictability), which selects codes for a frozen tokenizer by balancing how faithfully they reconstruct the image with how likely their sequence is under a pretrained autoregressive prior. On ImageNet, LlamaGen GPT-B trained on RecaP tokens achieves lower early-checkpoint FID against training images than GPT-B trained on nearest-neighbour tokens. The difference is smaller against held-out images. On an ImageNet subset, the prior improves generation at comparable reconstruction FID. Our code is available at: https://github.com/gmum/recap

## 277. CriticalScenes: Scalable Photorealistic Critical-Scenario Generation for Closed-Loop Driving Adaptation

- **作者**：Alexandros Theocharous, Holger Caesar, Ziqi Zhang
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：9 pages, 4 figures

Safety-critical interactions are rare in recorded driving data, leaving end-to-end driving planners with limited exposure to evasive behaviour during training. This lack of critical supervision can lead to unsafe behaviour and, in rare instances, collisions. To address this gap, we introduce CriticalScenes, a reconstruction-based framework that transforms ordinary recorded driving scenes into photorealistic safety-critical interactions with corresponding collision-avoidance supervision. Across 53 reconstructed source scenes, 979 of 1,060 attempted interactions yield feasible threat trajectories (92.4\%), while the inserted threat actors remain detectable in more than 95\% of non-occluded observations within 5--35\,m. Experiments with two structurally different end-to-end planners show that the generated supervision improves critical-scenario planning, while closed-loop transfer varies across planner architectures. SparseDrive achieves a 46.5\% relative reduction in collision rate on an independent photorealistic closed-loop benchmark, whereas UniAD shows only a modest closed-loop improvement despite substantial open-loop gains. The results further highlight the importance of closed-loop evaluation, as improvements in open-loop trajectory prediction do not necessarily translate into equivalent safety gains.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33255) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33255)

## 278. MonoQPM: Splitting Features into Concepts for Inherent Interpretability and Predictive Performance

- **作者**：Thomas Norrenbrock, Robert Zimmermann, Timo Kaiser et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.LG
- **备注**：Published in Transactions on Machine Learning Research (10/2026). OpenReview: https://openreview.net/forum?id=8bQNRlFQhZ

Alongside superposition, polysemanticity is one of the primary obstacles to achieving interpretable deep neural networks. While these phenomena are typically intrinsically linked, the Quadratic Programming Enhanced Model (QPM) naturally decouples them. By representing classes with a sparse binary assignment of very few features, QPM prevents superposition by design on its final features but still exhibits polysemanticity. However, measuring polysemanticity is an open problem. This work proposes a utility-focused approach to measuring polysemanticity by quantifying the decrease in interference-induced activation errors, which yields the practical utility of tighter prediction sets using Conformal Prediction (CP). We explicitly disentangle the polysemantic features of QPM into monosemantic concepts to create the Monosemantic QPM (MonoQPM). Because its features are disentangled, MonoQPM acts as a significantly more efficient Conformal Predictor. Additionally, we introduce CUBCars, a dataset providing ground truth information about shared concepts. Using this and other datasets, we demonstrate that polysemanticity emerges in QPM across all tested architectures, but is effectively alleviated by MonoQPM. For instance, MonoQPM guarantees 88% coverage using Adaptive Prediction Sets on ImageNet with just 66% of the frozen QPM's set size.

## 279. How Many Samples Is RL Worth? Best-of-N versus RL Post-Training for Text-to-Image Models

- **作者**：Salil Bhatnagar
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.LG
- **备注**：38 pages, 6 figures

Reinforcement learning (RL) on a reward that can be checked automatically has become a common last step in the training of text-to-image models. A simpler way to get more correct images is best-of-N: draw N images from the base model and keep the one that a picker prefers. We ask how many base model samples one RL sample is worth, and call this number the exchange rate. We measure it for Stable Diffusion 3.5 Medium and its public Flow-GRPO checkpoint on 398 new prompts in the style of GenEval, the benchmark behind the RL reward, with human labels on every prompt and two automatic judges other than the reward. The answer depends on the prompt. Four fifths of the prompts do not ask for a position (for example, one object left of another). On these, the base model catches up: best-of-16 with an off-the-shelf vision-language model (VLM) as the picker is about as good as one RL image. A person who looks through up to 3 base images finds a correct one as often as one RL image is correct. On the prompts that ask for a position, RL is far ahead: a person finds a correct image among 8 base images for only 52% of the prompts, against 92% for one RL image. Over all prompts, our primary test, which we fixed in advance, finds best-of-16 with the VLM picker 9.3 points behind one RL image when both automatic judges must pass an image. People see a smaller gap, 5.5 and 6.5 points in two checks. FLUX.1-dev shows a similar gap in the same test. RL also solves 20 prompts that the base model misses in 128 tries, and 1,024 tries recover half of them. The automatic judges are wrong in opposite directions: a detector-based judge like the reward, as used in our primary test, reports a larger gap than people see, and VLM judges report little or none. Comparisons of RL with best-of-N should therefore check the selected images by hand.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33232) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33232)

## 280. A comprehensive survey of deep face verification systems adversarial attacks and defense strategies

- **作者**：Sohair Kilany, Ahmed Mahfouz
- **投稿**：2026-11-24 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.CR

Face Verification (FV) systems have exhibited remarkable performance in verification tasks and have consequently garnered extensive adoption across various applications, from identity duplication to authentication in mobile payments. However, the surge in popularity of face verification has raised concerns about potential vulnerabilities in the face of adversarial attacks. These concerns originate from the fact that advanced FV systems, which rely on deep neural networks, have recently demonstrated susceptibility to crafted input samples known as adversarial examples. Although imperceptible to human observers, adversarial examples can deceive deep neural networks during the testing and deployment phases. These vulnerabilities raised significant concerns about the deployment of deep neural networks in safety-critical contexts, prompting extensive investigations into adversarial attacks and corresponding defense strategies. This comprehensive survey provides a comprehensive overview of recent advances in deep face verification, encompassing a broad spectrum of topics such as algorithmic designs, database utilization, protocols, and application scenarios. Furthermore, we conduct an in-depth examination of state-of-the-art algorithms to generate adversarial examples and the defense mechanisms devised to mitigate such adversarial threats.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33231) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33231)

## 281. Innata-0: A Native Embodied World System with Model-Agent Co-Design

- **作者**：Innata Team, Yu Bai, Ziyi Bai et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.CV
- **备注**：87 pages, 25 figures, Project Page: https://baai-eai.github.io/innata-0/

The physical world is open-ended, dynamic, and partially observable. While foundation models have advanced embodied intelligence, standalone models struggle with long-horizon execution, and simply integrating models with agents may lead to inconsistencies between planning and physical control. To support reliable and adaptive behaviors in the physical world, we introduce Innata-0, a native embodied world system. Innata-0 jointly designs an embodied world-action model and an agent to combine their complementary strengths. The model, Innata-0-WAM, adopts a tri-tower Mixture-of-Transformers architecture that integrates understanding, prediction, and action generation. The agent, Innata-0-Agent, coordinates long-horizon execution through memory, verification, and recovery. A co-design protocol is further proposed to align model training with agent execution and leverage validated experiences to support subsequent adaptation and model improvement. Extensive evaluations demonstrate strong embodied capabilities across understanding, world prediction, and robotic manipulation. Innata-0 represents our first step toward physical intelligence, and we see it as the beginning of a much longer journey.

## 282. Confidence-Based Adaptive Retrieval Fails Where It Matters Most: A Paradox in Biomedical Question Answering

- **作者**：Chengying She, Siyu Deng, Haiyan Ge et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：accepted by BIBM2027 Workshop

Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly used for biomedical question answering, but plausible incorrect answers remain a safety risk in clinical settings. Retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) can ground responses in medical literature, while confidence-based adaptive RAG retrieves only when the model appears uncertain. We evaluate this routing strategy on three biomedical QA benchmarks of increasing difficulty and compare models across a broad range of parameter scales. The results reveal a paradox: confidence is well calibrated (AUROC 0.78--0.89) on standard medical exam questions where retrieval adds little value (+2.4\% to -1.4\%), yet becomes \emph{near-random} (AUROC 0.506) on specialist-level questions where retrieval helps most (+11.2\%). Five uncertainty signals---first-token probability, margin, entropy, consistency-based sampling, and verbalized confidence---show the same failure pattern. Five post-hoc calibration correction strategies, including density-ratio reweighting and rank-preserving transformation, do not recover a useful routing signal on hard questions (best AUROC: 0.507). Cross-model comparison shows that larger parameter scale and higher base accuracy do not guarantee reliable confidence-based routing under specialist-domain difficulty shift. These findings challenge the premise of confidence-based adaptive RAG: in high-stakes biomedical settings, defaulting to always-retrieve is safer and more accurate than confidence-based routing when calibration on the target domain is unknown.

## 283. Confidently Lost: Silent Failure and Edge Deployment of Dense Visual SLAM under Photon Starvation

- **作者**：Shrikrishna Bhagirath Rajule, Srinath Neelamagam, Aniket Bera
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.CV
- **备注**：8 pages, 7 figures. Submitted to IEEE ICRA 2027

Dense visual SLAM built on learned two-view geometry is evaluated in good light; in low light it fails silently. Two frames of 9-level quantized noise, no scene at all, give MASt3R-SLAM's network a cross-view geometric self-consistency of 0.936; a real dark frame pair gives 0.935. Starved of structure, the network invents the same geometry for both views and the system trusts the agreement: on a dark sequence it consumes 1,605 frames, emits 7 keyframes, rejects no factor-graph edges and reports full frame rate, while over 95% of the correspondences it accepts violate the ground-truth epipolar geometry. One inflated statistic is read by two subsystems against thresholds in opposite directions, so keyframing starves while relocalization accepts almost every candidate. The same statistic, read at keyframe-pair baselines, separates the confident collapse from working runs on the ETH3D dark sequences without ground truth or extra computation (in-sample). Low-light-adapted weights take tracked trajectories from 1/6 to 5/6, as does retuning two thresholds; neither yields accurate ones, because the adaptation withdraws confidence from wrong geometry rather than finding right geometry. Replayed on a Jetson AGX Orin carried by a legged robot, the mechanism's signature reproduces on the two darkest of four routes, and which of its faces appears varies with route geometry and sensor encoding. The network does not lose confidence in the dark; it manufactures it.

## 284. Beyond the section plane: Robust 3D reconstruction of multiplex immunofluorescence exposes limitations of 2D spatial cell statistics

- **作者**：Junming Ma, Olle Edgren Schüllerqvist, Hui Yu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-21 ｜ **分类**：eess.IV, cs.CV

Spatial analysis of the tumour microenvironment with multiplex immunofluorescence (mIF) typically relies on single thin tissue sections, which misrepresent the 3D tissue organisation and bias spatial cell statistics in ways that remain largely unquantified. We reconstruct 24 breast and lung tumour tissue microarray cores in 3D, each from 15-20 serial mIF sections, and compare spatial cell statistics in 3D with those from individual sections. Our pipeline combines affine pre-alignment with dense deformable refinement using RoMa v2, achieving a median target registration error of 1.79 $μ$m, well below nuclear size, which allows nuclei to be linked across sections. While cell-type proportions are preserved in 2D (Pearson corr. $r \geq 0.99$), 3D analysis reveals up to 3.8 times shorter nearest-neighbour distances, 13-19% higher neighbourhood entropy, and 2-4 times more contacts per cell. Tissue composition can thus be reliably estimated from individual sections, whereas cell-cell interactions require volumetric analysis.
